## 5. Combat and interaction

### GDD-CMB-01 Stomp

Status: Proposed

Landing on top of a stompable enemy defeats it and bounces Poncho: 260 px/s up, or 410 px/s up if jump is held or buffered. Some enemies are not stompable (spikes) and are marked visually.

- **Built (details Proposed):** a landing counts as a stomp when Poncho is falling, his hurtbox (GDD-MOV-06) overlaps the enemy's box, and on the step before his feet were at most 4 px below the enemy's top. That makes a landing on top a stomp and walking into the side or from below a hit (GDD-CMB-03). A stomp is checked before the hit, so a stomp never hurts, and it works while Poncho is invulnerable. The bounce is 410 px/s if jump is held or was pressed within the buffer window (0.1 s, GDD-MOV-03), otherwise 260 px/s, and releasing jump cuts the rise short like any jump. Several enemies stomped in one step give one bounce. The Tlacuache's first stomp only makes it play dead (it bounces Poncho all the same); a stomp after it wakes defeats it (user decision, 2026-10-22; GDD-ENM-02). The Zopilote and the cactus buddy are not stompable (user decision, 2026-10-22): landing on one is a hit like touching it anywhere else, and no bounce. Neither is Don Zopilote (user, 2026-11-02, GDD-BOS-02): a stomp never hits him, and landing on him is a touch like any other, with no bounce: a hit while he does harm, and nothing at all while he does none (stuck in the sand, climbing, hurt or defeated). An enemy that cannot be stomped is marked by pale points on top: the Zopilote's cholla joint with three pale spines, stuck in its back, and the cactus buddy's three pale spines on his head (GDD-ENM-02), Don Zopilote's three cholla joints with pale spines, GDD-BOS-02, and World 2's spinning Trompo and La Estrella theirs, below. `STOMP` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **World 2 (built, details Proposed):** the Paloma can be stomped whatever it is doing, walking, telegraphing, rising, hovering or coming down, and the Chapulín crouched or in the air (GDD-ENM-03). The Trompo cannot while it spins, marked by three pale tacks on its crown, drawn only while it spins (GDD-ENM-03): landing on it is a hit; once a claw or a fireball has stunned it, a stomp defeats it, with the usual bounce. La Estrella cannot be stomped, marked by the pale tips of her three top cones, and landing on her is a touch like any other (GDD-BOS-03). A stomp in mid-air keeps an awning chain going, and a stomp and an awning landing in the same step give the stomp's bounce (GDD-WLD-10). `src/sim/paloma.test.ts`, `src/sim/chapulin.test.ts`, `src/sim/trompo.test.ts`, `src/sim/awning.test.ts`.

### GDD-CMB-02 Claw swipe

Status: Proposed

A short-range melee on the action button, a standard mechanic that Poncho always has (decided by the user, 2026-10-21; it is not a power-up): 0.25 s cooldown, hits a 20 x 32 px area in front of Poncho (as tall as his body; user, 2026-10-25, when the body grew) for 3 frames after a 2-frame wind-up. Defeats light enemies, stuns heavy ones for 1 s, breaks fragile blocks (papel picado walls). Cannot be used while taking a hit.

- **Built (details Proposed):** a press of the claw button (X or J, or the touch Claw button, GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03) starts a swing; holding it swings once, and a press during a swing or its cooldown is ignored. The timeline is a 2-step wind-up (the press step is the first), then 3 steps of swing in which the hit box is live, then idle. The 0.25 s cooldown (15 steps) runs from the press that started the swing, so the swings of a player who taps as fast as possible are 15 steps apart. The box is 20 px long and 32 px tall, level with the body, from the body's front edge outwards, facing the way Poncho faced at the press. Boxes that only share an edge with a tile do not touch it (GDD-PWR-06). A hit that leaves Poncho alive (a spike, an enemy, Don Zopilote or his cactus fruit in Taco or Fuego, GDD-CMB-03) ends the swing in progress but not its cooldown; there is no stun yet, so that is all "cannot be used while taking a hit" means for now. Losing a life ends any swing. The wind-up shows his paw drawn back, claws out, and the swing his paw flung forward with a claw swipe over the box, three pale crescent streaks with crimson edges, then a follow-through as the streaks break up (GDD-ART-04). It opens piñatas (GDD-PWR-05) and breaks fragile walls (GDD-WLD-05). It defeats the Alacrán, the Zopilote or the Tlacuache its box overlaps (also a Tlacuache playing dead), and passes through a cactus buddy, which only the Chile Fantasma's touch defeats (GDD-ENM-02, GDD-PWR-04). In a boss arena (GDD-BOS-02) it knocks a resting rock its box overlaps the way the swing faces, hits Don Zopilote only while he is stuck in the sand (user, 2026-11-02), and passes through his cactus fruit. Stunning heavy enemies waited until a heavy enemy existed (user, 2026-10-31: World 1 has none) and is built with World 2's Trompo (below).
- **The stun, with World 2 (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-04: "Three now, Zanate later", the Trompo among the three):** the first heavy enemy is World 2's Trompo (GDD-ENM-03). A swipe whose box overlaps a spinning Trompo stuns it for 1.0 s (60 steps, `TROMPO.stunSteps`): it lies on its side, harmless to touch, and a stomp then defeats it (GDD-CMB-01); a swipe on a stunned one does nothing more and does not renew the stun. A fireball stuns it the same way (GDD-PWR-03). The stun thuds like a stomp (GDD-AUD-02). In World 2 the claw also defeats the Paloma and the Chapulín it overlaps (GDD-ENM-03), unrolls a rollo whose bundle it overlaps, or whose bundle Poncho's own body box overlaps as the swing comes (GDD-WLD-11), hits La Estrella while she can be hit (GDD-BOS-03) and bursts her tejocotes, and passes through awnings and cordeles (their flutter is not drawn yet; GDD-WLD-10, GDD-WLD-11). `src/sim/trompo.test.ts`, `src/sim/papel.test.ts`, `src/sim/world2run.test.ts`, `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`. The claw sound plays when a swing starts (GDD-AUD-02). `CLAW` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/claw.ts`.

### GDD-CMB-03 Damage and invulnerability

Status: Proposed

- A hit is resolved by the power-up state (GDD-PWR-01): in Base it costs exactly 1 life (GDD-LIFE-02); in Taco or Fuego it only downgrades the state and costs no life. There is no separate health bar.
- After a hit Poncho is invulnerable for 2.0 s and knocked back 80 px/s away from the source (user, 2026-10-21; built for spike hits and the hits of the Alacrán, Zopilote, cactus buddy, Tlacuache, Don Zopilote and his cactus fruit (GDD-BOS-02), and World 2's Paloma, Chapulín, Trompo, La Estrella and her tejocotes (GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03), `HIT` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The push is a starting speed: the run controls take over at once, so it is a nudge off the hazard, not a launch. When Poncho is dead centre on the source, the push goes against the way he faces.
- Invulnerability does not protect from pits or crushing, and neither do Taco and Fuego: a pit or a crush costs one life in any power-up state (GDD-LIFE-02). A crush is not a hit, so there is no knockback.
- **Enemy hits (built, details Proposed):** touching a live enemy is a hit like a spike: the push goes away from the enemy's middle, and the hit also ends a claw swing in progress. A hit is never taken while Poncho is invulnerable (a stomp, a claw swipe and a fireball still defeat the enemy). Of the enemies, the one contact that is no hit is rising into a cactus buddy from beneath (GDD-ENM-02); while the Chile Fantasma runs no contact is a hit, and his touch defeats the enemy (GDD-PWR-04). If a spike and an enemy touch him in the same step it is one hit, from the spike. Don Zopilote and his cactus fruit (GDD-BOS-02) hit like an enemy: touching him while he laps, telegraphs, dives, drops or sweeps, or touching a fruit, falling or lying, is a hit, pushed away from his middle or the fruit's; touching him while he is stuck in the sand, climbing, hurt or defeated is no hit. They come after a spike and an enemy: a step where several touch Poncho is one hit, pushed away from the first of spike, enemy, Don Zopilote, fruit (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`).
- **World 2 (built, details Proposed):** the Paloma, the Chapulín and a spinning Trompo hit like the Alacrán, and a stunned Trompo is no hit (GDD-ENM-03); La Estrella and her tejocotes hit like Don Zopilote and his fruit, in his place in that order (her arena has no spike or enemy), and she does no harm while hurt or defeated (GDD-BOS-03; checked by `src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts`).

---

## 6. Power-ups

Decided (user, 2026-10-19): the game has Mario-style power-ups, themed to Poncho's world. The specific items below are Proposed. Power-ups are temporary and are lost when hit; permanent abilities (glide, wall cling) are separate (GDD-PRG-01).

### GDD-PWR-01 Power-up states

Status: Proposed

Poncho is always in exactly one state. The hitbox does not change between states (simpler levels, same on touch).

| State | Look | What a hit does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Base** | Empty hands | Costs 1 life (GDD-LIFE-02) |
| **Taco** | Holds a taco, as in the reference image | The taco is gone, no life lost, back to Base |
| **Fuego** | Taco plus glowing chile, can spit fire (GDD-PWR-03) | Back to Taco, no life lost |

- A hit that downgrades the state still gives 2.0 s invulnerability and knockback (GDD-CMB-03).
- Losing a life respawns Poncho in Base.
- Picking up an item that would not improve the state gives hilos instead (GDD-WLD-04).
- **Built (details Proposed):** Base, Taco and Fuego (GDD-PWR-03). A taco pickup is a 12 x 12 px square in the middle of its tile (`TACO` in `src/config/tuning.ts`), picked up when the body box overlaps it. While in Taco, a taco pickup is left where it is. A pickup and a spike hit in the same step: the pickup is resolved first, so the taco is spent on that hit. Losing a life puts Poncho back in Base and every taco pickup comes back, so a level is never left without its taco. A spike hit in Taco gives 2.0 s of invulnerability like a respawn. Unlike a lethal hit, a Taco hit does not stop an ofrenda touched in the same step from activating, since Poncho is still alive. Poncho is in Base from the moment a life is lost, so the HUD icon goes during the wait. Poncho holds a taco in his paw in front of his chest, on the side he faces, and the HUD shows a taco icon right of the triangles; in Fuego he holds a glossy chile beside the taco and the HUD shows both (drawn by `scripts/art/poncho/items.mjs`, GDD-ART-04). A hit in Fuego goes back to Taco, with the same 2.0 s of invulnerability and push, no life lost. A taco released from a piñata or lying in the level does not improve Fuego: it is left (a floor taco) or used up (a released one, see Open Question 12). `src/sim/taco.ts`, `src/sim/run.ts`.
- Lives are never granted by taco or chile. Only veladoras and ofrendas restore lives (GDD-LIFE-03).

### GDD-PWR-02 Taco (the mushroom)

Status: Proposed

Base to Taco. A taco walks away from a broken piñata at 40 px/s, turns at walls and falls off ledges, like a Mario mushroom. Collecting it plays a chomp, a 0.3 s freeze-frame and a sparkle. Poncho keeps the taco in hand until hit.

- **Built (details Proposed):** a taco released from a piñata (GDD-PWR-05) stands on top of it and walks right at 40 px/s (`TACO.walkSpeed`), turns round at walls, follows sand slopes up and down and turns at a slope's high side (GDD-MOV-09), falls off ledges and is gone once it falls below the level. It is a 12 x 12 px square, collected by touch (GDD-PWR-06). Collecting one in Base gives Taco. In Taco it would not improve the state, which gives hilos (GDD-PWR-01); that rule is not built yet, so it is just used up. This differs from the floor taco, which waits where it is: a released one walks away and cannot wait (Open Question 12). `src/sim/item.ts`.

### GDD-PWR-03 Chile and Fuego (the fire flower)

Status: Proposed

- Taco to Fuego by eating a chile (a stationary glowing pepper released from a piñata when Poncho is already in Taco or Fuego).
- In Fuego the Fuego button spits a salsa fireball: 180 px/s horizontal, bounces along the ground with a 20 px arc, vanishes on walls or after 3 s, maximum 2 on screen.
- Fireballs defeat most light enemies, melt ice and light unlit candles. Bosses take 1 damage per fireball unless a boss rule says otherwise (GDD-BOS-01); Don Zopilote's and La Estrella's do (GDD-BOS-02, GDD-BOS-03).
- The Fuego button is separate from Claw (GDD-CMB-02). On touch it appears only while in Fuego (GDD-CTL-03).

- **Built (details Proposed):** the chile is a stationary pepper that stands on top of its piñata (GDD-PWR-05) and is eaten by touch (GDD-PWR-06): Taco becomes Fuego, Fuego stays Fuego and the pepper is used up (it would give hilos, GDD-PWR-01, a rule not built yet), and Base becomes Taco (one step better, like a Mario flower picked up when small; the GDD did not say). A press of the Fuego button (C or K on the keyboard, the touch Fuego button, GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03) in Fuego spits one fireball on that press step; holding the button spits once. In Base or Taco it does nothing. At most 2 fireballs exist at once: a press while two are in flight is ignored. With a wall right against Poncho's front there is no room for it (GDD-PWR-06): the press does nothing.
- The fireball is a 6 x 6 px square. It starts at Poncho's front edge, level with the middle of his body, heading the way he faces at 180 px/s, and is not falling yet. It falls at 1500 px/s squared and bounces off the ground at the speed that climbs 20 px (about 245 px/s), so it bounces along the ground in a 20 px arc. On a sand slope it bounces straight up off the surface at the same speed and bounces on up the slope, and a slope's high side is a wall to it (GDD-MOV-09). It vanishes on a wall, under a ceiling, below the bottom of the level and after 3 s (180 steps). It is not stopped by spikes, ofrendas, keys, pickups or Don Zopilote's cactus fruit (GDD-BOS-02). A fireball in flight keeps going when Poncho is hit or leaves Fuego; a lost life clears them. Of the things a fireball can hurt, the Alacrán, the Zopilote, the Tlacuache, Don Zopilote and World 2's enemies, rollos and tejocotes are built (below). `FIREBALL` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/fireball.ts`.
- A fireball defeats the Alacrán, the Zopilote or the awake Tlacuache it overlaps (GDD-ENM-02) and is used up by it; it passes over a Tlacuache that is playing dead without being used up; a cactus buddy it overlaps is not defeated, but the fireball is used up all the same (GDD-ENM-02). Don Zopilote's rule (user, 2026-11-02, GDD-BOS-02): a fireball that touches him is a hit on him only while he is stuck in the sand, and is used up by him either way, unless he is defeated, when it passes through him. Not built yet: the other things fireballs hit (ice, candles), the Fuego sound, the spit pose. On a gamepad the east button spits (GDD-CTL-02).
- **World 2 (built, details Proposed):** a fireball defeats the Paloma and the Chapulín it overlaps and stuns a spinning Trompo (GDD-ENM-03, GDD-CMB-02), unrolls a rolled-up rollo whose bundle it touches (GDD-WLD-11) and bursts a tejocote, and is used up by each; it passes over a stunned Trompo without being used up; it is used up on La Estrella and does nothing to her, and passes through her once she is defeated (GDD-BOS-03). It passes through awnings, cordeles and strung rollos (GDD-WLD-10, GDD-WLD-11). In 2-2 and 2-3 a fireball unrolls a bundle the claw cannot reach (GDD-WLD-13, GDD-WLD-14). `src/sim/paloma.test.ts`, `src/sim/chapulin.test.ts`, `src/sim/trompo.test.ts`, `src/sim/papel.test.ts`, `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`.

### GDD-PWR-04 Chile Fantasma (the star)

Status: Proposed (where it is placed, the special piñata's look, what happens to his state, what his touch defeats, the warning flash, the two art references and the palette cap, GDD-ART-03, are Decided, user, 2026-11-10, below). Post-MVP (GDD-MVP-02), built ahead of it at the user's request (user, 2026-11-10: "/harness-feature gdd-pwr-04").

A rare ghost pepper hidden in special piñatas. For 8 s: Poncho is invulnerable (a golden glow, drawn from the user's reference strip, below), runs at 180 px/s and defeats enemies on contact (not bosses, below). Does not protect from pits or crushing. Music switches to a spicy variation. The last 2 s warn that it is wearing off (the dimmed glow, below).

- **Where (Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "One hidden per world"):** one special piñata per world, off the main path: in 1-2 and in 2-2, plus one in the sandbox for testing. None in the tutorial 0-1 or in a boss arena (1-4, 2-4). A special piñata always releases the Chile Fantasma, whatever Poncho's state, by the room rule of GDD-PWR-05. It looks like every other piñata (Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Looks the same"): the surprise is what comes out.
- **His state (Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Overlay, state kept"):** the Chile Fantasma is a timer on top of Base, Taco or Fuego, not a fourth state (GDD-PWR-01). He keeps his taco and chile, Fuego still spits fireballs, and nothing downgrades him while it runs. A taco or chile picked up while it runs works as usual.
- **What his touch defeats (Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Enemies yes, bosses no"):** every regular enemy he touches is defeated, World 1's and World 2's alike (GDD-ENM-02, GDD-ENM-03), the cactus buddy El Cuate included, and anything an enemy throws bursts on his touch. Bosses cannot hurt him, but touching one does not hurt the boss either; the boss keeps its own rules (GDD-BOS-01). The chile is never placed in a boss arena, so this matters only if a later rule brings one there.
- **The warning flash (Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "use the dimmed glow for the warning flash"):** for the last 2 s the golden glow dims to a pale gold, so the player sees it is wearing off. That it holds pale and steady rather than blinking between gold and pale gold is Proposed (the main session's reading of the reference strip's fourth frame).
- **Art:** drawn from the user's reference images (user, 2026-11-10: "I'll send references"). **Poncho's glow (Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Here is the reference picture for poncho in ghost pepper mode"):** `assets/characters/poncho/fantasma/glow.jpg`, a strip of four frames: a golden outline round his whole silhouette, outside the near-black one, with small gold sparkles and flame flickers near him while standing, golden speed streaks behind him while running, sparkles round him in a jump, and for the warning the outline dimmed to pale gold with no sparkles. It guides the effect only; his body stays the main pixel drawing (GDD-ART-02). **The ghost pepper pickup (Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Here is the ghost pepper"):** `assets/characters/poncho/fantasma/pickup.jpg`, three frames of a gentle bob: a plump pale lavender-white pepper with a curled green stem, a cheeky face (two dot eyes, a small open smile), its lower body tapering into a wispy ghost tail, a near-black outline inside a golden glow outline, and gold sparkles round it.
- **Built (details Proposed):** a special piñata (`special` in the level's piñata list, GDD-PWR-05) releases the Chile Fantasma standing on its top, whatever Poncho's state; it stays where it is, like the chile, and is eaten by touch (GDD-PWR-06). Eating it starts the 8 s (`FANTASMA.steps`, 480 steps) over, the step he eats it included, so a pepper and a spike touched in the same step are no hit; eating another while it runs starts the 8 s over. While it runs: a spike, a boss, the cactus fruit, La Estrella and a tejocote are no hit (no life, no push, no invulnerability, the state kept); every regular enemy his hurtbox overlaps is defeated outright, one playing dead or stunned included, and a stomp still throws him up; he runs at up to 180 px/s (`FANTASMA.runSpeed`) and, when it ends with the stick held, slows back to 120 px/s at the acceleration's rate. A pit or a crush still costs a life, and a lost life ends it. `src/sim/run.ts`, `src/sim/fantasma.test.ts`.
- **Drawn (Proposed, from the user's references):** the pepper bobs through its three frames, 8 steps each (`assets/sprites/poncho/fantasma.png`). Poncho's glow is four copies of his own frame tinted solid gold, one drawing pixel (half a game px) left, right, above and below him and behind him, so they follow every frame, mirror and blink; the outline in the user's strip is about one of his drawing pixels thick (measured). Standing, three sparkles and a flame flicker float near him; running, two sparkles and two speed streaks behind him; in the air, three sparkles (`assets/sprites/poncho/glow.png`, `src/ui/fantasmaGlow.ts`). For the last 2 s (`FANTASMA.warnSteps`) the copies turn the pale gold and the sparkles, flame and streaks go. The sandbox's special piñata hangs at column 26, row 2, 48 px above the top right ledge (GDD-PWR-05); it is reached on foot, and its pepper with a wall kick off the piñata's side and a glide back over it (measured by `src/levels/sandbox.test.ts`). The look approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-10: "Merge it"; `docs/screenshots/chile-fantasma/`).
- Not built yet: the special piñatas in 1-2 and 2-2 (the next step), the spicy music (there is no music yet, GDD-AUD-01), bursting what enemies throw (only bosses throw, and the chile is never in a boss arena), and any sound.

### GDD-PWR-05 Piñata blocks (the question blocks)

Status: Proposed

Hanging piñatas that open when Poncho hits them from below (head bump) or with a claw swipe, releasing an item or hilos. A piñata releases a Taco if Poncho is Base, otherwise a Chile; a special piñata always releases the Chile Fantasma (GDD-PWR-04); a multi-piñata gives up to 10 hilos over 4 s. An opened piñata becomes a broken shell. Piñatas are drawn so players recognise them at a glance and never hide a lethal surprise. World 2's boss, La Estrella, is a giant star piñata that hurts, and keeps that promise: she is met only in her own arena, under a boss bar, far larger than a piñata block and of another shape, and the piñata blocks stay their own shape (user, 2026-11-04: "La Estrella", a piñata boss in her own arena; GDD-BOS-03). She is hit by the head bump's rule and the claw like a piñata block.

- **Built (details Proposed):** the head bump, in Base and Taco. A piñata is a solid tile, closed or open. A bump is a step that ends with the body's head flush against its underside after Poncho was rising, so standing or walking under it, and a corner clip that slides him past the edge (GDD-MOV-03), do not open it. The body box must overlap the tile's column; with two neighbouring piñatas, the one whose middle is nearer his own wins. A piñata opens once.
- In Base it releases a taco standing on top of it (GDD-PWR-02). If there is no room above it (a wall or block in the way) it stays closed and releases nothing, so an item never spawns inside a wall (GDD-PWR-06). In Taco or Fuego it releases a chile instead (GDD-PWR-03), by the same room rule. The kind is decided by Poncho's state at the moment of the hit.
- Losing a life closes every piñata again and clears every released item, along with Poncho returning to Base (GDD-PWR-01).
- A claw swipe opens a piñata that its box overlaps, by the same rules as a head bump (GDD-CMB-02), for instance from the top of a wall next to it. The sandbox piñata can be reached that way from the 2-tile wall at columns 10 and 11.
- Not built yet: the multi-piñata with hilos, the empty piñata art that is not a shell, and sounds. The sandbox has three piñatas, each 48 px above where Poncho stands to jump at it, so a standing jump bumps it with his 32 px body: one at column 8, row 10, 48 px above the floor, at the right end of the Alacrán's platform and directly above the door, which it closes off, so it can be bumped from below only once the door is open (it moved from column 9, user, 2026-10-25, to open the way up the 2-tile wall, GDD-WLD-05), one at column 23, row 7, 48 px above the top of the 3-tile wall, which Poncho reaches by standing on the wall (a chile comes out when he is in Taco), and the special one at column 26, row 2, 48 px above the top right ledge, which always gives the Chile Fantasma (GDD-PWR-04). `src/sim/pinata.ts`, `src/sim/run.ts`.

### GDD-PWR-06 Item rules

Status: Proposed

- At most one active item per piñata. Items never spawn inside a wall.
- Items are collected by touch with the body hitbox (GDD-MOV-06).
- There is no reserve slot in the MVP. Whether to add one (as in some Mario games) is an Open Question.
- The veladora (a life, GDD-LIFE-03) is the only extra-life item.

## 7. Controls

### GDD-CTL-01 Keyboard

Status: Proposed

| Action | Keys |
| --- | --- |
| Move | Arrow keys or A / D |
| Jump | Space, Z or W / Up |
| Claw | X or J |
| Fuego (fireball) | C or K |
| Pause | Escape or P |
| Start (title screen) | Enter, Space or Z on "Start", or any claw, Fuego, left or right key |
| Menus: title, pause, settings (choose, pick) | Up or W, Down or S; Enter, Space or Z |
| Settings (change a value) | Left or A, Right or D |

Keys are remappable (GDD-UI-03). Pause, the title screen and the settings screen are built (GDD-UI-02); the pause keys, Enter and the menu keys go by their label, so they work on any layout.

### GDD-CTL-02 Gamepad

Status: Proposed

Standard gamepad mapping: left stick or D-pad to move (analog speed), south button to jump, west button to claw, east button for Fuego, start to pause. Dead zone 0.2.

- **Built (the menu buttons and the start button on the title decided by the user 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** the game reads every connected gamepad that reports the browser's standard mapping (others are ignored), all at once, together with the keyboard and the touch controls. Pads need no setup: the browser shows a pad once one of its buttons is pressed.
- **In a level:** south jumps, west claws, east spits a fireball in Fuego, start pauses. The D-pad's left and right move at full speed. The left stick moves by its sideways push: nothing inside the dead zone of 0.2, then speed grows evenly to full at the stick's edge (analog speed, GDD-MOV-01), as the slide stick does (GDD-CTL-03). The D-pad and the stick add up with each other and with the keyboard and touch, capped at full speed either way, so left on one and right on another cancel out. The stick counts as a press of Left or Right (for the screens that count presses, GDD-LIFE-02, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02) when its sideways push is past the dead zone and larger than its up or down push, so a push mostly down is neither. Up and down do nothing in a level.
- **In the menus** (the title menu, the pause menu, the settings screen, and since 2026-10-31 the results menu, GDD-UI-02; user, 2026-10-25): the D-pad and the left stick work like the arrow keys, south like Enter and start like Escape. Up and down choose a row, left and right change a volume, south picks the chosen row, and start pauses, resumes from the pause menu and leaves the settings screen. On the stick a direction counts once its push along that way is past half (0.5) and larger than its push across, so a diagonal counts as one direction. Each press counts once: a button or direction held down does not repeat, and the stick counts again only after it comes back under half.
- **On the title screen** (user, 2026-10-25): start starts the level, whichever row is chosen, and so does a new press of west, east, or Left or Right on the D-pad or the stick, as claw, Fuego, Left and Right do on the keyboard. South picks the chosen row, like Space. Start on the settings screen opened from the title leaves it, back to the title menu, and does not start the level.
- **Held through a pause:** the same rules as the keys (GDD-UI-02). A button or direction pressed while paused, or down when the level starts from the title screen, does nothing until it is released and pressed again: south, west and east like the jump, claw and Fuego keys, and the D-pad's left and right and the stick like the Left and Right keys. For the stick, "released" means back inside its dead zone. One held since before the pause and not let go keeps working after it; one released and pressed again while paused is a new press. The *Siete vidas gastadas* screen (GDD-LIFE-05) counts a gamepad press like any other.
- **Read once per frame:** browsers report a gamepad only when the game asks, so the pad is read once each frame, and a press shorter than one frame can be missed (user, 2026-10-25: accepted; the keyboard and touch latch such taps, GDD-CTL-04). Real presses last several frames.
- `GAMEPAD` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/gamepad.ts`. Remapping the buttons comes with GDD-UI-03.

### GDD-CTL-03 Touch

Status: Proposed. Required for the MVP (GDD-MVP-01).

- Landscape layout with two thumb zones. Left: horizontal slide stick or left/right buttons (player choice). Right: Jump (large) and Claw buttons, plus a Fuego button that appears only in the Fuego state (GDD-PWR-03).
- Minimum touch target 48 CSS px, buttons offset from the screen edge by the safe-area inset.
- Layout can be mirrored and controls opacity adjustable (GDD-UI-03).
- Multi-touch: moving and jumping at the same time must work. Prevent page scroll, zoom, long-press menus and text selection while playing.
- Short vibration (50 ms) on life loss where the platform supports it. Audio unlocks on first touch (GDD-AUD-01), which is usually the tap that starts the game on the title screen (GDD-UI-02).
- **Built so far:** left and right buttons bottom-left and a larger jump button bottom-right, drawn as a framed triangle (the poncho motif) semi-transparent, brighter and orange while pressed (their opacity is a setting, see "Opacity" below). Buttons are 40 px (jump 56 px) with 6 px from the screen edge (from the safe area's edge where an inset reaches into the picture, see "Safe-area insets") and a 4 px gap between left and right, and they grow on a small screen so none is under 48 CSS px (`TOUCH` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). They show only on devices that report touch, are hidden on the title screen, where a finger on the mode switch changes nothing (GDD-UI-02), and work together with the keyboard. Left and right pressed together cancel out. Moving and jumping at once works with two fingers.
- **Slide stick (built, details Proposed):** a small switch above the movement thumb zone (bottom-left, or bottom-right in the mirrored layout) swaps the left and right buttons for one track as wide as both, and back. The choice is remembered on the device (browser storage, where allowed). A finger anywhere in the lower left quarter of the screen (the lower right in the mirrored layout, see "Mirrored layout") steers, by how far it is left or right of the track's centre: nothing inside a dead zone of 0.2 of the half-track, then speed grows evenly to full at the track's edge and stays full beyond it (analog speed, GDD-MOV-01). Jump stays in the other thumb zone and works together with the stick. The switch is as large as the other buttons (48 CSS px minimum). A finger that went down while the game was paused, or that is down when the level starts from the title screen, presses no button and does not steer until it is lifted (GDD-UI-02).
- **Claw button (built):** a button the size of the left and right buttons sits beside Jump on the same floor line, on the screen's inner side (left of Jump, or right of it in the mirrored layout), a gap of 4 px from it, drawn with three slanted claw marks; it works together with moving and jumping (GDD-CMB-02).
- **Fuego button (built):** the same size as the claw button, directly above it with a gap of 4 px, drawn as an orange flame. It exists only while Poncho is in Fuego: it appears when he becomes Fuego and goes when he is hit or loses a life (GDD-PWR-03). A press spits a fireball, and, like jump and the claw, a tap shorter than a frame still counts (GDD-CTL-04).
- **Pause button (built):** top-right, see GDD-UI-02.
- **Vibration (built; user, 2026-11-01: built first of the touch pieces still missing; details Proposed):** the step a life is lost vibrates once for 50 ms, whatever took it (a hit, a pit, a crush) and the last life too; a step that keeps or gains lives does not. It is handed to the browser only where `navigator.vibrate` exists (Android browsers; Safari on iPhone has none, so nothing happens there), whichever device is being played with. `TOUCH.vibrateMs` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/vibration.ts`.
- **Opacity (built; user, 2026-11-01: three steps, "Low / Medium / High", Medium the look as it was, Low about half, High about one and a half times, a pressed button still brighter than an idle one; details Proposed):** the "Button opacity" row of the settings screen (GDD-UI-02) puts a factor on every alpha the touch controls draw, their fill, outline and icons, the slide stick, the mode switch and the pause button: 0.5 at Low, 1 at Medium and 1.5 at High, capped at 1. A tutorial tip's dashed outline round a button (GDD-UI-05) is not changed. `TOUCH_OPACITY` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/touchOpacity.ts`.
- **Mirrored layout (built; user, 2026-11-01: "Pause stays top-right", only the two bottom thumb zones swap sides; details Proposed):** with the "Layout" row of the settings screen (GDD-UI-02) at Mirrored, every touch control but the pause button is placed at its mirror image across the screen's vertical middle: jump bottom-left with the claw button right of it (and Fuego above that), the left and right buttons or the slide stick bottom-right with the mode switch above them. The left and right buttons trade their mirrored places, so the left button stays left of the right one. The slide stick then reads a finger anywhere in the lower right quarter of the screen (outside the mode switch) instead of the lower left, by its distance from the track's centre as before. The pause button stays top-right. `src/ui/touchLayout.ts`.
- **Safe-area insets (built; user, 2026-11-02: built after the mirrored layout, screenshots approved; details Proposed):** each touch button keeps its distance from the edges of the screen's safe area, the part no notch, rounded corner or home indicator covers, as the browser reports it (`viewport-fit=cover` in `index.html`, CSS `env(safe-area-inset-*)`), instead of from the screen's edges. On each side the inset that counts is how far the safe area's edge lies inside the picture, past the letterbox bar on that side (none where the bar is wider than the inset), turned into game px and rounded up. The pause button moves down by the top inset and every other button up by the bottom inset; each moves right by the left inset if its middle is in the left half of the screen, or left by the right inset if it is in the right half, so in the mirrored layout a button moves by the inset of the side it ends up on. Sizes and gaps stay, and with no insets nothing moves. The slide stick still reads its whole lower quarter of the screen, the inset strip included, by its distance from the moved track's centre. The insets are read every frame, so a phone turned round, which can move a cutout to the other side without changing the window's size, moves the buttons at once; they are laid out again only on a frame where the insets changed. On a landscape iPhone (844 x 390 CSS px, insets 47 left and right and 21 at the bottom) the side insets lie inside the letterbox bars and the bottom one is 15 px, so the bottom buttons end 21 px above the screen's bottom edge, clear of the home indicator. A side inset that reaches into the picture brings the buttons on its side over an end of the settings screen's rows when that screen is opened from the pause menu (GDD-UI-02): from 5 px on the claw's side (the claw or Fuego button) and from 21 px on the other (the right or left button, or the slide stick), on a landscape phone and on larger screens, in either layout (measured from the layouts in `src/ui/settingsMenu.test.ts`). That needs a screen of about 16:9 with a cutout, like the approved screenshot's 40 CSS px (24 px) cutout on the left; on wider phones the bars hold the side insets, and a bottom inset alone brings no button over a row. `src/ui/safeArea.ts`, `src/ui/touchLayout.ts`.

### GDD-CTL-04 Input latency budget

Status: Proposed

Input is sampled once per fixed step (GDD-TEC-02). A press of jump, claw, Fuego, left or right, on the keyboard or on a touch button, that starts and ends inside one frame is latched from the raw event and counts for the next step (`src/ui/inputLatch.ts`, L-006), so a quick tap or a hitch never loses it; a tap that short makes the shortest jump, moves Poncho for one step, or skips the respawn wait. Left and right also reach the simulation raw, before they cancel out into one move, so the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen sees a press of either (GDD-LIFE-05). A gamepad cannot be latched: the browser reports it only when asked, so it is read once per frame and a press shorter than one frame can be missed (GDD-CTL-02). Jump press to first upward movement happens on the same step. Total input to screen budget: under 100 ms on a mid-range phone (GDD-TEC-03).

---

## 8. World and levels

### GDD-WLD-01 Structure

Status: Proposed

Seven worlds, matching the seven lives and the seven triangles. Each world has 3 levels and 1 boss. A world map screen links them. 7 x 4 = 28 stages in the full game. Before them comes the tutorial level 0-1 (user, 2026-10-26; GDD-WLD-06), which counts as "World 0" and follows World 1's rules. Built: the tutorial, World 1 (1-1 to 1-3 and Don Zopilote's arena 1-4) and World 2 (2-1 to 2-3 and La Estrella's arena 2-4), played in that order (`src/levels/index.ts`, `src/levels/world2.test.ts`), and test levels opened by their address, in no world's order (GDD-TEC-04); no world map yet (GDD-UI-02).

### GDD-WLD-02 The seven worlds

Status: Proposed. Names and themes are a starting point for the user to react to.

| # | World | Setting | Signature element |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Desierto | Chihuahuan desert village, adobe, prickly pears, cholla and barrel cacti, agave fields (Decided, user, 2026-10-25: the Chihuahuan desert, with no saguaros, which grow only in the Sonoran; GDD-ART-06) | Tutorial world; basic enemies; sand slopes |
| 2 | Mercado | Rooftops, awnings and stalls of a hill town's market on market day: a tianguis, the market hall, the rooftops (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14), and a plaza for its boss, La Estrella (GDD-BOS-03) | Bouncy awnings (toldos, GDD-WLD-10); papel picado strung as cordeles and rolled up as rollos (GDD-WLD-11) |
| 3 | Sierra | Sierra Madre cliffs and old mines: a canyon, an old silver mine, the trestles and peaks above it (GDD-WLD-16 to GDD-WLD-18, designed, not built), and a boss arena for its boss, the mine locomotive La Catorce (GDD-BOS-04) | Mine carts on rails (vagonetas, GDD-WLD-15); crumbling ledges (GDD-WLD-05); the first world played with the glide (GDD-MOV-07, GDD-PRG-01) |
| 4 | Selva | Rain forest and ruins | Vines, water, glide-friendly updrafts |
| 5 | Cenote | Underground flooded caverns of the Yucatán | Water physics, darkness with light spots |
| 6 | Volcán | Volcano and lava fields | Rising lava, timed platforms |
| 7 | Mictlán | Día de Muertos finale, ofrendas and marigold paths | All mechanics combined; the final boss |

### GDD-WLD-03 Level anatomy

Status: Proposed (Decided: up to 8 screens from World 2 on, user, 2026-11-04: "8 screens", "World 2 on only"; World 3's levels may be taller than one screen, user, 2026-11-10: "Camera follows up and down", their height limit and rules Proposed and not built)

- Length: 3 to 6 screens wide at 480 game pixels (GDD-ART-01) in Worlds 0 and 1, and 3 to 8 screens from World 2 on (Decided, user, 2026-11-04: "8 screens", "World 2 on only"), so a perfect run of a World 2 level takes about 45 s (`LEVEL_RULES.maxScreens`, checked by `src/levels/validate.test.ts`). Mostly horizontal; vertical sections allowed. (The test levels have no exit: the sandbox and the slopes level are one screen wide (GDD-MOV-09), World 2's labs `lab-2m` and `lab-2e` and World 3's cart lab `lab-3c` two screens (GDD-TEC-04); the level validator, GDD-TEC-04, checks the length and the exit of every other level, except a boss arena. A boss arena (1-4, GDD-BOS-02; 2-4, GDD-BOS-03) is one screen wide too, with no exit and no ofrendas; the validator holds it to its boss-level rule instead, GDD-BOS-01.)
- 3 ofrenda checkpoints at roughly 25%, 55% and 85% of the level, and an exit (not in a boss arena, whose checkpoint is its start, GDD-BOS-01). (The sandbox test level puts the middle one at about 65% because 55% is in its pit; see GDD-WLD-05. The slopes level, a test level too, has its three between its slopes, at about 38%, 52% and 65% of its width; GDD-MOV-09. World 2's labs, test levels too, have three each where their exhibits need them, GDD-TEC-04.)
- **Exit (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-25: a placeholder clear screen):** a box one tile wide and three tiles tall (16 x 48 px) standing on the ground (the floor, or the top of 1-3's last cliff, GDD-WLD-09, or of a rooftop in 2-1, 2-2 and 2-3, GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14), its middle at the level's exit point. Poncho reaches it when his body box overlaps it, as with an ofrenda: boxes that only share an edge do not touch, and it works in the air. A step that costs a life (a hit in Base, a fall into a pit, a crush) does not reach it; a hit that the taco or the Fuego absorbs does. Reaching it ends the level: from that step nothing moves, and the level clear screen comes up (GDD-UI-02). Placeholder look: a wooden arch, two posts and a lintel, with a marigold pennant on top. A test level has none, nor does a boss arena: beating its boss clears it (GDD-BOS-01). `EXIT` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/run.ts`.
- 1 hidden secret per level (a room or a veladora; a boss arena has none, GDD-BOS-01).
- Teaching order: new mechanic is introduced in a safe spot, then combined with a threat, then used in a test.
- Gaps never exceed 3 tiles in World 1 and in the tutorial 0-1 (jump distance is about 4.1 to 4.3 tiles at full run, GDD-MOV-01 to GDD-MOV-04). The level validator checks it for every level (GDD-TEC-04).
- **World 2's gaps (built, Proposed, Open Question 13):** open gaps of at most 4 tiles (`LEVEL_RULES.maxGapTiles` `2: 4`). A 4-tile gap needs a run-up: worked out by hand, the body has to travel 52 px centre to centre, a jump at full run covers about 66 px and a standing jump about 54 px, so it is a real but fair step up from World 1's 3 and still a tile short of the 5 that a running jump crosses (below; a standing jump over one is not measured yet, L-003, while the bots of 2-2 and 2-3 cross their 4-tile open runs with running jumps, `src/levels/level2-2.test.ts`, `src/levels/level2-3.test.ts`). **Covered pits:** a pit column is covered by a whole awning (GDD-WLD-10), a cordel or a rollo (GDD-WLD-11, its whole line, rolled up or not) that spans the whole column, with its top at or above the ground at the pit's nearer edge (the left one when both are as near) and at most 3 tiles (48 px, `COVER.abovePx`) above it. A covered column does not count towards a gap, and no run of uncovered pit columns may be wider than the world's limit (`openGaps` in `src/levels/validate.ts`, checked part by part by `src/levels/validate.test.ts`). A rollo counts because the claw is a move Poncho always has (GDD-CMB-02) and a rollo always lies before the pit it bridges; a torn awning does not, since it is gone after one use. World 2's levels have pits up to 14 tiles wide (2-2's aisle) whose awnings, cordeles and rollos leave at most 4 tiles open in a row (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14).
- Measured with the real body box (12 x 32 since 2026-10-25, GDD-MOV-06) against tiles (`src/sim/collision.test.ts`): a full jump can climb onto a wall 3 tiles high but not 4, and a running jump can cross a gap of 5 tiles but not 6. Gaps are measured edge to edge. The body only has to overlap the far edge, so a 5-tile gap (80 px) costs about 4.25 tiles (68 px) of travel from centre to centre, which is why it fits the 4.1 to 4.3 tile jump distance above. Walls taller than 3 tiles block the path; gaps wider than 5 tiles are impassable without a power. Design to the World 1 limit of 3 so there is room for mistakes.
- **World 3's gaps (Proposed: the World 3 design swarm's consolidated rule, 2026-11-10; of it only the same-height limit of 6 tiles is in the validator, built with the cart lab `lab-3c`, `LEVEL_RULES.maxGapTiles` `3: 6`, GDD-TEC-04; Open Question 13):** World 3 is played with the glide (GDD-PRG-01), so its limit depends on the drop. An open gap is a run of uncovered pit columns, edge to edge, read left to right, the way the levels run.
  - **Width by drop:** at most 6 tiles, plus 1 tile for each tile the right bank lies below the left, up to 14 tiles (a drop of more than 8 tiles counts as 8).
  - **Width by rise:** with the right bank 1, 2 or 3 tiles higher, at most 4, 3 or 2 tiles. A rise of more than 3 tiles over a pit is not allowed; over ground it is a wall, and needs a lift, a spring or a cart.
  - **Roof:** the limit assumes 6 empty tiles over the left bank across the whole gap. Each tile missing takes 2 tiles off, never under 1; a headroom of 3 tiles with no drop is not allowed.
  - **Direction:** a gap that is legal only downhill is one-way, and no route may need it backwards.
  - **Banks and covers:** a crumbling tile is a bank at its top's height, not a pit column, since World 3's ledges come back (GDD-WLD-05). A rail, a trestle, a cart, a buffer and a moving platform cover nothing. World 2's covers keep their rule; World 3 has none of them.
  - **Rails** have limits of their own (the warning post, the level run before a broken end, the chasm past it and the headroom over a cart jump, GDD-WLD-15), and a Cacomixtle's rail has its sight stretch (GDD-ENM-04).
  - **Where the numbers come from (the table is measured by the swarm's scratch test on the real `step`, as GDD-MOV-07's; the limit is a choice laid over it):** the widest gap he crosses, in tiles, by how far the far side lies below the near side (a negative drop is a far side higher up). The limit is two thirds of what a perfect jump and glide crosses (6 of 9, 8 of 12, 10 of 16, 14 of 23), with at least 3 tiles spare. It holds with no run-up, and with the jump taken 1 or 2 tiles early at every drop from 2 tiles up to 8 down. It fails two measured mistakes: a short hop with the glide pressed late at the same height (5 tiles), and a jump 2 tiles early onto a far side 3 tiles up (1 tile). A gap over 5 tiles (6 from a drop of 2) cannot be crossed without the glide, so the first gap at the limit comes after the glide's safe teaching beat (GDD-WLD-16). Under a roof (empty tiles over the takeoff ground all the way across; best glide, then the jump 2 tiles early): 6 or more is open sky; 5 gives 8 and 5 tiles at the same height; 4 gives 5 and 2; 3 gives 3 and 0.
  - The three levels as designed keep the rule (a scratch check over the laid-out data of 3-1 and 3-3, a script over 3-2's grid; GDD-WLD-16 to GDD-WLD-18).

| Drop to the far side (tiles) | -3 | -2 | -1 | 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 6 | 8 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| No glide, best run-up | 4 | 4 | 5 | 5 | 5 | 6 | 6 | 6 | 7 | 8 |
| Glide, best run-up | 4 | 6 | 7 | 9 | 11 | 12 | 14 | 16 | 19 | 23 |
| Glide, standing at the lip, no run-up | 3 | 5 | 6 | 8 | 10 | 12 | 13 | 15 | 18 | 22 |
| Glide, jump taken 1 tile early | 2 | 4 | 6 | 7 | 9 | 11 | 13 | 14 | 18 | 21 |
| Glide, jump taken 2 tiles early | 1 | 3 | 5 | 6 | 8 | 10 | 12 | 13 | 17 | 20 |
| Short hop (0.1 s), glide pressed late | 0 | 3 | 3 | 5 | 7 | 8 | 10 | 12 | 15 | 19 |
| World 3's limit | 2 | 3 | 4 | 6 | 7 | 8 | 9 | 10 | 12 | 14 |

- **Tall levels (that World 3's levels may be taller than one screen is Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Camera follows up and down"; the numbers and rules are Proposed, the design swarm's, and not built):** a level is 17 to 51 rows tall (816 px, three screens). Every level built is 17 rows. 3-1 is 51 rows and goes down, 3-3 is 37 rows and goes up, and 3-2 stays 17 (GDD-WLD-16 to GDD-WLD-18). The camera follows Poncho up and down in a level taller than one screen (GDD-UI-04).
  - **A drop is not a pit:** empty space with ground below it inside the level is a drop: he lands and loses nothing. Only a column open at its bottom costs a life (GDD-LIFE-02).
  - **Raised bottom (Proposed, Open Question 38):** a level may give spans of columns a bottom row higher than its last row. In a span the floor line is under that row: he is lost when his body box is wholly below it, and the camera's bottom edge stops 2 px above it. So a pit high on a canyon's rim costs a life as fast, and looks the same, as a pit in a one-screen level, in place of a fall of up to 30 rows on camera. Under a raised bottom every column is solid all the way down or empty all the way down. A pit that costs a life has its raised bottom at most 4 rows under the lower of its two lips. Enemies, tacos, fireballs and rocks keep falling to the level's last row. The two tall levels were laid out with a pit as a column open to the level's last row; their raised bottoms are set with the build.
  - **Sight rules (Proposed; the layouts of 3-1 and 3-3 were not checked against them):** a landing meant to be reached by walking off or by a plain jump has its top at most 6 tiles below the lip. A deeper one is a glide drop: at most 16 tiles between footholds, with a line of hilos from the lip to a landing at least 6 tiles wide. Nothing that hurts stands within 3 tiles of where a drop of more than 6 tiles lands, and no pit column lies within 4 tiles of it on the far side. A landing he must jump up to is at most 3 tiles above his feet (a full jump climbs 3 tiles and not 4, above); anything higher is reached by a spring, a lift or a cart whose top end a hilo or a rail shows from its foot. Nothing harmful is in the 8 tiles over a spring. A cart's slope runs at most 8 tiles of height without a level stretch of 4 tiles or more. A landing, a post or an enemy is not placed where it would first show under a phone's buttons, the bottom 62 px of the screen's outer 120 px on either side (GDD-CTL-03). A respawn point stands on ground at least 3 tiles wide with 7 tiles of open air above. The exit and the level's first screen have 4 tiles of level ground on each side.
- **Ofrendas and carts (Proposed, World 3):** no ofrenda stands on or past a rail it serves, or on a stretch a Cacomixtle's cart rolls, and a lost life stands every cart at its start (GDD-WLD-15), so no respawn is past a cart he rides.

### GDD-WLD-04 Collectibles

Status: Proposed

- **Hilos (threads):** colourful threads scattered through levels; 50 per level (a boss arena has none, GDD-BOS-01). They unlock cosmetics for the poncho (GDD-PRG-01) and feed the score (GDD-PRG-03).
- **Hilo pickups (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-23: pickups and the HUD counter first, placeholder skeins in serape colours):** a hilo is an 8 x 8 px pickup in the middle of its tile, collected when Poncho's body box overlaps it (GDD-PWR-06); boxes that only share an edge do not touch. It is collected on any step he is in play, also the step he is hit or crushed, and not while he waits to respawn or during the respawn flip. A collected hilo is gone for good: losing a life does not bring it back or take it away (GDD-LIFE-02), and neither does the restart after the last life (GDD-LIFE-05). A hilo saved in an earlier run (GDD-PRG-02, saved at the level clear or when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up) is drawn faded, at 35 % opacity, and cannot be collected: Poncho passes through it, no sound plays, and it counts as collected from the run's start, on the HUD counter (GDD-UI-01), on the level clear screen and for the medal (GDD-PRG-03). A fresh run (quitting to the title, "Retry", the next level, the restart in 1-1 after the last life is lost in 1-2, 1-3 or the boss arena 1-4, or in 2-1 after one lost in a later World 2 level, a new visit) brings back every hilo not saved yet. Several touched in one step are all collected. Placeholder look: a round skein of thread with two dark wraps and a short loose end; the hilos take the serape's colours in turn, in the order the level lists them (red, yellow, green, blue, pink), so a trail reads like a poncho stripe. The sandbox has 20 (user, 2026-10-23: about 20 on the one-screen test level; real levels keep 50, and 0-1 has 50, GDD-WLD-06), in short trails along the path: on the floor before the door, over the Alacrán's platform, on the 2-tile wall, above the spring and on the spring ledge, one over the middle of the pit, a reward for a precise crossing: a jump over the cactus buddy from the top of the 2-tile wall that lets go of right in the air to come down on the spring, then holds right again, is thrown over the pit through it (holding right all the way flies past the spring since the ledge moved up, GDD-MOV-06) (other crossings, such as a running jump from the spring ledge, pass under it), over the spikes, on the 3-tile wall, over the crumbling tiles, along the moving platform's route and on the Tlacuache's ledge. `HILO` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/hilo.ts`. Each step that collects hilos plays the hilo sound, climbing a major scale (GDD-AUD-02). Not built yet: hilos from items that would not improve the state (GDD-PWR-01) and from the multi-piñata (GDD-PWR-05), and the score (GDD-PRG-03); the hilos are saved per level at the level clear and when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (GDD-PRG-02).
- **Veladora:** one per level at most, restores a life (GDD-LIFE-03).
- Collectibles guide the player's eye along the intended path and reward risk.

### GDD-WLD-05 Hazards and interactables

Status: Proposed (that a crumbled ledge comes back in World 3 is Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Come back after 2 s"; not built)

Core set: spikes (cactus, obsidian), pits, crumbling platforms (0.5 s warning), springs (bounce 480 px/s), moving platforms, moving blocks, fragile walls (claw-breakable), ofrendas (checkpoints), doors and keys. Each world adds one signature hazard (GDD-WLD-02).

- **Spikes (built, details Proposed):** a spike stands in one tile and hurts through the lower half of it (16 px wide, 8 px tall), so a jump that clears the points by a hair is safe. Touching it with the hurtbox (GDD-MOV-06) from any side costs one life, like a pit: the wait, the flip and 2.0 s of invulnerability follow (GDD-LIFE-02). Unlike a pit, a spike does not hurt while Poncho is invulnerable (GDD-CMB-03). Invulnerability only skips the hit, it does not move Poncho: if he is still on a spike when the 2.0 s end, he loses a life on the next step. If a spike hit and an ofrenda touch happen in the same step, the hit wins and the ofrenda is not activated. In Taco a spike hit spends the taco instead (GDD-PWR-01, GDD-CMB-03). The sandbox level has two spikes between the second ofrenda and the 3-tile wall. Cactus and obsidian art, and the "not stompable" marking for spike enemies (GDD-CMB-01), come later. `SPIKE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/hazards.ts`.
- **Crumbling tiles (built, details Proposed):** one-tile ledges that are solid like a floor. A tile starts to crumble when Poncho lands on it: his body box over its column and his feet on its top. A jump up from below, or walking into its side, does nothing. At the top of a sand slope it starts once he stands on its top, not while his feet are on the slope beside it (GDD-MOV-09). The warning lasts 0.5 s (30 steps) from the step he lands, shown by the tile shaking sideways and flashing pale in turn (movement and colour, GDD-UI-03). Then it is gone: Poncho falls, with the usual 0.1 s of coyote time to still jump (GDD-MOV-03). The countdown cannot be stopped: if he walks off, the tile still goes. Each tile has its own timer, so standing on the join of two starts both. A gone tile stays gone until Poncho loses a life, and then every tile is whole again when he respawns (GDD-LIFE-02). The sandbox level has three at tile columns 24 to 26, a jump up from the 3-tile wall. Level 1-2 has thirteen in three places: a walkway over the floor, a bridge over spikes and three a spring throws Poncho onto (GDD-WLD-08). Level 1-3 has five, a bridge over spikes between two sand ramps (GDD-WLD-09). Sand-coloured placeholder art with a crack. `CRUMBLE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/crumble.ts`.
- **Crumbling ledges in World 3 (that they come back is Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Come back after 2 s"; the details Proposed; not built):** in World 3's levels and in its arena (GDD-WLD-16, GDD-WLD-18, GDD-BOS-04; 3-2 has none) a crumbled ledge is whole again 2.0 s (120 steps) after it falls, so each has a beat of 2.5 s from the landing. It does not come back while Poncho's body box is in its tile (Proposed). Worlds 0 to 2 keep the rule above: a gone tile stays gone until a life is lost. A lost life still makes every tile whole at once. So in World 3 a ledge counts as a bank in the gap rule (GDD-WLD-03) and may be the only floor over a pit: he cannot wait on a ledge for the next one, since his own goes first, but from solid ground he can wait and try again. No cart, ridden or empty, starts a crumbling tile (a rider's feet are on the cart's floor, 8 px above the tile's top); a rail may lie on crumbling tiles and stays when they are gone (GDD-WLD-15); an Armadillo does not start one (GDD-ENM-04). In the arena La Catorce's slam starts a tile that Poncho's weight does not (GDD-BOS-04). World 3 draws the one mechanic two ways, a rock ledge and a plank (GDD-ART-06).
- **Springs (built, details Proposed):** a one-tile block, solid like a floor and set flush into it. Standing on its top, by landing on it or by walking onto it, throws Poncho up the same step. The plain launch is 480 px/s, about 77 px up, clearly above a jump (410 px/s, 56 px). A **timed press** gives a higher one: if Poncho pressed jump in the 0.1 s (6 steps) before he lands, counting the landing step itself, the launch is 600 px/s, about 120 px (the user's rule, 2026-10-21: a timed press only, holding jump does not count; the 600 is a proposal). Walking onto the spring, or holding jump from earlier, gives the plain launch. Either launch is fixed once it starts: releasing jump does not cut it short, and a jump pressed in the next steps does not replace it with the weaker 410 px/s jump, nor, beside a wall, with a wall kick (GDD-MOV-08). Touching the spring from the side or below does nothing. Red-and-cream placeholder art with a coil. The sandbox level has one in the floor between the 2-tile wall and the pit, clear of the touch buttons, and a ledge 112 px above the floor (tile columns 13 and 14, row 7) that only the timed launch reaches: the plain launch falls 35 px short and the timed one clears it by about 8 px. Level 1-2 has three, each set into the floor in front of what it throws Poncho onto (GDD-WLD-08). `SPRING` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/spring.ts`.
- **Moving platforms (built, details Proposed):** a slab 8 px thick and any whole number of tiles wide that travels in a straight line between two ends and back, at 40 px/s, turning round at each end without stopping. Where it is depends only on how many steps have passed, so it is identical on every run. It is a **one-way floor**: Poncho lands on its top when he comes down onto it, and while he stands on it he is carried with it (sideways and up and down). He lands on it when his feet end a step at or below its top and began the step at or above its top where it was or where it is now, whichever is lower, so a platform that rises past his falling feet within one step catches him too (user, 2026-11-02: "Fix it"; fixed with 1-3, GDD-WLD-09: before, he could drop through it); he does not land where standing on it would put his body box into anything solid above it (a level tile, a crumbling tile, a spring, a piñata, a shut door or a fragile wall), so a fragile wall over a rising platform stops his head like a ceiling (checked by `src/sim/platform.test.ts`). He passes through it from below, so he can jump up through it and land on top, and its sides and underside never block him, so it cannot crush him (only a moving block can, below). If it would carry him into a wall or a ceiling he stays against that tile and the platform slides on under his feet: it never pushes him through a tile. Jumping from it works at once; walking off an end drops him. Platforms ignore sand slopes, and a level keeps their paths clear of them (GDD-MOV-09). Platforms stand still during the 0.8 s respawn wait and start from their first end again when Poncho respawns (GDD-LIFE-02). The sandbox level has one, 3 tiles wide with its top at y = 128, 16 px below the spring ledge's top, starting against the ledge's right edge and travelling 96 px right, towards the crumbling tiles (near its right end the piñata at column 23 stops a Poncho it carries at x = 362, held there and not crushed); from its right end a jump reaches the Tlacuache's ledge in the top right corner, 32 px higher (GDD-ENM-02). Level 1-3 has three: two lifts that rise from 2 tiles above the floor to the top of a 6-tile cliff, and a shuttle over eight spikes (GDD-WLD-09). Teal placeholder art. `PLATFORM` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/platform.ts`.
- **Moving blocks (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-23: a solid block that moves like the platforms and crushes):** a solid block any whole number of tiles wide and tall that travels in a straight line between two ends and back at 40 px/s, turning round at each end without stopping, like a moving platform, and identical on every run. Unlike a platform it is solid on every side: Poncho lands on its top and is carried with it while he stands there (sideways and up and down), bumps his head on its underside, and is stopped by its sides. When it moves into him it pushes him along in the direction it moves. If that push, or carrying him up, would force his body box into a solid tile, he is **crushed** (GDD-LIFE-02). If it would carry him sideways into a wall he stays against the wall and the block slides on under his feet, as a platform does; carried down while part of him hangs over a tile beside it, he stays on that tile and the block goes on down without him (it never pushes him through a tile). Its sides are not walls to cling to (GDD-MOV-08). Blocks ignore sand slopes, and a level keeps their paths clear of them (GDD-MOV-09). Only Poncho collides with it for now: enemies, walking tacos and fireballs pass through it. Blocks stand still during the 0.8 s respawn wait and start from their first end again when Poncho respawns. The sandbox has one, one tile square, in column 28 of the pocket past the fragile wall: it travels from just under the Tlacuache's ledge (top y = 112) down to the floor (top y = 208) and back, starting at the top. So Poncho is crushed if he is under it when it reaches the floor, or if he rides it up into the ledge; column 29 beside it, against the level's right edge, is a safe spot. Level 1-3 has four crushers, one tile wide and two tall: two that come down to the floor, one in the open and one just past an Alacrán's pen, and two out of step in a low corridor; it places its enemies so that their own walks keep clear of them, but a Tlacuache let out of its pen walks through them (GDD-WLD-09). Grey stone placeholder art with dark corner studs. `BLOCK` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/block.ts`.
- **Doors and keys (built, details Proposed):** a door is one tile wide and any whole number of tiles tall, solid like a wall, and is opened as one piece by a single key. A key is a 12 x 12 px pickup in the middle of its tile, picked up by touch with the body box (GDD-PWR-06) and carried; all keys are alike. A shut door that Poncho touches at its side while he carries a key opens for good that step and uses up one key: he touches it when his body box, widened by 1 px on each side, overlaps its column and overlaps it in height, so walking into it counts and standing on top of it or bumping it from below does not. One key opens one door, even if he touches two at once. A key picked up the same step a door is touched opens that door. An open door is gone and does not close again. Losing a life drops every carried key, puts every key back on the floor and shuts every door, like taco pickups and piñatas (GDD-PWR-01, GDD-PWR-05). The HUD shows one small key per key carried, right of the power-up icon. The sandbox has one door three tiles tall at column 8, closed off above by the piñata at the platform's right end (GDD-PWR-05), and one key at column 5 on the way to it. Past the door, column 9 is open to the sky, so from the floor there a short hop lands Poncho on the 2-tile wall (checked by a test that plays the route from the spawn, in `src/levels/sandbox.test.ts`). Level 1-3 has three doors, each four tiles tall, with a key before each (GDD-WLD-09). Walking tacos treat a shut door as a wall (GDD-PWR-02). Colours or pairs of keys, doors that need a switch, and sounds come later. `KEY` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/door.ts`.
- **Fragile walls (built, details Proposed):** papel picado tiles, solid like a wall until a claw swipe (GDD-CMB-02) overlaps them, which breaks every tile its box overlaps, for good. A wall taller than the 32 px claw box is broken from the height Poncho swings at, every tile the box overlaps, so one swipe from the floor breaks the bottom two tiles and opens a gap two tiles high, which he can walk through (his body is 32 px tall, GDD-MOV-06). Losing a life puts every broken tile back (GDD-LIFE-02). Walking tacos treat an unbroken tile as a wall. Drawn as a pink paper tile with a cut-out diamond and a pale scalloped top edge (placeholder art). The sandbox has a wall four tiles tall, so a jump cannot clear it, at column 27 in the pocket at the right end. Level 1-3 has three, each four tiles tall; a full jump clears the second from the top of a 2-tile wall one floor column before it (GDD-WLD-09). Not built yet: other ways to break them (fireballs, GDD-PWR-03), a break animation and sound. `src/sim/fragile.ts`.
- **Ofrenda:** one tile wide and two tiles tall (16 x 32 px), standing on the floor. It is activated when Poncho's body box (GDD-MOV-06) overlaps it; boxes that only share an edge do not touch. It does not need a button press and works in the air, so a jump through it counts.
- Placeholder look until real art (GDD-ART-01): an outlined frame when unused, filled with a flame on top once used, so the two differ by shape and not only by colour (GDD-UI-03). The sandbox level has three, at about 25%, 65% and 85% of its width; the 55% spot of GDD-WLD-03 is in its pit.

### GDD-WLD-06 Tutorial level 0-1

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-10-25: the first real level, three screens long, teaching the basics, with an exit, the three ofrendas, 50 hilos and a hidden veladora; no glide, wall cling, Zopilote, Tlacuache, doors or moving blocks yet. User, 2026-10-26: it is called 0-1, a tutorial with texts and highlights popping up, GDD-UI-05, and its short run is fine). The layout is Proposed.

- **Place in the game (user, 2026-10-26):** 0-1 is a tutorial played before World 1, outside the seven worlds (GDD-WLD-01). It follows World 1's rules (gaps of at most 3 tiles, GDD-WLD-03). It starts with seven lives, and its lives do not carry into World 1: 1-1, which its results menu leads on to, starts with seven again (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-WLD-07). Its "Retry" starts it with seven again too, out of lives it restarts at its own start, and its lives are never kept in the save (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-PRG-02). Its texts are in the language chosen on the settings screen (GDD-UI-02). It is played once (user, 2026-10-27): "Start" plays it until it has started once, and the title menu's "Tutorial" row plays it again at any time (GDD-UI-02).

- **Size:** 90 x 17 tiles (3 screens, 1440 x 272 px), the shortest length GDD-WLD-03 allows. The floor's top is row 14 (y = 224), and Poncho starts on it at tile column 3. The glide and the wall cling are off (GDD-MVP-02). `src/levels/level0-1.ts`.
- **Teaching order (GDD-WLD-03).** Screen one teaches running and jumping with nothing that hurts: a 1-tile step (columns 7 and 8), a 2-tile wall (11 and 12), a 1-tile gap (16) and a 2-tile gap (19 and 20), then the first ofrenda (23). Screen two brings one threat at a time: an Alacrán on the floor (starting at column 32) between two 1-tile blocks (27 and 37) that it turns at, a piñata at column 40, row 10 (48 px above the floor) that gives the taco, two spikes (43 and 44), the second ofrenda (49) and a 3-tile gap (53 to 55). Screen three combines them: a 3-tile wall (58 and 59), a raised platform (row 10, columns 62 to 68, top y = 160) with an Alacrán on it and three spikes on the floor under it (64 to 66), a 3-tile gap (71 to 73), the third ofrenda (76), a third Alacrán (starting at column 81) between a 1-tile block (78) and a last 2-tile wall (83 and 84), and the exit at column 87.
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the veladora (GDD-LIFE-03) lies off the path on a 2-tile ledge high over the second 3-tile gap (row 7, columns 70 and 71, top y = 112). A full jump from the right end of the raised platform, 48 px below it, reaches it. The tiles cannot hide anything yet, so it is in plain view (Open Question 15).
- **Ofrendas** at columns 23, 49 and 76, about 26%, 55% and 85% of the width. Every Alacrán starts at least 3 tiles from the start and from each ofrenda (GDD-ENM-01).
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), in trails along the path: on the floor at the start, over the step and the walls, over each gap, in the first Alacrán's pen, beside the piñata, over the spikes, on the 3-tile wall, over the raised platform, one on the veladora ledge, in the third Alacrán's pen and on the last wall. Each one is within a jump of a floor in its column or within two columns of it.
- **Hilos in play:** holding right from the start, with no jump, collects the first three on the floor by the 150th step (2.5 s).
- **Played through:** holding right and jumping at each wall, gap, spike and Alacrán reaches the exit with all seven lives in under 15 s (900 steps), checked by `src/levels/level0-1.test.ts`. That is far shorter than the 2 to 4 minutes of GDD-LOOP-02, which is fine for a tutorial (user, 2026-10-26).
- **Tips:** twelve, one for each thing it teaches, listed in GDD-UI-05.
- **Known phone issue:** on a landscape phone the camera stops at the level's right edge, so the exit (column 87, game x 1392 to 1408, on screen 432 to 448) and Poncho at it sit under the touch Jump button (GDD-CTL-03). It still plays; moving the exit and the last wall a few columns left would clear it, which is the user's call.
- Drawn with World 1's backdrop and ground tiles (GDD-ART-06); its other things keep their placeholder art until theirs is drawn.

### GDD-WLD-07 Level 1-1

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-10-27: the first level of World 1, five to six screens long; it brings in the Zopilote, the cactus buddy and a piñata with a chile, on top of what 0-1 taught; a press on 0-1's clear screen started it, and since 2026-10-31 picking "Next level" on 0-1's results menu does, GDD-UI-02). Screen three brings in the cactus buddy, on the floor at column 68, then a 2-tile wall (72 and 73) beside the second piñata, at column 75, row 10: it gives a taco in Base and a chile in Taco or Fuego (GDD-PWR-05). A chile stays on top of the piñata, where a hop from the wall top reaches it; from the wall top a claw swipe opens the piñata too. Then a pen between two 1-tile blocks (78 and 88) holds two Alacranes (starting at columns 82 and 85), which fireballs spat from the top of its left block defeat (GDD-PWR-03). Screen four combines them: a 2-tile wall (94 and 95), the second ofrenda (99), a 3-tile wall (103 and 104), and a raised platform (row 10, columns 107 to 113, top y = 160) with an Alacrán on it (from column 110) and three spikes on the floor under it (109 to 111), then a 3-tile gap (116 to 118). Screens five and six test them: a 2-tile wall (122 and 123) with a cactus buddy past its foot (128), a 3-tile gap (130 to 132) under the second Zopilote (cruising at y = 136 from column 131), an Alacrán (from column 140) between two 1-tile blocks (136 and 145), the third ofrenda (153), a cactus buddy (158), a last 2-tile wall (162 and 163), and an Alacrán (from column 167) between that wall and a 1-tile block (171), then the exit.
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the veladora (GDD-LIFE-03) lies on a 2-tile ledge high over the 3-tile gap after the raised platform (row 7, columns 115 and 116, top y = 112). A full jump from the right end of the raised platform, 48 px below it, reaches it, as in 0-1. It is in plain view (Open Question 15).

- **Enemies (GDD-ENM-02):** six Alacranes, two Zopilotes and three cactus buddies, each cactus on the floor. No Tlacuache (user, 2026-10-27).
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), in trails along the path: on the floor at the start, over the blocks, walls and gaps, in the pens, beside the piñatas, over the spikes and the cactus buddies, under the first Zopilote, on the raised platform, one on the veladora ledge, and on the last wall. ### GDD-WLD-08 Level 1-2

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-02: "I want to spawn a agent swarm so we do progress on the lvls generate all lvls left for world one."; World 1's levels are to "Add sand slopes too"; "1-2: the Tlacuache, crumbling tiles and springs. 1-3: moving platforms, doors and keys, fragile walls and moving blocks. Everything is built already, so agents only lay out and test the levels."; sand slopes are "Ramps you run on": "Poncho runs up and down at his usual speed, his feet stay on the slope going down, and he jumps from it as from flat ground. Enemies walk on them too."; "Three PRs in turn": "Built in parallel, landed one after another (1-2, then 1-3, then the boss)."; its screenshots: "Approved"). The layout is Proposed.

- **Place in the game:** World 1's second level (GDD-WLD-01), played after 1-1, with World 1's rules (gaps of at most 3 tiles, GDD-WLD-03). Picking "Next level" on 1-1's results menu starts it (GDD-UI-02) with the lives Poncho has at 1-1's exit (GDD-LIFE-05; user, 2026-11-02: "Build it as decided"), "Retry" on its own results menu starts it again with the lives at its exit (user, 2026-11-02: "Lives at the clear"), and `?level=1-2` opens it with seven lives; its results menu's "Next level" starts 1-3 (GDD-WLD-09, GDD-UI-02) with the lives Poncho has at 1-2's exit (GDD-LIFE-05). Running out of lives in it shows the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen, and the press that ends it starts 1-1, World 1's first level, with seven lives (GDD-LIFE-05). "Start" plays it while the World 1 run the save keeps is at 1-2: from 1-1's clear, with the lives at that clear, until 1-2 is cleared, which moves the run on to 1-3 (GDD-WLD-09), or World 1's lives run out (GDD-UI-02, GDD-PRG-02; user, 2026-11-02: "Follow the run", "Save the lives"). It has no tips (GDD-UI-05) and no placeholder lines. The glide and the wall cling are off (GDD-MVP-02). `src/levels/level1-2.ts`.
- **Size:** 180 x 17 tiles (6 screens, 2880 x 272 px), as 1-1. The floor's top is row 14 (y = 224), and Poncho starts on it at tile column 3. The exit stands at column 174 (GDD-WLD-03), as in 1-1: with the camera at the right edge it is on screen from 384 to 400 px, left of the phone's Jump button (from 418 px).
- **Sand slopes (GDD-MOV-09)** run through the whole level, 45 slope tiles: gentle dunes a tile high (`rR` up, `Ff` down), 45° hills and ramps (`/` up, `\` down) up to three tiles high, and one long 45° slope six tiles down. Every slope has a level tile under it (checked by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04). Poncho runs over them at his usual speed with no jump: holding right from the start, he runs over the first dune and the first hill on the ground every step and first leaves the ground where the ramp after them ends at a gap, its middle over the gap's left edge (x = 416 to 418; measured by `src/levels/level1-2.test.ts`).
- **Teaching order (GDD-WLD-03).** Sand slopes first, then the crumbling tiles, the Tlacuache and the spring, each in a safe spot, then with a threat, then in a test:
  - Screen one brings in sand slopes with nothing that hurts: a gentle dune (columns 8 to 13: `rR`, a top at 10 and 11, y = 208, `Ff`), a 45° hill two tiles high (17 to 22: `/` at 17 and 18, a top at 19 and 20, y = 192, `\` at 21 and 22) and a gentle ramp (`rR` at 24 and 25) up to the edge of a 2-tile gap (26, 27), jumped from the slope.
  - Screen two puts a threat among the slopes and brings in crumbling tiles in a safe spot: a piñata at column 30, row 10 (48 px above the floor), a 1-tile block (33), two gentle dunes without a flat top (`rRFf` at 35 to 38 and 41 to 44) with two spikes in the dip between them (39, 40), jumped from one slope to the other, the first ofrenda (47), and a walkway of four crumbling tiles (52 to 55, row 11, top y = 176), reached by a 45° ramp three tiles high (`/` at 49, 50 and 51) and left by a 2-tile-wide pillar (56, 57, top y = 176) and a 45° ramp down (58 to 60). The walkway is over the floor, 48 px up: running over it holding right never leaves the ground, standing on it drops Poncho to the floor with no life lost, and from that floor a full jump climbs back onto the pillar (measured by `src/levels/level1-2.test.ts`).
  - Screen three brings in the Tlacuache and the spring in safe spots: a pen between two 2-tile walls (62, 63 and 72, 73) with a Tlacuache in it (starting at column 68). Standing on top of either wall, 32 px above its feet, Poncho is never spotted (measured by `src/levels/level1-2.test.ts`), so he can watch it walk before he drops in; the second piñata hangs over the pen (column 65, row 10). Then a spring set into the floor (77) in front of a wall four tiles high (78, 79, top y = 160): a jump cannot climb it (GDD-WLD-03: 3 tiles, not 4), and running onto the spring holding right throws him onto its top (measured by `src/levels/level1-2.test.ts`).
  - Screen four puts the spring and the crumbling tiles with threats: a cactus buddy just past that wall's foot (81), then a spring (88) in front of two spikes (89, 90) and a second 4-tile wall (91, 92): running onto the spring throws him over the spikes onto the wall, and a jump over the spring lands him on the spikes (measured by `src/levels/level1-2.test.ts`). The second ofrenda (99). A bridge of six crumbling tiles (105 to 110, row 11, top y = 176) over six spikes, between a 45° ramp three tiles high (102 to 104) and a 2-tile-wide pillar (111, 112) with a ramp down (113 to 115): running across holding right is safe, and standing still on it costs a life (measured by `src/levels/level1-2.test.ts`). Then a 3-tile gap (118 to 120).
  - Screen five puts the Tlacuache on slopes and tests the spring with the crumbling tiles: a pen between two 1-tile blocks (122 and 134) holding a 45° hill two tiles high (126 to 131, a top at 128 and 129, y = 192), with a Tlacuache on its top (starting at column 128) that walks up and down it and charges down it at Poncho on the pen's floor (measured by `src/levels/level1-2.test.ts`). Then a spring (137) that, run onto holding right, throws him onto three crumbling tiles (140 to 142, row 10, top y = 160) over six spikes (138 to 143), from which a jump reaches the top of a wall six tiles high (144, 145, top y = 128) before they go; standing still on them costs a life (measured by `src/levels/level1-2.test.ts`). A long 45° slope runs from that wall's top down to the floor (146 to 151).
