- **Piñatas (GDD-PWR-05, L-047):** two, each 48 px over the roof, one empty column past a wall 2 tiles high, as in 1-3: in Base a jump from under it opens it and holding right once the taco has dropped catches it; in Taco a claw from the wall's right end opens it, the chile stays on its top, and a hop takes it (measured for both). The first gives the taco, the second the chile for the Fuego route, which comes after the second ofrenda.
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), each in an empty tile outside every awning, none touched standing at the start or an ofrenda (measured): a floor trail, W1's and T1's columns at the tier heights (the Ki puzzle), over the gaps and the pen's wall, over and in the two pens, six along the torn chain's arcs climbing with its tiers, the breather roof and the second piñata's wall, the mixed chain (the edge, Wa's column, over the chimneys, Wc), L and the step, the Fuego pair on R2's line, the roof before the alley, the alley's column to the bundle, F1, the top of the jump arc from F1 to M, M, over A4, K1, the cordel, K2, the exam's rise and the exit roof.
- **Played through:** from a fresh start with seven lives, a bot (holding right; jumping at walls, drops off level tiles and piñatas, waiting while a walker is where it would come down; clawing enemies in reach; letting a spinning Trompo heading away go and hopping onto a stunned one; holding jump through the torn chain; in the mixed chain hopping in place on Wa, steering onto Tb, letting go past the tall chimney to settle on Wc and waiting there until the Trompo on L has turned at the pit's edge; at each far-post rollo steering its rebounds under the bundle and clawing on the rise; waiting out of the Paloma's sight) reaches the exit through all three ofrendas losing no life, in 2574 steps (42.9 s; asserted under 2700), tearing T2, T3, T4, Tb, Td and T5, stringing R1 and R4, standing on Wc but never on a torn awning, and defeating the Trompo on L, with 40 of the 50 hilos: all but W1's and T1's columns (the ladder and the chain take them), the top two of Wa's column (the hop in place takes them) and the Fuego pair (R2's line takes them); after waiting 1, 2, 3 or 4 s at the start it still clears it losing none (measured). A player's first clear takes longer; whether it reaches the 2 to 4 minutes of GDD-LOOP-02 is for playtesting.
- Drawn with World 2's art (GDD-ART-06), with 20 decor items of all seven kinds (measured by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`); its look approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`). Its rooftops use World 2's ground tiles; chimneys and high roofs of their own are not drawn (Open Question 32). The decor rule stands a maceta on the tall chimney's 1-tile top and a farol on the chimney beside Wc (visible in the shots), as it would on any roof.

### GDD-WLD-15 Vagonetas (mine carts)

Status: Decided (World 3's signature element, from GDD-WLD-02's "moving carts"; the main session's design, 2026-11-10, written before any code, user, 2026-11-10: "Design first"; the cart has no brake, rolls one way and defeats every enemy it hits, user, 2026-11-10: "Approved"; a rider still in a cart at a broken rail end falls on his own, with no crash rule, user, 2026-11-10: "He falls on his own"; a jump keeps the cart's speed, a rolling cart can be boarded and an empty cart comes back after 2 s, user, 2026-11-11: "Yes to all three, build the cart"). Its speeds, sizes and the other details below are Proposed: the World 3 design swarm's detail (user, 2026-11-10: "an agent swarm to design the remaining parts"), reconciled by the main session. Built in part (see "Built" below): the rail, the cart, boarding, rolling, riding, the carry rule and the buffer; not built: the broken end, the warning post and the rail's limits, the empty cart's return, and what a rolling cart does to enemies. A number marked "measured, rule not built" is the real jump flown by the swarm's scratch test in the frame of the speed he left the cart with (GDD-MOV-07's test, outside the repository); every other number is a target until the code exists and measures it.

A vagoneta is an old ore cart standing on a rail. Poncho jumps in and it rolls; he decides when to jump out. **Its one idea:** the cart sets the pace and the path, the jump out is the player's.

- **The rail (riel):** a track fixed in the level, made of level runs and slopes of one tile up or down per tile along, drawn on its sleepers. It is scenery to Poncho: he walks and falls through it, and only carts touch it. A sloped rail lies over slope tiles (GDD-MOV-09) or, over air, on a trestle the game draws under it (GDD-ART-06). A rail may lie on crumbling tiles and on a tile that hangs in the air, and it stays when the tiles under it are gone (GDD-WLD-05). It covers nothing in the gap rule (GDD-WLD-03). A rail ends in a **buffer** (tope, a timber stop), or it is **broken**, its end hanging over a drop. The level gives the way a cart rolls, right or left; for a cart that rolls left every rule here is mirrored. No two carts share a stretch of rail.
- **The cart:** 2 tiles wide and 1 tile tall (32 x 16 px), open on top. Its floor is a one-way floor like a moving platform's (GDD-WLD-05) and rides 8 px over the rail's ground (target): he lands in it from above, is carried with it, and can jump up through it from below. Its sides do not block him; they hide his feet, so he reads as standing in it.
- **Boarding (a rolling cart can be boarded: Decided, user, 2026-11-11; built):** any landing on its floor from above boards it, standing or rolling: a hop, a drop from a ledge, or a walk off a step one tile up, whose 8 px drop onto the floor is a landing. Walking through a standing cart at the ground's height neither boards nor starts it. On the level a rolling cart cannot be caught (it gains 30 px/s on him); on a climb he gains 35 px/s on it, which is the second chance, and a drop from a ledge onto a passing cart works.
- **Waiting, then rolling (built):** a cart stands still at its start until Poncho lands in it. The landing step is its step 0, and it first moves on the step after (the main session's choice, Proposed). From then it rolls along its rail, away from its start, at 150 px/s on the level, 120 px/s uphill and 180 px/s downhill, each measured along the rail: faster than he runs (120 px/s, GDD-MOV-01) on the level and downhill, and no faster uphill. Across the screen that is 150, 84.9 and 127.3 px/s, and it climbs at 84.9 px/s and drops at 127.3 px/s (from the speeds). The speed changes on the step the cart's middle passes a join. One tile column of rail takes 0.107 s on the level, 0.189 s uphill and 0.126 s downhill, and one screen of level rail 3.2 s. It never stops or turns for him: there is no brake. Where it is depends only on the steps since he landed in it.
- **Riding:** he stands in it and can walk inside it, jump, swing the claw and spit Fuego as on any moving floor.
- **A jump keeps the cart's speed and never beats it (the carry rule; Decided, user, 2026-11-11; built, its details Proposed):** on the step his feet leave a rolling cart's floor (a jump, a walk off its end, or the floor leaving him) he keeps the cart's speed across at that step as a carried speed until he lands. In the air, no input and forward input both hold exactly that speed; holding against the roll slows him by up to 120 px/s at the usual air acceleration of 600 px/s². He is never faster across than the cart he left, and the carried speed does not follow the cart afterwards. A wall ends it, and he drops straight down the wall's face. On landing on anything but a rolling cart his speed across is his own again and GDD-MOV-01's ground rules take it from there: from 150 px/s he is back at 120 in 3 steps holding forward, and stops in 9 steps and 11.25 px with no input (measured, `src/sim/cart.test.ts`). Landing in a rolling cart, his speed becomes his speed in the cart again, the cart's taken off it: so a Poncho who drops at a standstill onto a passing cart slides back in it as he would stop on the ground, and off its back if he lands in its back third. Jump height, gravity and the glide are unchanged. (Today's code keeps none of a moving floor's speed in a jump, which is right at a platform's 40 px/s and wrong at a cart's.)
- **What the carry rule gives (measured over the built rule, `src/sim/cart.test.ts`; the figures of this section's first text, from a scratch test, are corrected here):** at 150 px/s on level rail a full jump with no input lands him back in the cart 33 steps (0.55 s) after takeoff, 82.5 px on from where the takeoff step left him (85.0 px from where he stood before it), on the spot in the cart he left; forward input changes nothing. With the glide held it is 67 steps (1.12 s) and 170.0 px. Holding against the roll from the takeoff step he comes down 28.9 px ahead of his takeoff and 58.6 px behind the cart's middle: the cart has left him, and he is never slower across than 30 px/s in the air. From a cart on a climb (84.9 px/s across) the jump lands back in it after 26 steps and 38.2 px, since the floor rises to meet him; on a descent (127.3 px/s) after 50 steps and 108.2 px, since it falls away. Still from the swarm's scratch test, to be measured when a level needs them: down to the rail's ground, 8 px lower, the jump carries 86.1 px (5.38 tiles) and the glided jump 187.1 px (11.69 tiles), and each tile of drop adds 34.3 px (2.14 tiles) of glide. A ledge reached by a jump from a cart has its top at most 3 tiles over the rail's ground (40 px over the floor, under the jump's 56.4 px).
- **At a buffer** the cart stops and he walks off it. A buffer blocks carts only: Poncho, the walking enemies and a rolling Armadillo pass it (GDD-ENM-04).
- **At a broken end (that the rider falls on his own is Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "He falls on his own"; the details Proposed):** the cart leaves the rail on the step its middle passes the end, and from that step it is no floor. A rider still in it is in the air at the carried speed, with the usual 0.1 s of coyote time (GDD-MOV-03; 15 px at 150 px/s) to jump, and he can glide; if he does nothing he falls onto whatever lies below, the ground, a spike or a pit. There is no crash: staying in costs what the fall costs and nothing more. The cart keeps its speed across and falls at 2250 px/s² up to 360 px/s; it breaks on the first level tile, spike or floor it touches, or falls out of the level. A falling cart is no floor and defeats nothing; in the arena it is what hurts La Catorce, unless he rode it off (GDD-BOS-04).
- **The warning post and a rail's limits (Proposed; the validator's, GDD-TEC-04):** a lit warning post stands 6 tiles (96 px) before every broken end, 0.64 s at 150 px/s (the 2 tiles of this section's first text are 0.21 s, under the 0.5 s telegraph of GDD-ENM-01). The post is derived from the rail, not placed by hand: every broken end has one, also an end that is not to be jumped from (3-2's rail F, GDD-WLD-17), and a buffer has none. A broken end comes after at least 6 tiles of level rail, so the cart is at 150 px/s, and at least 12 tiles (1.28 s) from the cart's start. From the rail's end to the far bank is at most 5 tiles plus one per tile of drop: a glided jump taken as early as the post still lands (187.1 px less 96 px; measured, rule not built). The first broken end of the world, in 3-2, is at most 3 tiles from its bank, which a plain jump taken up to 2 tiles early crosses (a rule about the order of levels, so 3-2's own test holds it). Where he must jump from a cart there are 7 empty tiles over the rail's ground: the floor's 8 px, the jump's 56.4 px and his 32 px make 96.4 px, so 6 tiles are 0.4 px short. The boss arena is exempt from the post, the level run and the 12 tiles (GDD-BOS-04).
- **An empty cart comes back (Decided, user, 2026-11-11; not built, the details Proposed):** a cart that reaches its rail's end with nobody in it, stopped at a buffer or fallen from a broken end, is back at its start, standing, 2.0 s (120 steps) later; a cart he rode to a buffer returns 2.0 s after he steps off it. So a Poncho who jumps out early and lives is not stranded on the near side of a chasm. In the arena it takes 3.0 s (GDD-BOS-04). A Cacomixtle's cart is the exception: with its rider or emptied, it stays where it stops until a life is lost, and an emptied one can be ridden from there (GDD-ENM-04). Not settled: where a returning cart appears if Poncho or an enemy stands at its start, and what counts as stepping off (Open Question 37; the swarm's cross-check suggests the arena's hoist for every cart, drawn arriving for its last 30 steps and not yet a floor).
- **Enemies:** a rolling cart defeats every enemy its own box runs into, the heavy ones too, with the usual defeat (GDD-ENM-01), a rolling Armadillo and a Cacomixtle among them (GDD-ENM-04). The rider's body is still his own: a Murciélago at his head's height is his hit, not the cart's (GDD-CMB-03). A standing cart is a floor to them and nothing more, and a level keeps its walkers and a rolling Armadillo away from a standing cart's start. An enemy riding a cart is its own enemy (GDD-ENM-04).
- **A lost life** puts every cart back at its start, standing (GDD-LIFE-02), so a missed jump can be tried again from the last ofrenda.
- **Low beams (vigas):** a mine's roof timbers are solid tiles. A beam's underside is 2 tiles (32 px) over the rail's ground, 24 px over the cart's floor: it stops a rider (his body is 32 px tall, GDD-MOV-06) while the cart rolls on, and a walker passes under it. A roof 3 tiles over the ground (40 px over the floor) lets a standing rider through. Two uses: a **roof** of low beams ends the ride (he is left standing and drops 8 px to the ground), and a **hanging beam** one tile along, with open space over it, is a hop: he jumps it and lands back in the cart. The hop's takeoff window is 9 steps (0.15 s), 25 to 45 px before the beam's near face, or 16 steps (0.27 s), 25 to 62.5 px, with the glide held (measured geometry on the real jump path, rule not built). A hanging beam is never 2 tiles along (a window of 2 steps) and stands only where there is ground beside the rail. They hurt nobody.
- **A boss's hit-stop** holds carts still like everything else (GDD-BOS-01).
- **Look (Proposed, not drawn):** the rail, the buffer, the broken end, the warning post, the cart and the low beam are described in GDD-ART-06; the three rail ends differ by shape, a block, splayed tips and a diamond on a pole with a lit lamp.
- **Built (details Proposed):** `src/sim/cart.ts`, with the carry rule in `src/sim/movement.ts` and the shared one-way floor in `src/sim/platform.ts`; `CART` in `src/config/tuning.ts` (`width` 32, `height` 16, `floorPx` 8, `speedLevel` 150, `speedUp` 120, `speedDown` 180). A level lists its rails (`rails`), each a start, a way (right or left), an end (a buffer or broken) and its stretches, each level, a climb or a descent and a whole number of tiles across; each rail has one cart, standing at its start with its back edge on the rail's start. The carried speed is the cart's speed across on the step he leaves it, with his own speed in the cart added only where it is against the roll, at most 120 px/s of it; holding against the roll in the air slows him at 600 px/s² to at most 120 px/s under the carried speed, also while the Chile Fantasma runs (GDD-PWR-04); a wall, a slope's high side or any landing ends it, and a moving platform or block still gives a jump none of its 40 px/s (GDD-WLD-05). A low beam is a solid tile and needs no rule of its own: the cart is not carried into it with him. Tested in `src/sim/cart.test.ts`, over the test level `lab-3c` in `src/levels/lab3c.test.ts` (GDD-TEC-04) and in `e2e/cart.spec.ts`. Drawn as placeholders in World 3's colours (GDD-ART-06) until its art is drawn: the rail's two rows on its sleepers, the buffer block, and a rust tub whose front wall is drawn over his feet; the placeholder look's screenshots await the user's approval.
- **Open (Open Question 37):** the speeds and the details the user has not confirmed.

### GDD-WLD-16 Level 3-1

Status: Proposed (Decided: the name "La Barranca", user, 2026-11-10: "Approved"; that the level is taller than one screen and the camera follows, user, 2026-11-10: "Camera follows up and down"; that its crumbled ledges come back, user, 2026-11-10: "Come back after 2 s"). The layout below is Proposed: the World 3 design swarm's, written before any code. Not built. "Measured" in this section means measured by the swarm's scratch tests, which built this layout as level data and played it on the real `stepRun` with the glide on, outside the repository; the level's own test must pin each number when it is built (L-003, L-014). The Murciélago is not built, so "against the proposed loop" is a measured path checked against GDD-ENM-04's loop with the game's stomp rule. The ledges' return and the camera are not in those tests: what is said of them is a target.

- **Place in the game:** World 3's first level, "La Barranca" (the canyon; the name Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Approved"): the path leaves the hill town and goes down into a canyon of the Sierra Madre, to the mine's mouth. "Next level" on 2-4's results menu starts it with seven lives, and once 2-4 is cleared "Start" plays World 3's run, as World 2's rules have it for 1-4 and 2-1 (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02). Clearing 2-4 unlocks the glide (user, 2026-11-10: "Glide everywhere", GDD-PRG-01), and this is the first level designed for it; the wall cling is off. `src/levels/level3-1.ts` (to be).
- **What it teaches:** the glide (GDD-MOV-07), then crumbling ledges in rows (GDD-WLD-05), then the Murciélago (GDD-ENM-04), with the Alacrán as the review enemy (GDD-ENM-02). No cart, no rail, no cactus buddy.
- **Size:** 240 x 51 tiles (8 screens wide, 3 screens tall, 3840 x 816 px), the tallest GDD-WLD-03 allows. Poncho starts at column 3 on the rim (row 9, y = 144) and the exit stands at column 234 on the canyon floor (row 48, y = 768) in the mine's mouth, at the screen position 2-1's has. The 51 rows are 9 rows of sky over the rim (so the camera, stopped at the level's top, shows him at 53% of the screen's height), 39 rows of descent and 3 of floor.
- **Shape: one descent, no climb.** The only steps up are seven 1-tile steps, stones and pen rocks. The path folds back under itself down a cliff face: right along a ledge floor, left along a gallery under it, right again under that. It is 280 tiles long in 240 columns.
- **Ground (the top row of each stretch; a pit is open to the level's bottom, and takes a raised bottom with the build, GDD-WLD-03):** columns 0 to 17 row 9; 18 row 11 (the trench's step); 19 to 23 row 13 (the trench's floor); 24 to 37 row 9; 38 and 39 row 8 (a stone); 40 to 63 row 13; **64 to 70 a pit**; 71 to 77 row 15; 78 and 79 row 14; 80 to 85 row 15 (the pen); 86 and 87 row 14; 88 to 103 row 15; **104 to 112 a pit**; 113 to 133 row 19; 134 and 135 row 18 (a stone); 136 to 168 row 38 (the lower gallery's floor, with pen rocks at 147 and 155, row 37); **169 to 186 a pit**; 187 to 194 row 41; **195 to 205 a pit**; 206 to 216 row 43; 217 to 221 row 47 (the pillar); **222 to 226 a pit**; 227 to 239 row 48.
- **The cliff face (columns 136 to 164), solid pieces over the lower gallery:** the upper floor F1, columns 136 to 157, rows 25 and 26, with a rock at column 150 (row 24); the middle gallery's floor F2, row 31, columns 139 to 145 and 150 to 160, with four crumbling tiles between (146 to 149); the buttress, columns 161 to 164, rows 17 to 31. Two shafts join them: 158 to 160 (F1 down to F2, 6 rows) and 136 to 138 (F2 down to the lower gallery, 7 rows). The middle gallery is 4 tiles high, the lower one 6.
- **Teaching order (kishōtenketsu, GDD-WLD-03).**
  - *Ki, columns 0 to 63: the glide with no way to fail.*
    - *On the flat (0 to 17).* The tip (below) and four hilos in the line a glided jump draws over level ground: held, a jump lands 8.5 tiles on; let go at the top, 4.3 (measured).
    - *The trench (18 to 23).* 6 tiles at the same height, its floor 4 tiles down, its far wall 4 high: the way out is the step at column 18, back to the near side. Held from the lip he lands at column 26.2; without the glide he lands in the trench (measured). The tip stays up.
    - *The long glide (38 to 63).* A piñata on the rim (below), a 1-tile stone as the lip, then solid ground 5 tiles down for 24 columns: held, he lands at column 56.8, 17 tiles and 2.4 s on (measured). Five hilos on the line: height buys distance. The first ofrenda (59).
  - *Shō, columns 64 to 135: real pits, the bat alone, the bat in a glide's way.*
    - *The first chasm (64 to 70):* 7 tiles, 2 down (limit 8). Without the glide there is no crossing; held, he lands at column 75.7 (measured).
    - *The pen (78 to 87):* two rocks, a 6-tile pen and an Alacrán between them (x = 1287 to 1369, measured). A held jump from rock to rock carries him over (measured): World 3's widest gap at the same height, rehearsed over safe ground.
    - *The first Murciélago (column 93), alone over flat ground:* it bobs (`ry` 24) about y = 188, half its loop in his way, half over his head. Walking through blind is a hit in 8 of 24 start times (measured against the proposed loop). Two hilos over it are the stomp's held bounce (target).
    - *The second chasm (104 to 112), a bat in a long glide's path:* 9 tiles, 4 down (limit 10); the far bank is on screen from the lip. A bat bobs under the line (column 108, y = 230): the held glide passes over it in 17 of 24 start times and comes down on its back in 7, a stomp, never its side (measured against the proposed loop). Held, he lands at column 119.1 (measured).
    - *Rest (113 to 135):* the second piñata, the second ofrenda (131), a stone as the lip.
  - *Ten, columns 136 to 186: the ledges fall, and the path folds back.*
    - *Down the face, over a floor.* From the stone: the ledge L1 (139 to 141, row 21) and L2 (145 to 147, row 22), 3 crumbling tiles each, with F1 (row 25) under them all: a miss costs nothing and the low road goes the same way. A held glide overshoots a 3-tile ledge, so here he learns to let go (held from the stone and let go at column 138.4, he is on L1 at 141.1, measured). From L2 a held glide flies the length of F1, over its Alacrán (x = 2423 to 2521) and a bat (column 153, y = 351) that it clears in 17 of 24 start times and stomps in 7, into the shaft at 158 (measured; the bat against the proposed loop).
    - *The middle gallery, right to left (160 to 139).* Under F1, 4 tiles high: no room to glide. Its floor is four crumbling tiles wide in the middle (146 to 149): at a run he is across in 0.5 s; if he stops he drops into the lower gallery, which is the way on. The shaft at its left end (136 to 138) drops 7 tiles to the lower gallery.
    - *The lower gallery, left to right (136 to 168).* A second pen (rocks at 147 and 155, an Alacrán, x = 2375 to 2473) under a 6-tile roof: a hop over the first rock into the pen and one held jump from there land him on the far rock (measured by the bot); a rock-to-rock glide over its 7 tiles is a target, and the stomp and the claw are the other answers. The gallery opens to the sky at column 165.
    - *Over the pit (169 to 186, 18 tiles; the ledges are the only floor).* L3 (172 to 174, row 39), L4 (178 to 181, row 40), then solid ground (187, row 41): gaps of 3, 3 and 5 tiles, each 1 down or level (limits 7). Three ways across, all measured: short jumps from ledge to ledge; one held glide from the gallery's edge that skips L3 and lands on L4 at column 180.1; and from L4 a held glide to the rest at 191.9. One way down: a held glide from L3 flies past L4 into the pit. That is the first ledges' lesson with a price.
  - *Ketsu, columns 187 to 239: all of it.* Rest (187 to 194). A 4-tile gap, a crumbling ledge (199 to 201, row 42), a 4-tile gap, a solid ledge (206 to 216, row 43) with the third ofrenda (208) and a bat patrolling its far end at body height (column 214, y = 672, `rx` 16): a held glide from the crumbling ledge lands at column 211.9, a tile and a half short of the bat, and one hop clears or stomps it (10 stomps and no hit in 24 start times, measured against the proposed loop). Then the last glide: 10 tiles on to the canyon floor, 5 down, over the pillar and a 5-tile pit, past two bats. The first (column 220, y = 664) bobs under the line: passed over or stomped, never a side hit (9 stomps in 24). The second (column 225, y = 640) bobs across the line and must be read: blind, it is a hit in 9 of 24. Held from the lip he lands at column 233.8, at the arch, after 2.4 s; without the glide he does not arrive (measured).
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the pillar (217 to 221, row 47) stands under the last lip, 4 tiles down, with the veladora on it (column 219, row 46), in plain view (Open Question 15). The glide carries him over it; walking off the lip with jump up drops him onto it at column 219.4 and takes it (measured). The way on is 5 tiles to the floor 1 down: the held glide lands at 231.9 (measured), and a plain running jump crosses it too (GDD-WLD-03's table: 5 tiles at a drop of 1; the swarm's scratch test had it falling in, which the review of PR #93 corrected with a probe on the real simulation), so leaving the secret never needs the glide. Neither bat reaches that lower line (0 of 24 start times). It keeps every hilo.
- **The ledges come back (GDD-WLD-05; the user's rule, 2026-11-10: "Come back after 2 s"):** twenty crumbling tiles in six places. A ledge is gone 0.5 s after he lands and whole again 2.0 s after that. He cannot wait on a ledge for the next one (his own goes first), but from solid ground he can: a Poncho who lands on L3 and hops back to the gallery waits at most 2.0 s and tries again. A run never waits, since every ledge ahead is whole. No ledge is a way up, none holds an item and no enemy stands on one. The four in the middle gallery's floor return over his head once he has dropped through.
- **Pits and long falls:** five pits. Everything else is a fall onto a lower path, and each has a way on: the trench (its step), under L1 and L2 (F1, the low road, the same way as the ledges), the middle gallery's crumbling floor and both shafts (the lower gallery). No fall leaves him where he cannot go on.
- **Ofrendas** at columns 59, 131 and 208 (24.8%, 54.8% and 86.9% of the width). The longest replay is from the pit of Ten back to the second ofrenda, about 12 s through both galleries (Open Question 41).
- **Gaps (GDD-WLD-03's World 3 rule; measured by the scratch check, crumbling tiles as banks):** 7 (drop 2, limit 8), 9 (4, 10), 3 (1, 7), 3 (1, 7), 5 (1, 7), 4 (1, 7), 4 (1, 7) and 5 (1, 7). Every left bank has open sky: the lower gallery's roof ends 4 columns before the pit. No gap is needed leftward.
- **Enemies: nine.** Three Alacranes (from columns 82, 153 on F1 and 151 in the lower gallery) and six Murciélagos (columns 93, 108, 153, 214, 220 and 225; the last at `phase` 120, the rest 0; `rx` at most 16, `ry` 24). No enemy starts within 3 tiles of a respawn; no bat's loop comes within 82 px of one or touches a tile (measured). A stomped bat stays gone, and no bat is a needed step. Four of the six can only be stomped or passed from the main line; two must be read.
- **Piñatas (GDD-PWR-05):** two, each 48 px over the ground with a stone to its right that turns the taco back: column 30, row 5 (the rim) and column 126, row 15 (the rest before the cliff face). Each taco is back at a waiting Poncho 5.5 s after the bump (measured).
- **Hilos:** 50, none in a tile and none touched at a respawn (measured): 4 on the flat's arc, 3 over the trench, 5 on the long glide, 4 over the first chasm, 1 and 3 at the pen, 2 over the first bat, 3 over the second chasm, 1 at the stone, 2 on L1, 2 on L2, 3 on the glide into the shaft, 2 in the middle gallery, 2 over the lower pen, 5 over the pit, 3 at the Ketsu ledge, 2 at the last lip, 2 on the far bank (where both the glide and the pillar's jump come down) and 1 at the arch. Those in the air are placed on the measured flight paths. A run that never stops takes 45, and with the flat's arc first 48; the last 2 are the first bat's bounce.
- **The tip (proposed text; not in GDD-UI-05's tables or the game's texts until the level is built):** one tip, shown over columns 0 to 23 (the flat and the trench), its highlight the four hilos of the flat's arc. For the keyboard, touch and the gamepad: "Hold Space in the air to glide. Let go to drop", "Hold the jump button in the air to glide. Let go to drop", "Hold the bottom button in the air to glide. Let go to drop"; "Mantén Espacio en el aire para planear. Suéltalo para caer", "Mantén el botón de salto en el aire para planear. Suéltalo para caer", "Mantén el botón inferior en el aire para planear. Suéltalo para caer"; "Leertaste in der Luft halten zum Gleiten. Loslassen zum Fallen", "Sprungknopf in der Luft halten zum Gleiten. Loslassen zum Fallen", "Untere Taste in der Luft halten zum Gleiten. Loslassen zum Fallen". The longest is 68 characters, under 0-1's longest in each language (two lines; a target, not rendered). The second sentence is the designer's addition (Open Question 41).
- **Played through (measured; the bats as loops):** a bot that holds the way the path runs, jumps at each lip, holds across the chasms and lets go over the ledges reaches the exit in 2271 steps (37.9 s) with seven lives and three ofrendas, stomping the patrol bat and touching no other. With three stops of 1 s it takes 40.9 s. The low road under L1 and L2 reaches the lower gallery 31 steps later than the ledges. To measure when built: the stomp's bounce over each bat and where it lands him, a hit in a glide over a pit (Open Question 36), the ledges' return in play, and a bot that waits for the bats.
- **Where a first-time player loses a life:** the first chasm, jumping and letting go as for two worlds (the trench was the same jump with a floor, the tip names the move, four hilos hang on the line, the ofrenda is 5 columns behind); the ledges over the pit, landing on L3 and holding the next jump past L4 (L1 and L2 taught exactly that over a floor, both ledges and the far bank are on screen from the gallery's edge, and a ledge he ruined is back in 2.0 s); the last glide, flying into the second bat (both bats are on screen from the lip, neither moves toward him, the ofrenda is 9 columns behind).
- **Camera (GDD-UI-04):** the layout was drawn for the up-and-down camera: every landing is to be on screen from its lip, except two long glides over unbroken solid ground (the long glide of Ki and L2's glide along F1), where the ground under him is in view all the way and the hilos draw the line. What the layout asks of the camera beyond GDD-UI-04's rule is in Open Question 39.
- **Look:** the canyon's backdrop, the rock ledge for its crumbling tiles (GDD-ART-06, Proposed, not drawn).

### GDD-WLD-17 Level 3-2

Status: Proposed (Decided: the name "La Mina", user, 2026-11-10: "Approved"; that a rider still in a cart at a broken end falls on his own, user, 2026-11-10: "He falls on his own", GDD-WLD-15). The layout below is Proposed: the World 3 design swarm's first-round layout, written before any code, with the user's decisions of 2026-11-10 applied by the main session where they touch it. Not built. Every number is a target, to be pinned by the level's test when it is built (L-003, L-014), unless it is marked measured, which here means measured on the real jump by the swarm's scratch tests for GDD-MOV-07, GDD-WLD-15 or GDD-ENM-04. A script checked the grid's pits against World 3's gap rule, the 50 hilos and the 3-tile enemy rule; nothing was played.

- **Place in the game:** World 3's second level, "La Mina" (the name Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Approved"): the galleries of an old silver mine, timbered, lit by lamps. Lives carry on from 3-1 and into 3-3 (GDD-LIFE-05). The glide is on, the wall cling off (GDD-PRG-01). `src/levels/level3-2.ts` (to be).
- **What it teaches:** the vagoneta (GDD-WLD-15) and the Armadillo (GDD-ENM-04), and the glide under a low roof, in short hops. The Alacrán returns as the cart's target (GDD-ENM-02). It has no crumbling tile, no Murciélago and no Tlacuache.
- **Size and heights:** 240 x 17 tiles (8 screens, 3840 x 272 px), one screen tall, so the camera does not move up or down (GDD-UI-04). Five floor heights and five roof heights:

| Columns | Floor top (row, y) | Roof (solid rows; underside y) | Headroom |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 to 7, the start | 8, y = 128 | 0 and 1; 32 | 6 tiles |
| 8 to 29, the upper gallery | 9, y = 144 | 0 and 1; 32 | 7 |
| 30 to 33, a ramp down (4 slope tiles, GDD-MOV-09) | 9 to 13 | 0 and 1 | |
| 34 to 117, the main gallery | 13, y = 208 | 0 to 4; 80 | 8 |
| 118 to 128, the Armadillo's pen | 13, y = 208 | 0 to 7; 128 | 5 |
| 129 to 138, the pen's far wall and the hops | 11, y = 176 | 0 to 6; 112 | 4 |
| 139 to 239, the upper level | 11, y = 176 | 0 to 2; 48 | 8 |
| 169 to 175 and 232 to 238, two cuttings under a ledge one tile thick (row 11) | 15, y = 240 | the ledge; 192 | 3 |

- **Rails and carts (GDD-WLD-15).** A rail lies in the row above its ground. Every broken end has 6 or more level tiles before it, is 12 or more tiles from its cart's start and has its lit warning post 6 tiles (96 px) before it. Every place where Poncho jumps from a cart has 7 or 8 tiles over the rail's ground. Buffers block carts only. The ramps are slope tiles.

| Rail | Cart starts (columns; middle x) | Segments (columns; ground row, y) | Ends | Ride |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A | 8, 9; 144 | level 8 to 22; row 9, y = 144 | buffer, column 22 | 12 tiles, 1.28 s |
| B | 25, 26; 416 | level 24 to 29, y = 144; down 30 to 33 (4 tiles); level 34 to 43, y = 208 | buffer 43 (back buffer 24) | 1.78 s |
| C | 46, 47; 752 | level 45 to 59, y = 208; 58 and 59 on a trestle | broken, end x = 960; post column 54 | 13 tiles, 1.39 s |
| D | 68, 69; 1104 | level 67 to 81, y = 208; 80 and 81 on a trestle | broken, end x = 1312; post column 76 | 13 tiles, 1.39 s |

| F | 140, 141; 2256 | level 139 to 164, y = 176, 144 to 146 on a trestle; down 165 to 168; level 169 to 175, y = 240 | broken, end x = 2816; post column 170 | 2.56 s to the ramp, 3.8 s to the end |

- **Beams (GDD-WLD-15):** two hanging beams, one tile along, their underside 2 tiles over the rail's ground, both over solid ground: column 97, row 10 (rail E) and column 209, row 8 (rail K2). One rib, the roof coming down to the same height: columns 104 to 106, rows 5 to 10 (rail E). The two cutting ledges are 3 tiles over their rails, so a standing rider passes under them (40 px needed, 48 px there).
- **Pits (GDD-WLD-03's World 3 rule: 6 tiles plus the drop, less 2 for each tile of headroom under 6):** 80 to 84, 5 tiles under a headroom of 8 (limit 6), rail D's broken end, 3 tiles from the rail's end to the bank (the world's first broken end over a pit: at most 3); 131, 132 and 137, 138, 2 tiles each under a headroom of 4 (limit 2), the two short hops under the low roof; 144 to 146, 3 tiles (limit 6), under rail F's trestle; 188 to 190, 3 tiles (limit 6), under rail K1's trestle; 196 to 200, 5 tiles (limit 6), K1's broken end, 3 tiles from the rail's end (at most 5); 216 to 221, 6 tiles (limit 6), K2's broken end, 4 tiles from the rail's end (at most 5). Two drops are not pits: the sunken floor under rail C's end (columns 58 to 62, row 15, 2 tiles down) and the spike hollow under rail F's end (columns 176 to 178, floor row 16, three spikes in row 15, a wall at 179).
- **Teaching order (kishōtenketsu, GDD-WLD-03).**
  - *Ki, columns 0 to 44: the cart with no way to fail.* Poncho starts at column 3 on a step one tile above the upper gallery. Walking off the step at column 8 drops him into cart A, so the first ride needs no jump and no tip. It rolls 12 tiles to its buffer under three hilos only a rider's head reaches. He walks two tiles of floor and over the first tiles of rail B (the rail is scenery, shown once on safe ground), hops into cart B under one hilo, and rides the ramp down: 4 tiles at 180 px/s, hilos down the slope, 10 level tiles and a buffer. No enemy, no pit, no beam. A jump from cart A's last tile lands in cart B, and from B's last tile in cart C (86 px at 150 px/s, measured for the jump, rule not built; the landings are targets).
  - *Shō, columns 45 to 111: the jump out, three times.*
    - *Rail C, the broken end over solid ground.* The first lit post (column 54), the rail on a trestle past the floor's edge (column 57) and its torn end at x = 960, over a floor 2 tiles down and 5 wide; the far bank starts at column 63, 3 tiles past the rail's end. A jump from the cart's last two tiles lands on the bank (measured for a plain jump taken up to 2 tiles early). A rider who stays in falls on his own onto the sunken floor, the cart broken beside him and no life lost; a 2-tile wall on each side lets him out either way. The first ofrenda stands on the bank (column 65).
    - *Rail D, the same picture over a pit.* Cart, 13 tiles, post, trestle, torn end: C's twin to the tile, with the pit 80 to 84 where the sunken floor was.
    - *Rail E, the beams.* A hanging beam (column 97): jump when it is 2 tiles ahead (25 to 45 px before its face, 9 steps, measured geometry) and land back in the cart; hitting it leaves him standing on the floor with the cart gone. Then the rib (104 to 106): it stops every rider, the cart rolls on under it to its buffer, and he walks under (two hilos on the floor row show the way) to the second ofrenda (column 110). The hanging beam is met here so that the chain's beam has been met safely.
  - *Ten, columns 112 to 182: the Armadillo, then the cart as weapon and as danger.*
    - *The pen (on foot).* A piñata (column 114), a 2-tile block (116, 117) that is the seat, the pen (118 to 128) under a roof 5 tiles up, and a 2-tile far wall (129, 130). An Armadillo starts at column 124 and walks the pen. From the seat his feet are 32 px over its feet, so it is not startled (its sight is 24 px up or down): he watches it, drops in, it tucks for 0.5 s and rolls at him, he jumps or glides over it (the roof leaves a 48 px rise), it hits the wall behind him and lies dazed for 1.0 s. Three hilos at jump height cross the pen.
    - *The hops.* From the far wall's top, under a roof 4 tiles up: a 2-tile pit, a 4-tile pillar (133 to 136), a 2-tile pit, and an island (139 to 143) where cart F stands. Under this roof a held glide carries at most 5 tiles (measured, GDD-WLD-03), so each hop held to its end still lands on the pillar or the island.
    - *Rail F, the gallery and the cutting (one ride, two lessons).* The cart crosses a 3-tile pit on a trestle and runs down an Alacrán (from column 149), an Armadillo (153) and an Alacrán (157) on the gallery floor (147 to 164): stay in. Then the rail dives down a ramp (165 to 168) into a cutting whose lit post (170) and torn end (x = 2816) are on screen from the gallery, with three spikes beyond. The way on is the ledge above the cutting (its lip at column 169, x = 2704, level with the gallery floor): a plain jump from the cart's last 28 px before the ramp (x = 2612 to 2640) lands on it, and a jump held into a glide lands on it from anywhere after x = 2511, 0.86 s of rail (both from the measured 86.1 and 187.1 px of GDD-WLD-15). A rider who stays in falls on his own from the rail's end onto the spikes: one life, or his taco, which is what a spike costs and no more (the user's rule, GDD-WLD-15; this end is one not to be jumped from, and it has its lit post like every broken end). The third ofrenda stands past the hollow (column 181). The cart that kept him safe is the one he must leave.
  - *Ketsu, columns 183 to 239: three carts.* K1 crosses a 3-tile pit on a trestle, runs down an Alacrán on the bank beyond (191 to 195), passes its post and breaks: a jump from its last tiles lands in cart K2, 3 tiles on. K2 has a hanging beam (column 209) 0.45 to 0.58 s after he lands, its post where he comes down from the hop, and a 4-tile jump to cart K3 (takeoff from x = 3460; with the glide from x = 3359). K3 is rail F's picture with no lit post: a jump from before the ramp (228) lands on the ledge (its lip at column 232) where the lift cage stands.
- **Exit:** the lift cage up to the peaks, at column 234 on the last ledge (x = 3752, y = 176): on screen at 384 to 400 px with the camera at the right edge, as 2-2's.
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the veladora is at column 238, row 13, at rail K3's buffer in the cutting under the exit. Staying in K3 rides down to it: a rail with no lit post ends in a buffer. One hilo (236, 13) lies on the way. The way out is back up the ramp on foot and a 4-tile jump from its top (column 227) over the ramp to the lip. In plain view (Open Question 15).
- **Ofrendas** at columns 65, 110 and 181 (27.3%, 46.0% and 75.6% of the width, earlier than GDD-WLD-03's rough 25%, 55% and 85%; Open Question 42): each stands on plain floor after a ride's end and 3 to 30 tiles before the next cart's start (68; 140, past the pen; 184), so no respawn is past a cart he rides. They are early because nothing before column 65 can cost a life and the last 56 tiles are the three-cart chain, kept in one piece.
- **Carts are never required:** every pit keeps the gap rule on foot, no wall on the route is over 2 tiles, beams and the rib leave 32 px to walk under, and each ramp's lip is a 4-tile jump from the ramp's top over solid slope. A cart that rolled off without him is back at its start 2.0 s after its rail's end (GDD-WLD-15), and every cart's start can be walked back to: the floors under A, B, E and K2 are unbroken, C's sunken floor has 2-tile walls, F's and K1's trestle pits are 3-tile hops, and both ramps can be walked up. A Poncho in a cutting (he stayed in F or K3, or jumped short onto the ramp) walks back to the ramp, and from its top the lip is a 4-tile jump whose miss lands on the ramp again. On the spikes with a taco, the hollow is 1 tile deep with 4 tiles of headroom: a hop out to the left.
- **Enemies:** two Armadillos (from columns 124 and 153) and three Alacranes (149, 157, 193), each 192 px or more from every respawn (checked by the script). The pen's walls stop the first Armadillo's roll; the second's roll ends in the pit 144 to 146 or down the ramp in the spike hollow, so neither crosses a standing cart's start. The gallery's walkers are held by that pit and the hollow, K1's Alacrán by pits on both sides. The Alacranes and the Armadillo walk a ramp as they do a sand slope (GDD-MOV-09).
- **Piñata (GDD-PWR-05, L-047):** one, column 114, row 9, 48 px over the floor. In Base its taco stands on top, walks right, drops to the floor at column 115, turns at the seat and walks back under the piñata, where he catches it, so a Base Poncho meets the Armadillo with a taco. In Taco or Fuego the chile rests on its top (y = 144), taken by a hop left from the seat (top y = 176, one column away), as 2-2's.
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), as (column, row). A rider's hilo hangs 3 rows over a rail's ground: a rider's head touches it (4 px of overlap with the floor 8 px up), a walker's does not.
  - The start: (4,7) (5,7), leading off the step into cart A.
  - Rider's, along a ride: A (12,6) (15,6) (18,6); B (26,6) over its cart, (37,10) (40,10); C (52,10); D (73,10); E (93,10); F (145,8) over the trestle, (150,8) (154,8) (158,8) through the enemies; K1 (188,8) (191,8).
  - Down B's ramp: (31,8) (32,9) (33,10).
  - Marking a jump out, an arc from the cart's last tiles to where he lands: C (59,9) (61,8) (64,11); D (81,9) (83,8) (86,11); F (164,8) (166,6) (168,7) and (171,9) on the ledge; K1 (197,7) (199,6) (202,9); K2 (217,7) (219,6) (223,9); K3 (227,8) (229,6) (231,7).
  - Over a beam, at the hop's top: (97,8), (209,6). Under the rib, on the walking row: (104,12) (106,12).
  - On foot: the seat (116,9); the pen's jump line (121,10) (123,10) (125,10); one in each hop (132,9) (138,9).
  - The secret: (236,13). The main route collects 49.
- **A perfect run** is about 28 s (a target counted by hand: about 120 tiles ridden and 48 flown at 150 px/s, 18 s; about 72 on foot, 10 s); without any cart about 34 s (Open Question 44).
- **To play through when built:** a bot that rides every cart; a bot that never boards one and still reaches the exit; a bot that stays in F (loses one life) and one that stays in K3 (takes the veladora and still exits).
- **Where a first-time player loses a life:** rail D's broken end (he has just made the same jump at rail C, tile for tile, where staying in cost nothing; the post gives 0.64 s, the hilos draw the jump, the gap is 3 tiles where the jump carries 5.4, and the ofrenda is 3 tiles before the cart); the spike hollow at rail F's end (the third lit post, on screen 0.9 s before the ramp; the hilos arc up to a ledge in plain sight; holding the jump works for 0.86 s of rail; it costs one life or the piñata's taco); K2's 4-tile jump to K3 straight after the beam hop (both pieces were done alone on safe ground, and a miss of the beam is harmless).
- **Look:** the mine's backdrop and floor, its rails, carts and beams (GDD-ART-06, Proposed, not drawn).

### GDD-WLD-18 Level 3-3

Status: Proposed (Decided: the name "Las Cumbres", user, 2026-11-10: "Approved"; that the level is taller than one screen and the camera follows, user, 2026-11-10: "Camera follows up and down"; that its crumbled planks come back, user, 2026-11-10: "Come back after 2 s"; that a rider at a broken end falls on his own, user, 2026-11-10: "He falls on his own"; that the arena it leads to is the engine shed, user, 2026-11-10: "Engine shed"). The layout below is Proposed: the World 3 design swarm's, written before any code. Not built. "Measured" in this section means measured by the swarm's scratch tests, which built this layout as level data outside the repository and ran it on the real `stepRun` and `step`: the gaps and their limits, every hilo in an empty tile, the enemies' clearances, the three glides on foot, the plank stairs, the spring and its lift, and the secret with and without the glide. "Measured, rule not built" is the real jump flown in the frame of the cart's 150 px/s (GDD-WLD-15's carry rule) against this layout's banks and walls. Everything about the carts' rides, the Cacomixtles, the bats, the Armadillo, the Zopilote, the camera, the planks' return and the run times is a target. The level's own test must pin each number when it is built (L-003, L-014).

- **Place in the game:** World 3's third level, "Las Cumbres" (the peaks; the name Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Approved"), the last before La Catorce: from the mine's lift cage up its timber trestles and peaks to the gable of the engine shed, La Casa de Máquinas (GDD-BOS-04). Lives carry on from 3-2 and into 3-4 (GDD-LIFE-05). The glide is on, the wall cling off (GDD-PRG-01). `src/levels/level3-3.ts` (to be).
- **What it brings:** nothing new but the Cacomixtle (GDD-ENM-04). It is World 3's test, and it goes up where 3-1 went down: four carts of his own (GDD-WLD-15), the glide, crumbling planks, a spring, three lifts and a timber perch (GDD-WLD-05), with a returning Armadillo, three Murciélagos and a Zopilote (GDD-ENM-02). No spikes, blocks, doors, keys, fragile walls, beams or roofs. A rail's line "in row r" lies on the ground line y = 16 r, and its cart's floor is 8 px over it.
- **Size:** 240 x 37 tiles (8 screens wide, 3840 x 592 px, a little over two screens tall). Poncho starts at column 2 on ground in row 34; the exit stands at column 234 on ground in row 15: the level ends 19 tiles (304 px, more than a screen) above its start. The climbs win 34 tiles and the descents give back 15: up 8 (a cart), down 2, up 3 (a cart), down 4 (a glide), up 7 (a spring and a lift), down 3 (a glide), level (the bridges), up 8 (a lift), up 8 (planks), down 3 (a cart), down 3 (a glide). The lowest ground is row 35 and the highest row 9; 9 empty rows over the highest rail give the 7 tiles of headroom a jump from a cart needs, and 2 rows of rock lie under the lowest ground.
- **Pits and long falls:** four open pits and three plank bridges over pits; each pit takes a raised bottom with the build (GDD-WLD-03, Open Question 38; as laid out a pit is a column empty to the level's last row, and a fall from the last shelf takes about 1.5 s to leave the level, a target). Every other drop ends on ground with a permanent way back up (below).
- **Teaching order (kishōtenketsu, GDD-WLD-03).**
  - *Ki, columns 0 to 66: the trestle, then the Cacomixtle alone.*
    - *Ground:* the landing G0 (columns 0 to 9, row 34); a pit (10 to 13); the valley shelf (14 to 31, row 35); the first peak P1 (34 to 52, row 26).
    - *Cart C1* at columns 5 and 6. Rail R1: level in row 34, columns 5 to 10; up, 11 to 14; level in row 30, 15 and 16; up, 17 to 20; level in row 26, 21 to 38; a buffer at 39. From column 10 to 33 it is a trestle with up to 9 tiles of air under it. The ride is 4.0 s (target). Out of the cart he lands on the shelf, and the lift MP0 (2 tiles wide, columns 32 and 33, its top from y = 560 to y = 416 and back, 3.6 s each way) is the way up.
    - *K1, met alone, uphill and slow.* Rail R2 (K1 rolls left): a buffer at 41; level in row 26, 42 to 52; a slope, 53 and 54 (slope tiles); level in row 28, 55 to 58, on the terrace T1 (55 to 71, row 28); a buffer at 59. K1 waits at 57 and 58. Its sight stretch is 17 tiles (272 px), level and then uphill for it. It wakes when his feet are on P1 past x = 728 (column 45.5), his feet 24 px over its floor, 40 px inside its 64 px of sight; a jump across that line only delays it to his landing, at most 66 px nearer. It rolls 3 tiles, climbs 2 columns at 84.9 px/s across, and reaches column 46 on P1 1.8 s after its first flash (target).
    - *The perch:* a timber slab 2 tiles wide at columns 45 and 46, its top 40 px over P1 (a moving platform that does not move: a one-way floor, so nothing is blocked). It stands on the wake line with two hilos on it. A hop puts him above the rail; the cart passes 18 px under his feet and parks at its buffer behind him. "Off the line or above it" (GDD-ENM-04) is learned where waiting is free; a Poncho who stays on the rail jumps the cart or stomps the ringtail.
    - *Rest:* T1, the first piñata (column 64, row 24, one empty column past a wall 2 tiles high at 62, L-047), the taco, and the first ofrenda (65).
  - *Shō, columns 65 to 130: the glide from the cart, the way back up, a threat on the peak.*
    - *Cart C2* at 68 and 69. Rail R3: level in row 28, 67 to 71; up, 72 to 74 (slope tiles); level in row 25, 75 to 89 (15 tiles); the warning post at 84; a broken end at x = 1440. The peak P2 is columns 75 to 88; the rail hangs 1 tile past its lip. The ride is 2.5 s (target).
    - *The chasm:* columns 89 to 98, 10 tiles, to the ledge L1 (99 to 107, row 29) 4 tiles down: at the gap limit (10), and 9 tiles from the rail's end, at the broken end's limit (5 plus the drop). L1's far wall is the face of P3 at column 108.
    - *The held jump (measured, rule not built):* from the post it lands at column 104.3; from 3 tiles before the end at 107.3; from the end it meets P3's face 19 px over L1 and drops onto it. A jump not held falls short from all three (the best, from the end, by 23 px; taken in the last step of coyote time, by about 8 px, a target). A rider who stays in falls on his own beside the cart, into the chasm. On foot from P2's lip a held jump lands at column 104.1, one taken 2 tiles early at 102.3, and a plain one is lost (measured).
    - *Back up:* a spring at column 106 under the lift MP1 (3 tiles wide, columns 105 to 107, its top from y = 416 to y = 352 and back, 1.6 s each way, flush with P3). The plain launch puts him on the lift at any arrival time: on P3 in 94 to 278 steps (1.6 to 4.6 s, measured over 24 arrival times, no button pressed); the timed launch lands on the lift wherever it is (target).
    - *The third peak P3* (108 to 119, row 22): an Armadillo (from column 114), 3-2's lesson on a narrow peak with a lip either side: jump it, or bounce on its ball. Over it, a row of three Murciélagos climbing to the right (columns 111, 116 and 121, 48, 80 and 112 px over P3, the last over the pit's second column): a reward route to four hilos and a higher, longer glide. Nothing needs it, and the plain glide passes 17 px under the last bat (a measured path against the proposed bat).
    - *A glide on foot:* a pit, 120 to 126 (7 tiles, 3 down, limit 9), to P4 (127 to 140, row 25). A held jump from the lip, or from 2 tiles early, lands at column 131.6; a plain jump and a walk off the lip are lost (measured).
  - *Ten, columns 127 to 180: the trestle falls.*
    - *P4:* the second ofrenda (130), the second piñata (column 134, row 21, past a wall at 132), the chile, and cart C3 at 137 and 138.
    - *Bridge A:* twelve planks in row 25, columns 141 to 152, nothing under them and no pier. Rail R4: level in row 25, 136 to 155; a buffer at 156 on the stone Q (153 to 158). A cart does not set a plank off, so the ride (1.8 s) crosses what a walker would bring down.
    - *Bridge B, the only way on:* planks at 159 to 166 and 168 to 175, one stone pier at 167, then P5 (176 to 198, row 25). The two spans are 8 tiles at the same height, more than the 6 a glide is held to: here the planks are needed (they are banks in the gap rule, and they come back). Rail R5 (K2 rolls left): a buffer at 157, level in row 25 from 158 to 178, a buffer at 179; K2 waits at 177 and 178 on P5. Its sight stretch is 21 tiles (336 px), level, at his own height, so no jump hides him or wakes it early. It wakes when he passes x = 2648 (column 165.5), one and a half planks before the pier, and reaches the pier 1.6 s after its first flash (target). A plank falls 0.5 s after he lands on it: the bridge can be run, not stood on. The pier is the one place to stand. From it he jumps the cart (13 steps of window, GDD-ENM-04), stomps or claws the ringtail, or burns it from afar with the chile; its cart then rolls on to Q and stays there.
    - *Second tries:* a plank is whole again 2.0 s after it falls, unless his body is in its tile (GDD-WLD-05). A Poncho who turns back finds the bridge again; one who stops on a plank falls into the pit.
  - *Ketsu, columns 176 to 239: everything, then the climb, then the last ride.*
    - *P5, the cart under the vulture.* Rail R6 (K3 rolls left): a buffer at 181, level in row 25 from 182 to 197, a buffer at 198; K3 waits at 196 and 197. Its sight stretch is 16 tiles (256 px), level, at his height. It wakes at x = 2952 (column 184.5). A Zopilote flies its line over columns 187.5 to 193.5 (from 190, its lowest point 48 px over P5), over solid ground 11 tiles from the nearest pit: under it no jump is safe (the pale points, GDD-CMB-01). So the cart is met before the vulture's stretch (it reaches column 187 1.6 s after its first flash: wait and jump it there), or the vulture is clawed first, or the ringtail is burnt. A hilo on the ground under the vulture says "walk here".
    - *The lift MP2* (3 tiles wide, columns 199 to 201, its top from y = 400 to y = 272 and back, 8 tiles, 3.2 s each way) up the face of the shelf A (202 to 210, row 17). It is behind K3's start, so nothing on it wakes K3. The third ofrenda (203) is on A.
    - *The plank stairs, the only way up:* three pairs of planks, columns 204 and 205 in row 15, 206 and 207 in row 13, 208 and 209 in row 11, each 2 tiles above the last, then the last peak P6 (211 to 215, row 9) one column on and 2 tiles up. Each landing starts 0.5 s. Holding right and jumping on each landing climbs them in 111 steps (1.9 s, measured). A fall lands on A, and the planks are back in 2.0 s: the stairs cannot cost a life.
    - *Cart C4* at 212 and 213. Rail R7: level in row 9, 211 to 215; down, 216 to 218 (slope tiles); level in row 12, 219 to 226 (8 tiles, so the cart is back at 150 px/s); the post at 221; a broken end at x = 3632. The shelf under it is 219 to 225; the rail hangs 1 tile past its lip. The ride is 1.5 s (target).
    - *The exit glide:* a pit, 226 to 231 (6 tiles, 3 down, limit 9; 5 from the rail's end, limit 8), to the exit bank (232 to 234, row 15) against the shed's stone gable (235 to 239, solid from row 4 down). The exit is at column 234, against the gable, at the screen position the other levels use. A held jump from the post meets the gable 33 px over the bank, inside the exit's 48 px; from the end 78 px over it, and he slides down the gable into the exit (measured, rule not built). A jump not held from the post is lost; from the very end it lands on the bank (measured, rule not built). On foot from the shelf's lip a held jump, also 2 tiles early, flies into the exit, and a plain one is lost (measured).
- **Rhythm:** a ride, the perch, a piñata, an ofrenda; a ride into the first lethal jump, the spring and the lift, the Armadillo and the bats, an easy glide, an ofrenda; a safe ride over planks, bridge B (the peak of the level), the cart under the vulture, the long lift (a rest), an ofrenda; the stairs, the last ride. Each ofrenda is 3 to 9 tiles before a cart of his.
- **Carts:** his own are C1 (column 5), C2 (68), C3 (137) and C4 (212). A lost life puts carts, planks and enemies back. An empty cart of his is back at its start 2.0 s after it reaches its rail's end; a Cacomixtle's stays where it stops (GDD-WLD-15). Both broken ends have the post 6 tiles before, 15 and 8 level tiles before, 22 and 15 tiles from the cart's start, and 7 or more empty tiles over the rail. No rail is shared. Slope tiles lie under R2's, R3's and R7's slopes; R1's are on a trestle. No cart is needed: C1 has the shelf and MP0, C2 the slope and P2's lip, C3 the planks, C4 the slope and the shelf's lip (the two glides on foot measured).
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the veladora is in a hollow of the trestle's pier V under the rail he rode in on: a wall at column 22 from row 26 down, a floor at 23 and 24 (row 31), and a cap in row 26 over 22 to 25 that carries the rail and hangs 1 tile past the floor. The hollow (rows 27 to 30) opens to the right; the veladora is at column 23, row 30, in plain view (Open Question 15). With the glide (measured): from the lift MP0, a held jump to the left while its top is between y = 432 and y = 500 comes in under the cap (35 of 72 start times of its round); from P1, a held jump back taken 1 or 2 tiles before the lip meets the cap's face and drifts in; one taken at the lip lands on the cap's top. Without the glide, nothing reaches it (measured): not from P1, not from the lift at any height, not from the cap's top, and from the shelf the best jump is 7.6 px short of the floor. A rider who hops out lands on the cap. Missing costs nothing: the shelf and MP0. Two hilos hang on the lift's line under the rail. It asks for the one move the level never needs, a glide the wrong way.
- **Ofrendas** at columns 65, 130 and 203 (27.1%, 54.2% and 84.6% of the width).
- **Gaps (measured against GDD-WLD-03's World 3 rule):** 4 (1 down, limit 7), 10 (4 down, limit 10), 7 (3 down, limit 9) and 6 (3 down, limit 9); three pits bridged by planks, 12, 8 and 8 tiles. No pit has a higher far side. The walls over 3 tiles each have their way up: P1's face (MP0), L1's wall (the spring and MP1), A's face (MP2), P6's face (the stairs).
- **Enemies: eight.** Three Cacomixtles (K1 at x = 928, K2 at 2848, K3 at 3152), an Armadillo (from column 114), three Murciélagos (111, 116, 121) and a Zopilote (190). The nearest to a respawn is K1, 120 px from the first ofrenda (measured). Each Cacomixtle's rail is level or uphill for it for 256 px or more, with every ground in the stretch at its own height or (K1) 40 px inside its sight, so no ledge beside a stretch wakes one early (GDD-ENM-04). No bat is on a cart glide's line. A Cacomixtle parks on stone or rock only (P1, Q, P5), where he hops it or stomps it.
- **Piñatas (GDD-PWR-05, L-047):** two, at columns 64 and 134, the taco before the first lethal jump and the chile before bridge B.
- **Hilos:** 50, each in an empty tile (measured), none above row 7; those on glides are placed on the measured paths, and the bats' four are targets. As (column, row): into C1 (3,33) (4,32); along R1 at the rider's chest (8,32) (13,30) (15,28) (19,26) (23,24) (27,24) (31,24) (35,24); the secret (30,27) (27,28); the perch (45,22) (46,22); the slope and the piñata's wall (53,25) (62,25); R3 and its glide (70,26) (73,24) (77,23) (81,23) (85,23) (88,23), then (93,20) (96,21) (99,23) (102,24), the line of a held jump from the rail's end; the spring (106,26) (106,24); the bats (113,14) (118,12) (123,10) (124,10); the glide on foot (122,17) (126,19); P4 and bridge A (132,22) (144,23) (148,23); the pier (167,23); under the vulture (190,23); MP2 (200,21) (200,18); the stairs (205,13) (207,11) (209,9); R7 and the exit (217,8) (221,10) (224,10), then (229,7) (232,8) and (233,13).
- **Long falls that are not pits:** out of C1, or off P1's left lip (up to 9 tiles), he lands on the shelf, and MP0 is the way on; in the hollow or on V's cap he steps off onto the shelf; off P2's left side he is on T1, with the slope under R3 or C2, back in 2.0 s; on L1, the spring and MP1; off P3's left lip (7 tiles), L1 or MP1; off A's left lip or off MP2 (8 tiles), P5 or the lift's foot, and MP2; off the stairs or off P6's left lip (8 tiles), A and the stairs, whole again in 2.0 s. Past L1 and past the exit a wall stands behind each, and he drops down its face.
- **Played through (a target, counted by hand on the measured pieces):** an expert's line is about 35 s; a careful run (on the perch for K1, the plain spring, on the pier for K2, waiting for K3, an average wait for MP2) about 43 s. No bot has run the whole level: carts are not built (Open Question 44).
- **Where a first-time player loses a life:** R3's broken end, staying in or not holding (the post is 0.64 s before the end, the hilos draw the line, any held jump from the post on lands and the wall stops the longest; the retry is 3 tiles to the cart); bridge B, stopping on a plank or meeting K2 on the run (he has just ridden over the same planks, K2's whole rail is in view at his height before its bell, the pier is stone with a hilo, and the taco or the chile takes a hit); the cart under the vulture, jumping K3 under it (both are in view before K3 wakes, 8 tiles of bare ground lie before the vulture's stretch, and it is over solid ground with the taco's hit to spare).
- **Camera (GDD-UI-04):** the layout was drawn for the up-and-down camera. The three glides drop 4, 3 and 3 tiles and their banks begin 10, 7 and 6 tiles from the lip, so each landing is on screen at takeoff. At the start the camera rests on the level's bottom, and on P6 on its top. What the layout asks of the camera beyond GDD-UI-04's rule (a view upward on a climb, a lead sideways in a rolling cart, the phone's buttons against a Cacomixtle's cart) is in Open Question 39.
- **Look (GDD-ART-06, Proposed, not drawn):** trestles by rule under R1 (columns 10 to 33), under R3's and R7's last tile and under the plank bridges; the planks are the plank ledge; the last five columns are the shed's stone gable.

## 9. Enemies and bosses

### GDD-ENM-01 Enemy design rules

Status: Proposed

- One idea per enemy, readable at a glance, with a 0.5 s telegraph before any attack. (Built for the Zopilote's dive, the Tlacuache's charge and Don Zopilote's dive and sweep, GDD-BOS-02, and for the Paloma's startle, the Chapulín's hop (the last 0.5 s of its crouch) and La Estrella's drop, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03, while the Trompo, which has no attack, needs none. His cactus fruit has none of its own: it falls in view for 43 to 56 steps, 0.7 to 0.9 s, before it reaches a Poncho standing on the floor below it, Open Question 20.)
- Every enemy can be beaten at least one way: stomp, claw or avoidance.
- Enemies never spawn on screen within 3 tiles of Poncho's respawn. (Checked for every enemy in every level, the sandbox included, by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04.) Don Zopilote starts at least 3 tiles from the spawn of his arena, its only respawn, and starts there again whenever Poncho respawns (checked by the level validator's boss-level rule, GDD-TEC-04; GDD-BOS-01), and so does La Estrella in hers, at the start of each of her three phases (GDD-BOS-03).
- Enemy contact is resolved by GDD-CMB-03 and never costs more than 1 life. (Built for the Alacrán: it is a hit like a spike, one life in Base.) Avoidance is a built way to beat an enemy: the cactus buddy cannot be defeated at all (GDD-ENM-02), except by the touch of a Chile Fantasma Poncho (GDD-PWR-04).

### GDD-ENM-02 World 1 bestiary

Status: Proposed (the enemies' look is Decided, user, 2026-11-04: "Approved"; see The look below)

| Enemy | Behaviour | Defeated by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alacrán (scorpion) | Patrols a platform, turns at edges | Stomp or claw (also a fireball, GDD-PWR-03) |
| Zopilote (vulture) | Flies a sine path, swoops after a 0.5 s telegraph | Claw (also a fireball, GDD-PWR-03; not stompable) |
| Cactus buddy (el Cuate) | Static, spiky, harmless from below | Avoid only (not stompable; the claw passes through it and a fireball is used up by it); only the Chile Fantasma's touch defeats it (GDD-PWR-04) |
| Tlacuache (opossum) | Fast charger, plays dead after hit | Claw (even while it plays dead), stomp after wake (also a fireball while awake, GDD-PWR-03) |

- **Alacrán (built, details Proposed):** a 14 x 10 px scorpion standing on the ground that walks at 30 px/s (`ENEMY` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). It starts at the place the level gives it, heading right. It turns round when its box meets a wall, flush against it, and when the ground under its leading edge ends, so it never walks off a ledge and never falls. It walks up and down sand slopes with the middle of its feet on them, a slope's high side turns it like a wall, and the ground under its leading edge is a level tile or a slope in the row of its feet or the row below (GDD-MOV-09). A stomp (GDD-CMB-01), a claw swipe whose box overlaps it (GDD-CMB-02) or a fireball that overlaps it (GDD-PWR-03) defeats it; it is then gone. Every fireball that overlaps a live enemy in a step is used up, whatever defeats the enemy that step. Touching it any other way is a hit (GDD-CMB-03). It uses Poncho's hurtbox against its own box, like spikes: boxes that only share an edge do not touch. A defeated one stays defeated until a life is lost, when every enemy is alive again and back at its start, heading right (GDD-LIFE-02). It is drawn as the Durango scorpion (the look below). The sandbox has one, on the platform at row 10, columns 4 to 7, which Poncho reaches from the top of the 2-tile wall; the piñata at column 8 is the platform's right end, at the same height, so the Alacrán walks across its top too and turns at its far edge (x = 71 to 137); the platform is 64 px above the first ofrenda, clear of its respawn (GDD-ENM-01). Level 0-1 has three, level 1-1 six and level 1-3 one, penned behind a fragile wall, its walk clear of the moving block just past its pen (GDD-WLD-06, GDD-WLD-07, GDD-WLD-09), and the slopes level one, which walks over its hill, its dune and its first bump (GDD-MOV-09). In World 2, 2-1 has one, penned by two crates, and 2-3 one, on the roof before the Trompo's pen (GDD-WLD-12, GDD-WLD-14). Not built yet: sounds. `src/sim/enemy.ts`, its walk in `src/sim/walker.ts`.
- **Zopilote (built, details Proposed):** a 16 x 10 px vulture that flies (`ZOPILOTE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The level gives the middle of its start and its cruise line, and it ignores tiles. Cruising, it flies at 40 px/s up to 48 px either side of its start, turning round there (it starts heading right), while bobbing up and down 12 px on a sine with a period of 2.5 s (150 steps). When Poncho's hurtbox is within 96 px to either side of it and between 0 and 80 px below it, it spots him: it freezes and flashes for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps, the telegraph of GDD-ENM-01; the body flashes red and the wings are raised). It then dives in a straight line at 150 px/s to the spot Poncho's hurtbox centre was at when the telegraph ended (it aims once and does not follow him on), and from there climbs straight back up to its cruise line at 60 px/s, then cruises again from where it is, and may spot him again at once. It cannot be stomped (GDD-CMB-01): landing on it is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), and so is touching it any other way. A claw swipe (GDD-CMB-02) or a fireball (GDD-PWR-03, which is used up) defeats it. It is defeated for good until a life is lost, when it is alive again on its cruise line at its start, heading right (GDD-LIFE-02). It is drawn as a black vulture of Don Zopilote's court (the look below). The sandbox has one, cruising at y = 64 from x = 216 (168 to 264), above the middle of the level: from the floor Poncho's hurtbox centre is 144 px below its cruise line and it never spots him; on the Alacrán's platform he is 80 px below it, the edge of its reach, so it spots him there whenever it is at or below its cruise line: half of every bob, and on its first step (user, 2026-10-25: kept as the taller body makes it, a harder platform) (a Poncho who lingers there is dived on); and the spring ledge and the moving platform, 32 px and 48 px below it, are well within reach. The Tlacuache's ledge, from x = 400, is out of its sideways reach, so it never spots Poncho there. It is far from every respawn (GDD-ENM-01). Level 1-1 has two, each cruising at y = 136, 72 px above the hurtbox centre of a Poncho on the floor, so it spots him there, and neither reaches a respawn point (GDD-WLD-07). Level 1-3 has one, cruising at y = 112 over the shuttle's spikes, 64 px above the hurtbox centre of a Poncho waiting on the step before the shuttle, so it spots him there, and it never spots him at the start or at an ofrenda (GDD-WLD-09). Not built yet: sounds, a swoop that leaves the screen. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **Cactus buddy (built, details Proposed):** a static 12 x 16 px cactus standing on the ground at the place the level gives it (`CACTUS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). It never moves, and it is "avoid only" (user decision, 2026-10-22; the one exception is the Chile Fantasma's touch, user, 2026-11-10, GDD-PWR-04): a stomp does not defeat it (it cannot be stomped, GDD-CMB-01, and landing on it is a hit), a claw swipe passes through it, and a fireball that overlaps it is used up without harming it, so it can cover Poncho from fireballs. Touching it is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), except that it is harmless from below: a contact is no hit while Poncho is moving up and his hurtbox top was at or below its underside on the step before, once his head is above that line he is inside it and the usual hit applies. That can only happen for a cactus with no solid tile directly beneath it: a cactus standing on the ground or a ledge has the top of that tile as its underside, which Poncho's head cannot get below while he overlaps it (tile collision, GDD-MOV-06). So no cactus buddy that stands on something can be passed from below, and no level has one with nothing beneath it yet; the rule is only covered by unit tests on a cactus in mid-air. It uses Poncho's hurtbox against its own box, like the other enemies. It is drawn as el Cuate (the look below). It is alive again, simply where it was, after a lost life (GDD-LIFE-02). The sandbox has one on the right half of the top of the 2-tile wall (columns 10 and 11, top y = 192), at x = 184, leaving the left 18 px of the wall top free to stand on and to jump from to the Alacrán's platform; it is more than 3 tiles from every respawn (GDD-ENM-01; checked by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04). No spot in the sandbox shows the from-below case (see above), so that is covered by unit tests. Level 1-1 has three, and levels 1-2 and 1-3 one each, each on the floor (GDD-WLD-07 to GDD-WLD-09). Not built yet: sounds. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **Tlacuache (built, details Proposed):** a 16 x 10 px opossum standing on the ground (`TLACUACHE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). It starts at the place the level gives it, heading right, and walks at 24 px/s, turning at walls (flush against them) and at ledge ends, and walking and charging up and down sand slopes, like the Alacrán (GDD-MOV-09). It spots Poncho when his feet are within 16 px of its own, up or down (its feet where they are now, on a slope too), and his hurtbox centre is in front of it (the way it faces) by more than 0 and up to 80 px (user decision, 2026-10-22: sight, then dash). It then freezes and flashes red for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps, the telegraph of GDD-ENM-01) and charges at 150 px/s the way it faces, without turning to follow him, until a wall or the end of its ground stops it (as in its walk, it never runs off a ledge); there it turns round and walks again, and may spot him again at once. Touching it is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), while it walks, telegraphs or charges. Its first stomp (GDD-CMB-01) does not defeat it: Poncho bounces as from any stomp and it plays dead for 2.0 s (120 steps), lying still on its back (user decision, 2026-10-22: stomp twice). Playing dead it is out of play except for the claw: touching it is no hit, landing on it is no stomp and a fireball passes over it without being used up, but a claw swipe that overlaps it defeats it (user decision, 2026-10-22). Then it wakes facing the way it faced, walking, and touching it is a hit again (so a Poncho standing in it when it wakes is hit; its feet twitch in its last 0.5 s as a warning). After it has played dead once, a stomp defeats it. The claw (GDD-CMB-02) defeats it at any time, and a fireball (GDD-PWR-03) at any time it is awake, whether or not it has been stomped; either wins over a first stomp in the same step. It is drawn as the opossum who stole fire (the look below). A lost life brings it back at its start, walking, not yet stomped (GDD-LIFE-02). The sandbox has one in the middle of a ledge 5 tiles long in the top right corner (row 6, columns 25 to 29, x 400 to 480, top y = 96), which it walks end to end; Poncho reaches the ledge with a jump from the moving platform at its right end, 32 px lower. The ledge is out of the Zopilote's sideways reach and 128 px above the third ofrenda, far from every respawn (GDD-ENM-01; checked by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04). The slopes level has one, which walks its second bump (GDD-MOV-09). Level 1-2 has three, each in a pen: one on the floor, which does not spot Poncho on the pen's walls, one on a 45° hill and one on a gentle dune (GDD-WLD-08). Level 1-3 has three: on a sand dune penned by a block and a door, behind a fragile wall, and on the last cliff's top, where it walks past the exit; the one behind the fragile wall, let out and left alive, walks through moving blocks (GDD-WLD-09). In World 2, 2-2 has one, penned by a block against a pit's edge, and 2-3 one, on a high roof between two awnings (GDD-WLD-13, GDD-WLD-14). Not built yet: sounds. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **The look (built; the designs Decided, user, 2026-11-04: "Approved", from a design page that played every animation at game speed, and the cactus buddy's name, "El Cuate"; the frame rules Proposed):** the four are real animals and plants of the Chihuahuan desert, each with a true Mexican story, none of them dressed (GDD-VIS-04), drawn in code like Poncho and Don Zopilote (GDD-ART-01) by `scripts/art/enemies1/` (one module each and a shared kit; `scripts/art/make-enemies.mjs` writes `assets/sprites/enemies/`), at the scale of Poncho's drawing (2 x 2 art px a drawing pixel), outlined (GDD-ART-05) and only in World 1's 24 colours (GDD-ART-06; checked by `scripts/art/enemies1.test.mjs`), facing right and mirrored when they face left. Each body fills its box, only a tail, a claw, a flower, a wing or a spine tip standing outside it (fair hits, GDD-VIS-03), as far as `ENEMY_ART.reach` says (pinned by `scripts/art/enemies1.test.mjs`, L-059). A walker never reaches below its feet. A tail, a claw or a wing beside the box may lie over a wall's face, as the Tlacuache's tail does when it turns flush against a pen wall in 1-2 and 1-3 (user, 2026-11-05: "Allow it, pin it"), or over a slope's sand, as the Tlacuache's and the Alacrán's tails do walking down a sand hill, trailing uphill behind them (up to 9.5 px on 1-2's 45° hill: only the middle of a body's feet meets a slope, GDD-MOV-09, so Poncho's drawing dips into it the same way) and a diving Zopilote's wing tip at a cliff in 1-3 (user, 2026-11-05: "Allow beside the body"). Nothing of any frame reaches into a tile or a slope's sand over or below its box in the real levels: checked at every step over every level with a World 1 enemy by `scripts/art/enemies1.levels.test.mjs`, with Poncho at his spawn and dropped beside each enemy so that it shows every look it has in play. The design and its notes: `assets/characters/world1-enemies/`.
  - **Alacrán: the Durango scorpion** (*Centruroides suffusus*), the emblem of Durango, whose people call themselves alacranes: a busy little patroller on duty. Red-brown with the species' two dark stripes down its back, honey pincers, a tail of five beads arched over its back in a C, a cream stinger (the hazard mark), and big stern eyes. Frame 24 x 16 px, feet on its bottom row: a four-frame walk, one frame for every 2 px it walks (its x), the legs in alternating pairs, the body bobbing, the tail a frame behind and the pincers snipping.
  - **Zopilote: a black vulture of Don Zopilote's court** (*Coragyps atratus*, the zopilote over every Mexican road): navy-black plumage, a bare grey-lilac head with a pale hooked beak and a sly eye, the black vulture's pale patches at the base of its five finger feathers, and one cholla joint stuck on its back, green with three pale spines, the not-stompable mark it shares with its king (GDD-CMB-01). Frame 32 x 24 px centred on its box: cruising, four flap frames of 6 steps each; in its telegraph, wings flared high, beak open in a hiss and talons out, flashing red on alternate 4-step blocks as before; diving, wings swept back, a steep frame when the dive is at least 1.3 times as tall as it is long (aim point against where it is), else a shallow one, facing the way it dives; climbing back, tilted up, two frames of 4 steps.
  - **Cactus buddy: el Cuate** ("cuate" is Mexican for buddy, from Nahuatl *cōātl*, twin): a chubby ribbed column with two raised arms (the shape no scenery plant has, GDD-ART-06), pale spine clusters all round and three on top (the hazard and not-stompable marks), a red claret-cup flower on his head (2 px above his box, his spines 2.5 px when he is alarmed) and a sleepy face. Frame 16 x 24 px. He dozes, breathing in and out every 45 steps; he peeks with one eye at Poncho when Poncho's hurtbox centre is within 64 px of his middle sideways and 48 px up or down; and within 28 px sideways and 32 px up or down he throws his arms up, eyes wide and spines bristling, shivering every 4 steps. Peeking or alarmed he faces Poncho. Drawing only: he never moves and nothing of his rules changes.
  - **Tlacuache: the opossum who stole fire** (in Mazatec and Nahua tellings it carried fire to people in its tail, which is why the tail is bare; a faint scorch near its tip): grizzled grey-lilac fur, a white pointed face with a dark eye mask, round dark ears and a mischievous eye. Frame 28 x 16 px, feet on its bottom row: a four-frame waddle, one frame for every 2 px it walks; its telegraph crouched, rump up, ears flat and jaws open in a toothy hiss, flashing red as before; its charge a stretched gallop, one frame for every 5 px; playing dead on its back, legs up, X eyes and tongue out, its feet curling in the last 0.5 s as before.
