  - **The defeat poof (built, Proposed):** a defeated Alacrán, Zopilote or Tlacuache no longer just vanishes: for 8 steps it shows a squashed frame (stomped; never the Zopilote, which cannot be) or a knocked-over frame rising 6 px (the claw, a fireball), then a Nintendo-style poof centred on its box, a white star burst and a dust cloud that swells and breaks into three puffs, 4 steps a frame, 24 steps (0.4 s) in all. It is drawing only: nothing touches it, and a lost life clears it. World 2's Paloma, Chapulín and Trompo go the same way (GDD-ENM-03). A cactus buddy, which only the Chile Fantasma's touch defeats (GDD-PWR-04), has no knocked-over frame: it goes straight to the poof.
  - `ENEMY_ART` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: the frames, `walkPx` 2, `chargePx` 5, `flapSteps` 6, `climbSteps` 4, `flashSteps` 4, `steepRatio` 1.3, `dozeSteps` 45, `peek` 64 x 48, `alarm` 28 x 32, `shiverSteps` 4, `defeatSteps` 8, `hitRise` 6, `poofSteps` 4, and `reach` (game px behind, in front, above and below the box: the Alacrán 4.5, 1.5, 3, 0, the Zopilote 6, 0.5, 6.5, 3.5, the cactus buddy 1, 1.5, 2.5, 0, the Tlacuache 5.5, 3, 0.5, 0; World 2's in GDD-ENM-03); the frame rules in `src/ui/enemyPose.ts` and `src/ui/defeatFx.ts`.

### GDD-ENM-03 World 2 bestiary

Status: Decided (three new enemies in this round, the Paloma, the Chapulín and the Trompo, and the Zanate later, user, 2026-11-04: "Three now, Zanate later"). Their rules, names and numbers are Proposed. Built (`src/sim/paloma.ts`, `src/sim/chapulin.ts`, `src/sim/trompo.ts`, every rule that depends on the kind a table over every kind in `src/sim/enemy.ts`; tested in `src/sim/paloma.test.ts`, `src/sim/chapulin.test.ts`, `src/sim/trompo.test.ts`, `src/sim/world2run.test.ts`, the test level `lab-2e`, `src/levels/lab2e.test.ts`, and `e2e/enemies2.spec.ts`); their placeholder look approved from screenshots with World 2's (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`); their final art designed and approved (user, 2026-11-08: "Approved, build them into the game"; see Look below). The details the main session settled during the build (2026-11-04: the Paloma startled from either side, only while it walks; its bob drawing only; the Chapulín's look-before-it-hops rule and its start crouched) are Proposed.

World 2's enemies follow GDD-ENM-01: one idea each, a 0.5 s telegraph before any attack, each beaten at least one way, none starting within 3 tiles of a respawn, never more than one life per contact. They are animals of a Mexican town and animated toys from its stalls: no people, and no rats, cockroaches or flies, which in a food market say "dirty" (GDD-VIS-04). Each new one is met alone on safe ground before it is combined with others: the Paloma in 2-1, the Chapulín in 2-2, the Trompo in 2-3 (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14).

| Enemy | Behaviour | Defeated by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Paloma (pigeon) | Walks on the ground and on cordeles; come close and it flutters up, hovers, and comes back down | Stomp, claw or fireball; or walk under it while it hovers |
| Chapulín (grasshopper) | Crouches, then hops in a fixed arc; an awning throws it high | Stomp, claw or fireball |
| Trompo (spinning top) | The first heavy enemy: spins along the ground | Claw or fireball to stun it, then a stomp (not stompable while it spins) |
| Tlacuache, Alacrán | Return from World 1 with their rules (GDD-ENM-02) | As in World 1 |

- **Paloma (pigeon):** a 14 x 12 px pigeon, World 2's basic enemy from 2-1 on, which teaches that an enemy can react to how Poncho comes at it. It walks at 20 px/s on the ground, along a cordel or the strung part of a rollo (GDD-WLD-11), turning at walls and at the ends of its ground, like the Alacrán (GDD-ENM-02); off the end of a tile ledge it walks on onto a cordel that goes on at the height of its feet, not onto one lower down. **Startle:** while it walks, when Poncho's hurtbox centre is within 48 px of its middle across, on either side, and his feet within 24 px of its feet up or down, it stops and telegraphs for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps, frozen on the ground: puffed up like a courting pigeon, chest out, neck ruff flared, wings half raised and tail fanned (the Look below), flashing red 4 steps on and 4 off), then flies straight up 40 px at 120 px/s (2 px a step, 20 steps, 0.33 s), hovers 1.0 s (60 steps; drawn bobbing 2 px, which moves only the drawing), comes back down at 60 px/s to where it was (40 steps, 0.67 s) and walks on; it is not startled while it flies, and once down it can be startled again at once. Hovering, its box's bottom is 40 px above its feet, 9 px over the hurtbox top of a Poncho standing where it stood (8 px over his body box), so he can pass under it for 73 steps (1.22 s), from 0.27 s into its rise to 0.15 s into its descent (measured by `src/sim/paloma.test.ts`). A Poncho whose feet are more than 24 px above it does not startle it, so a jump onto it from higher up is a stomp, and it can be stomped in every phase, walking, telegraphing, rising, hovering or coming down: a stepping stone over a gap (2-1's pit, GDD-WLD-12). Sprinting into one is the mistake it punishes, after a clear warning: 48 px at the 120 px/s run takes 0.4 s, inside its telegraph, so running on into it is a hit (measured). A stomp, a claw swipe or a fireball (used up) defeats it; touching it any other way is a hit (GDD-CMB-03). A lost life brings it back at its start, walking. Not built yet: a coo for its telegraph (no sound of the game's set fits; GDD-AUD-02). `PALOMA` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `width` 14, `height` 12, `speed` 20, `sightX` 48, `sightY` 24, `telegraphSteps` 30, `riseHeight` 40, `riseSpeed` 120, `hoverSteps` 60, `bobPx` 2, `descendSpeed` 60.
- **Chapulín (grasshopper):** a 12 x 10 px green field grasshopper, from 2-2, the first enemy whose danger is an arc to read, and the one that plays with the awnings. It starts crouched. It crouches still for 0.6 s (36 steps: legs folded, body low; in its last 0.5 s, 30 steps, it flashes red 4 steps on and 4 off, its telegraph), then hops the way it faces at 300 px/s up and 60 px/s across, falling as Poncho falls (GDD-MOV-04): its feet peak 30.2 px up, 25 steps (0.42 s) in the air, 25 px along (measured by `src/sim/chapulin.test.ts`), and it crouches again where it lands. Before it hops it plays the hop's arc out: if the hop would meet a wall, or end lower than it stands (a pit, the end of a ledge, a step down) and not on an awning, it turns round instead and crouches again, so it never falls; it climbs a step 1 tile high and turns at a wall 2 tiles high (measured). A crouch with no ground under it falls. **On an awning:** a fall whose feet cross the top of an awning its box is over throws it up at 480 px/s, the awning's tier 1 (GDD-WLD-10), at every landing, without crouching and keeping its 60 px/s across: each bounce peaks 77.1 px up and lasts 40 steps (0.67 s) and 40 px, so on a 4-tile awning it bounces twice, then falls off the end and crouches again on the ground (measured). A level tile at the awning's height wins in the same step. A torn awning tears under its bounce as under Poncho's (`src/sim/world2run.test.ts`). It passes up through an awning from below. Over an awning it is a tall, regular hazard that Poncho times his own rebound against, or stomps in mid-air for a bounce of his own. Its hop passes through cordeles. Jump over it while it crouches, or stomp, claw or fireball it; it is soft, so a stomp defeats it crouched or in the air. Running under its hop does not work: its feet at its peak reach 0.8 px into a standing Poncho's hurtbox (measured), which its first meeting shows on safe ground. Touching it any other way is a hit (GDD-CMB-03). A lost life brings it back at its start. No food framing, and nothing that recalls a famous TV character's costume. `CHAPULIN` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `width` 12, `height` 10, `crouchSteps` 36, `flashSteps` 30, `hopUp` 300, `hopAcross` 60, `awningUp` 480 (`AWNING.tiers[0]`).
- **Trompo (spinning top):** a 14 x 14 px spinning top from the toy stall, 2-3's new enemy and the game's first **heavy** enemy, the one the claw's stun waited for (GDD-CMB-02); it teaches the first two-verb combo, claw, then stomp. It spins along the ground at 70 px/s, turning at walls and ledge ends like the Alacrán, faster than it but slower than Poncho's 120 px/s run, so he can always back off. It has no attack and so no telegraph: its threat is contact. **Not stompable while it spins:** landing on it is a hit, marked by the three pale points on its crown (GDD-CMB-01). **Stun:** a claw swipe that overlaps it, or a fireball (used up), stuns it for 1.0 s (60 steps), with the stomp's thud (GDD-AUD-02): it topples onto its side and lies still, harmless to touch, and rocks (its `stunWobble` frame) on every other 4 steps in its last 0.5 s (30 steps, drawn only; the Look below), as the Tlacuache twitches (GDD-ENM-02). A claw on a stunned top does nothing more and does not renew its stun, and a fireball passes over it without being used up; a **stomp on a stunned top defeats it**, and Poncho bounces as from any stomp. After the second it gets up spinning the way it faced, and a Poncho inside it then is hit. The claw's box reaches 20 px in front of him, and a short hop from there lands on the stunned top well within the second (measured by `src/sim/trompo.test.ts`, and in 2-3's first pen by `src/levels/level2-3.test.ts`). A lost life brings it back at its start, spinning. `TROMPO` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `width` 14, `height` 14, `speed` 70, `stunSteps` 60, `wobbleSteps` 30.
- **Returning from World 1:** the Tlacuache (opossums raiding a market are true to life) and a few Alacranes (in fruit crates; the review enemies of 2-1's opening), with the rules of GDD-ENM-02: an Alacrán in 2-1 and in 2-3, a Tlacuache in 2-2 and in 2-3. Walkers ignore awnings and cordeles, and a level never lets a walk cross an awning (GDD-WLD-10; each World 2 level's tests check it). The Zopilote and the cactus buddy stay in the desert.
- **Defeats (Proposed, Open Question 26):** a defeated bird flies off the screen in a puff of feathers and a defeated toy tumbles away: nothing is shown dying (GDD-VIS-04). **Built for now (Proposed):** World 1's defeat poof (GDD-ENM-02): a stomped Paloma, Chapulín or Trompo shows its squash frame for 8 steps (the Paloma's with two loose feathers, the Trompo's with a crack), a clawed or burnt Paloma or Chapulín its knocked-over frame rising 6 px, then the poof. A Trompo is only ever defeated by a stomp, so it has no knocked-over frame. The flight off the screen and the tumble are not built.
- **Later, the Zanate (great-tailed grackle; Proposed, not built; user, 2026-11-04: "Zanate later"):** the bold black bird of every Mexican plaza and market, a thief whose one idea is that it wants Poncho's food, not Poncho. In Base it ignores him; in Taco or Fuego, after a 0.5 s telegraph, it swoops at him once from its perch (a tile, a post or a cordel), and touching it in the swoop takes his taco or chile as a hit in those states does, never a life (GDD-CMB-03); it flies slowly back to its perch with it, and defeating it on the way (a stomp, the claw or a fireball) drops what it carries for him to take back. Its rules and numbers are put to the user when it is built.
- **Look (built; the designs Decided, user, 2026-11-08: "Approved, build them into the game", from an animated design page, designed without reference images, "No, design them"; the frame rules Proposed):** like World 1's (GDD-ENM-02), a real animal of the town or a real toy of its market, each with a true Mexican story and none of them dressed (GDD-VIS-04), drawn in code by `scripts/art/enemies2/` (one module each and a kit of World 2's colours; `scripts/art/make-enemies.mjs` writes `assets/sprites/enemies/`), at the scale of Poncho's drawing (2 x 2 art px a drawing pixel), outlined (GDD-ART-05), light from the upper left, and only in World 2's 24 colours, the Chapulín's darkened leaf #3E7A3C (user, 2026-11-04: "Darken it", Open Question 34) and the telegraph's red, World 1's (GDD-ART-06, GDD-ENM-01; checked by `scripts/art/enemies2.test.mjs`), facing right and mirrored when they face left. One dominant shape each, told apart from each other, from the returning enemies and from Poncho by silhouette: round and puffed, a zig-zag of legs, a pear on a spike. Each stands on its feet at the frame's bottom middle; only a wing, a tail, a beak, a leg, an antenna or the Trompo's tacks stand outside its box, as far as `ENEMY_ART.reach` says (pinned, L-059), never below its feet; what stands beside the box may lie over a wall's face, as World 1's tails do (GDD-ENM-02; user, 2026-11-08: "Allowed"); nothing of any frame lies in a tile over or below its box at any step of World 2's levels and `lab-2e`, with Poncho at his spawn and dropped beside each enemy so it shows every look it has in play, a Trompo also started stunned (`scripts/art/enemies2.levels.test.mjs`). The design and its notes: `assets/characters/world2-enemies/`.
  - **Paloma: the rock pigeon of the plaza** (*Columba livia*), the bird of Tomás Méndez's huapango "Cucurrucucú paloma", whose refrain is its coo: plump and proud, pigeon grey with dark wing bars, a teal and rosa neck sheen, a gold eye with a smug half-lid and pink feet. Frame 24 x 20 px: a four-frame strut with the pigeon's head-bob, one frame for every 2 px it walks; its telegraph the courting pigeon's puff (chest out, neck ruff flared, wings half raised, tail fanned, beak open in a coo, eye wide), flashing red 4 steps on and 4 off; rising, a wing clap of four flap frames, 4 steps each; hovering the same four, 6 steps each, drawn `PALOMA.bobPx` lower on every other 15 steps; coming down a glide, wings up in a V and feet dangling; stomped a squashed frame with two loose feathers; knocked on its back.
  - **Chapulín: the grasshopper of Chapultepec** (*Sphenarium purpurascens*): Chapultepec is Nahuatl *Chapoltepēc*, "hill of the grasshopper", and its glyph is a green grasshopper with a red mouth. The real species has no wings, so it only hops, its one idea. Leaf green with a yellow-striped belly, a big hind leg folded in a high Λ with rosa shins and pale spines, a rosa mouth and a big round eye. Frame 20 x 16 px: crouched it chews (two frames, 3 steps each) until its telegraph, the last 0.5 s of its crouch, a tighter wind-up flashing red; in the air it rises nose up with its hind legs flung back, falls nose down with its front legs reaching, and while it rises faster than its own 300 px/s hop (only an awning's throw does) it is flung splayed, eye wide (`boing`); squashed or knocked on its back as the others. No food framing.
  - **Trompo: the wooden top of the toy stall**, the one kids wind with a cord and play la troya with: turned wood, lacquer bands of rosa, teal, yellow and cobalt, a yellow button on its crown and an iron nail for a tip, and a stern painted face. Frame 20 x 20 px: spinning, four frames of 3 steps each in which the face goes round (front, the edge, the back with a little star, coming in), the body leaning and dust at the tip; three glaze-white tacks on its crown in every spin frame, the not-stompable mark (GDD-CMB-01), and glaze nowhere else; stunned it lies on its side, crown behind, dizzy spirals for eyes and no tacks, and in the last 0.5 s of its stun it rocks (`stunWobble`) on every other 4 steps; stomped it squashes, cracked, with X eyes.
  - `ENEMY_ART` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: the frames, `riseFlapSteps` 4, `hoverFlapSteps` 6, `bobSteps` 15, `chewSteps` 3, `spinSteps` 3, `rockSteps` 4, and `reach` (the Paloma 3.5, 3, 6, 0, the Chapulín 3.5, 2, 3.5, 0, the Trompo 1, 0.5, 3.5, 0); the frame rules in `src/ui/enemyPose.ts` and `src/ui/defeatFx.ts`.

### GDD-BOS-01 Boss structure

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-02: World 1's boss built "as written", the numbers Proposed for the user to adjust after playing; what a lost life does to the fight "As planned"; user, 2026-11-04: World 2's boss "La Estrella", GDD-BOS-03; user, 2026-11-08: the hit feedback, hit-stop and screen shake, for every boss, "Yes I like the idea", below)

Each world ends with a boss in a single-screen arena: Don Zopilote in World 1 (GDD-BOS-02), La Estrella in World 2 (GDD-BOS-03), and La Catorce in World 3 (GDD-BOS-04, designed, not built). Three phases, each teaching or testing the world's signature element. Bosses do not drop extra lives; the fight must be doable with the lives the player has left. Checkpoint at the arena start.

- **A boss level (built, details Proposed):** the arena is a level of its own, the last of its world, one screen wide (30 x 17 tiles): `1-4` for World 1 (GDD-BOS-02), and `2-4` for World 2 (GDD-BOS-03). A level with a boss is a boss level, and which boss it holds is read in one place (`levelBoss` and `isBossLevel` in `src/sim/bossLevel.ts`), for the validator and the boss bar. It has no ofrendas, no exit, no hilos and no veladora: no extra lives and no secret. Poncho starts, and respawns after every lost life, at the arena start the level gives (GDD-LIFE-02). The boss takes 3 hits; 1.0 s (60 steps) after the hit that defeats him the level clear screen comes up as at an exit (GDD-UI-02): the progress is saved (GDD-PRG-02), and the results menu greys "Next level" while no level comes after it: 1-4's "Next level" starts World 2's 2-1 (GDD-UI-02), and 2-4's is greyed while there is no World 3 (`e2e/world2.spec.ts`, `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`).
- **A life lost mid-fight (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned"; details Proposed):** the boss keeps the hits he has taken and starts the phase he is in again from its start (the next phase, if a hit's flashing had not ended), with all his fruit gone and every rock back on its spot (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`); La Estrella the same, every tejocote gone, and the middle awning she tore away in phase 3 still gone (GDD-BOS-03; checked by `src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts` and `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`). Running out of lives in the arena restarts the world from its first level with seven lives, as anywhere in the world (GDD-LIFE-05; 1-1 in World 1, 2-1 in World 2): the fight starts afresh, with all three of the boss's hits, when Poncho next reaches the arena (checked for World 1 by `e2e/world1.spec.ts`).
- **The validator's boss-level rule (GDD-TEC-04):** a level with a boss has no ofrendas and no exit and is exactly one screen wide, and the boss's starting point is at least 3 tiles from the spawn (GDD-ENM-01): La Estrella's, the middle of her box, at the start of each of her three phases, since a lost life starts her phase again. It keeps the other checks every level gets: no gap wider than the world's limit, no enemy starting within 3 tiles of a respawn, and the sand slope checks (GDD-MOV-09).
- **The boss bar:** the HUD shows the boss's own name ("Don Zopilote", "La Estrella") and the hits the boss has left, a pip for each of the boss's three hits (GDD-UI-01).
- **Each boss's own rules:** a boss's rules may override a general one, and its section says so: Don Zopilote is hit by the claw and fireballs only while he is stuck in the sand (GDD-BOS-02); La Estrella is hit by a bump or the claw, and fireballs do nothing to her (GDD-BOS-03).
- **Hit feedback (user, 2026-11-08: "Yes I like the idea", of the main session's boss design for World 2; for every boss; built; the numbers Proposed):**
  - **Hit-stop:** on the step a hit lands on a boss, the whole simulation holds still for 4 steps (67 ms) after a hit that leaves him hurt and 12 steps (200 ms) after the hit that defeats him: no step runs, so the boss, Poncho, the fruit, the tejocotes, the rocks and the level's clock all wait, and the frame of the hit's step stays on screen. A press made during it is latched (L-006) and used by the first step after it, and a held button is still held then, so a rebound chain off an awning goes on (GDD-WLD-10). The pause still works during it (GDD-UI-02). It is not motion, so the reduced motion setting does not turn it off (GDD-UI-03). Built in the scene's fixed-step loop, not in the simulation: after each step the loop asks how long to hold (`hitStopSteps` in `src/sim/hitStop.ts`, `HIT_STOP` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `hurtSteps` 4, `defeatSteps` 12) and then runs no step for that many ticks, which spend no latched press; so `stepRun`, the bots and every count of steps in the tests are unchanged, and a test stepping the game by hand is never held. Poncho is drawn where the hit's step left him; the boss arenas have no moving platform or block, the other things drawn between steps, so nothing else could slide during it. Tested in `src/sim/hitStop.test.ts` and `e2e/hitstop.spec.ts` (the holds of 4 and 12 steps, the clock waiting, Poncho drawn still, a Space, claw, touch jump and Fuego tap during it used after it, the pause during it, a quit to the title during it ending it). A gamepad tap shorter than the hold is not kept, as one shorter than a frame is not (GDD-CTL-02).
  - **Screen shake:** drawn only (the camera's offset on screen; the simulation never reads it), up and down only, the HUD and the touch controls not shaken, and none at all while the Screen shake row of the settings screen is Off (GDD-UI-02, GDD-UI-03); a new shake that starts while one is running replaces it when it is at least as big as what is left of the running one, else is dropped (so the tear's 4 px shake, still running at the thud 8 steps later, is not cut to 2 px). La Estrella: 1 px for 6 steps when she lands on an awning, 2 px for 10 steps when she thuds on the floor, 4 px for 20 steps, dying away, when she tears the middle awning (GDD-BOS-03), 3 px for 16 steps when her empty pot hits the floor at her defeat. Don Zopilote: 2 px for 10 steps when a dive sticks him in the sand, 3 px for 16 steps when he lands on the floor at his defeat (GDD-BOS-02), or, when the hit that defeats him lands while he is stuck in the sand (the usual third hit, a claw or a fireball), 3 px for 16 steps from the step after that hit, as he slumps (user, 2026-11-09: "Shake as he slumps"; the hit's own step still shakes nothing). A hit on a boss shakes nothing: the hit-stop and the boss's white flash show it, and a shake would blur where he is. **Its rhythm (the main session's, 2026-11-09, Proposed):** the world is drawn its size down for 2 steps, then up for 2, and so on, from the step it starts, in whole px; a shake dying away has the size `px * (1 - age / steps)` at each step, rounded up to a whole px, so the tear's 4 px is 4 for its first 5 steps, then 3, 2 and 1 for 5 steps each (still 3 px at the thud 8 steps after the tear, so the thud's 2 px is dropped) (`SHAKE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The whole picture shakes, the far backdrop (the sky, the sun, the clouds and the far layers, GDD-ART-06) with the level, so no band opens between them (user, 2026-11-09: "Shake everything", after the screenshots of the first build, where the backdrop stayed still and a dark band opened between the plaza houses and the floor); for that moment a strip as tall as the shake at the screen's top or bottom edge shows the camera's background colour. Built in `src/sim/shake.ts` (`shakeStarts`, `nextShake`, `shakeSize`, `shakeOffset`) and the scene's camera, tested in `src/sim/shake.test.ts` and `e2e/shake.spec.ts`; measured in the real 2-4 fight, the thud comes 8 steps after the tear. A shake keeps running through a lost life and the respawn's wait; at most 20 steps, it has ended long before Poncho is back.  It moves on with the steps the loop runs, so it waits through a hit-stop and a pause; set to Off mid-shake, the world is drawn still from that frame. "What is left" of a running shake is its size now: all of it for one that does not die away.
- **World 3's arena, 3-4 (Proposed, not built; GDD-BOS-04):** the first arena with things that move between steps and with level parts of its own: two rails with carts, two springs and crumbling tiles. The hit-stop holds its carts still like everything else (the sentence above, that an arena has nothing else to slide, is true of the two arenas built). The boss-level rule keeps 3-4's springs and crumbling tiles under the checks every level gets and skips three of GDD-WLD-15's rail limits for it (GDD-TEC-04). Its cart's return of 3.0 s in place of the levels' 2.0 s is La Catorce's own rule. Her shakes: 1 px for 6 steps at each slam in phases 2 and 3, 2 px for 10 steps when she breaks a wheel stop, 3 px for 16 steps when her plate touches the floor.
- `src/sim/boss.ts`, `src/sim/estrella.ts`, `src/sim/bossLevel.ts`, `src/sim/hitStop.ts`, `src/levels/level1-4.ts`, `src/levels/level2-4.ts`, `src/levels/validate.ts`, the boss bar in `src/ui/bossBar.ts`.

### GDD-BOS-02 World 1 boss

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-02: "Don Zopilote as written"; "Placeholders for now", shapes "like the other enemies have"; the speeds, timings and arena filled in as Proposed "for you to adjust after playing"; his rules "As planned", marked below; the placeholder look approved from screenshots, user, 2026-11-03: "Approved, merge"; his real art from the design canvas the user approved, user, 2026-11-03: "Ok merge it and implement it!", its details Proposed).

A giant vulture, Don Zopilote, circles the arena and drops cactus fruit. Phase 1: dodge dives. Phase 2: knock rocks onto it with claw swipes. Phase 3: faster dives plus ground hazards. Defeat in 3 hits.

- **The arena, level 1-4 (built, details Proposed):** 30 x 17 tiles, one screen (480 x 272 px), World 1's last level: "Next level" on 1-3's results menu starts it (GDD-UI-02, GDD-WLD-09), and `?level=1-4` opens it. Drawn with World 1's backdrop and ground tiles (GDD-ART-06). The floor is flat (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned"): its top is row 14 (y = 224) across the whole width, with no pit and no sand slope, so World 1's boss does not test the sand slopes, as GDD-BOS-01 asks of each boss. A pillar 4 tiles wide and 3 tall stands at each side, the arena's walls (columns 0 to 3 and 26 to 29, rows 11 to 13, top y = 176): a full jump from the floor beside either one lands on its top and a short hop does not (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). A rock rests on the inner edge of each pillar top, its middle at x = 56 and x = 424. Poncho starts on the floor at column 14 (x = 232). No ofrendas, exit, hilos, veladora or other enemies (GDD-BOS-01). `src/levels/level1-4.ts`.
- **Don Zopilote:** a box of 48 x 30 px, three times the Zopilote's (GDD-ENM-02), that ignores tiles. **His look (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-03: "Ok merge it and implement it!", of the design canvas with its key art, lore, pose sheet, crown, tunas and rocks, and palette; the design saved in `assets/characters/don-zopilote/`):** a chubby turkey vulture, the zopilote of the Chihuahuan desert, who crowned himself king of the desert ("El Rey del Desierto"): brown plumage with a cool blue shadow, silvery lilac flight feathers, a fluffy dark ruff, a bare red head, a pale hooked beak and a pale eye under a grumpy brow. Story (Proposed): he has circled over Poncho since the curse took his lives, sure the cat is done for, and drops tunas from the nopales to hurry things along; he holds none of Poncho's lives (GDD-BOS-01). Funny because he is vain, never a caricature (GDD-VIS-04). Three cholla joints stuck in his back, green with pale spines (a jumping cholla he landed in), mark that he cannot be stomped (GDD-CMB-01, GDD-ART-05): landing on him is a touch like any other (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). His crown, of hammered gold tin with a tuna as its jewel, has a point for each hit he has left: three, then two and one, none once he is defeated, as the boss bar's pips (GDD-UI-01). Drawn in code (GDD-ART-01) by `scripts/art/boss.mjs` into `assets/sprites/boss/don-zopilote.png`, at the scale of Poncho's drawing (2 x 2 art px a drawing pixel), outlined (GDD-ART-05) and only in World 1's colours (GDD-ART-06: 20 of its 24 and the outline, checked by `scripts/art/boss.test.mjs`); a frame is 75 x 62 game px with his box's middle at its middle, drawn facing right and mirrored when he faces left. His frames (`src/ui/bossPose.ts`): his wings flap, raised for 8 steps and lowered for 8, while he laps or drops, and while he climbs, tilted upwards; are raised through his telegraph, where his beak is open, his eye wide and his talons out; are tucked while he dives, head first, while he sweeps the floor, talons out and beak open, with no flap, while he is hurt (crossed eyes) and while he is defeated (tumbling, then on his back with the three dots, no crown: see his defeat below); and are held lowered while he is stuck (his beak in a sand mound on the floor, tail up, the three dots over him). Only his lowered wings and his two tumbling frames reach below his box's bottom: the lowered wings by 14 px (`BOSS_ART.wingsBelow`), the tumbling frames by 7 and 12 px, shown only while they fit (see his defeat below); every other frame stays above it (checked by `scripts/art/boss.test.mjs`). While he flaps, his wings stay raised whenever his box's bottom is less than 14 px above the floor, as at the end of a phase 2 drop or the start of a climb from the floor, so nothing of him ever reaches into the floor (the rule checked by `src/ui/bossPose.test.ts`, every frame of a bot's fight through 1-4 by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`, whose bot hits him with a rock during his only phase 2 drop, and the end of a drop in play by `e2e/boss.spec.ts`). The red and white flashes paint every pixel but his outline (`BOSS_ART` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). He follows a fixed pattern with nothing left to chance; only where Poncho is changes it. `BOSS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/boss.ts`, `src/ui/bossPose.ts`.
- **Circling:** he laps an ellipse 152 px to either side of a middle the level gives and 16 px above and below it (x = 240, y = 72 in 1-4), once in 4.0 s (240 steps), starting and ending at its right or left end (x = 392 or 88 in 1-4): from the right end he goes leftwards along its lower half first, from the left end rightwards along its upper half first. The fight starts with him at the right end. He drops a cactus fruit on the 40th, 100th, 160th and 220th step of each lap, its top at his box's bottom: in 1-4, from the right end they fall at x = 316, 108, 164 and 372, from the left end at 164, 372, 316 and 108 (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts`). He faces the way he moves across; when he does not move across he keeps the way he faced.
- **Telegraph (GDD-ENM-01):** at the end of each lap he freezes for 0.5 s (30 steps) before his attack, facing Poncho, wings raised and body flashing red, 4 steps on and 4 off. In phases 1 and 3 the attack is a dive, in phase 2 a sweep.
- **Dive (phases 1 and 3):** as the telegraph ends he takes the middle of Poncho's hurtbox, across only, and dives in a straight line to the floor below it (his box's bottom on the floor's top), that x kept between the ends of his lap (88 to 392 in 1-4), so he never clips a pillar. He dives at 180 px/s in phase 1 and at 300 px/s in phase 3. Then he is stuck in the sand, dazed (three pale dots over his head): 1.0 s (60 steps) in phase 1, 0.6 s (36 steps) in phase 3. Then he climbs in a straight line at 120 px/s to the nearer end of his lap (the right one when he is in the middle) and laps from there. In 1-4 a phase 1 dive aimed at the start (x = 232) sticks him in the sand 71 steps after it starts (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts`).
- **Sweep (phase 2):** as the telegraph ends he drops straight down at 180 px/s until his box's bottom is on the floor, sweeps along it at 150 px/s to below the other end of his lap, and climbs straight up at 120 px/s to that end, then laps from there; so the sweeps go right to left and left to right in turn. In 1-4 his drop or his sweep reaches Poncho standing anywhere on the floor between the pillars, and neither reaches him on a pillar top away from its inner edge (see "Touching him") (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`): the pillars are where he dodges it.
- **Cactus fruit:** a red prickly pear (a tuna, with pale glochids and a green cut end, squashed flat when it lies; drawn in code into `assets/sprites/boss/fruit.png`, 9 x 9 px over its box) with a box of 8 x 8 px, that falls straight down at 120 px/s from where he dropped it. In phases 1 and 2 it bursts and is gone when it touches a tile. In phase 3 it lies on that tile's top for 3.0 s (180 steps), then it is gone: these lying fruit are phase 3's ground hazards (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned"). Touching one, falling or lying, is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), pushed away from the fruit. The claw and fireballs pass through fruit (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). A fruit has no telegraph of its own: it falls in view for 43 to 56 steps (0.7 to 0.9 s) before it reaches a Poncho standing on the floor below where it was dropped (the first of a lap from the right end, dropped on its 40th step at x = 316, reaches him on the 83rd), longer than the 0.5 s of GDD-ENM-01 (measured by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`; Open Question 20). In 1-4 no fruit falls on a pillar top (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). `FRUIT`.
- **Touching him** is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), pushed away from his middle, while he laps, telegraphs, dives, drops or sweeps. Stuck in the sand, climbing back, hurt or defeated he does no harm. A step where several things touch Poncho is one hit, pushed away from the first of spike, enemy, Don Zopilote, fruit (GDD-CMB-03; checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). In 1-4, on a pillar top nothing of his reaches a Poncho standing there in any phase, neither he nor his fruit, while Poncho's middle is at least 5 px in from the pillar's inner edge (x = 59 or less on the left pillar, 421 or more on the right); nearer that edge (he can stand with his middle up to just under 6 px past it), the dive or the drop beside that pillar, his box's side flush with the pillar's wall, reaches him (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`; Open Question 19).
- **Rocks:** a rock is a 12 x 12 px box resting on its spot, drawn as an outlined lump of red sandstone 13 x 13 px (`assets/sprites/boss/rock.png`). A claw swipe (GDD-CMB-02) whose box overlaps a resting rock knocks it the way the swipe faces, at 90 px/s across and 150 px/s up, from the next step; it then falls at 1500 px/s², and it breaks when it touches a tile, falls out of the level or touches Don Zopilote. A broken rock is back on its spot 2.0 s (120 steps) later. A rock never hurts Poncho (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). In 1-4 a rock knocked inwards off the left pillar breaks on the floor on its 23rd step, about 35 px out from where it rested, and one knocked back over its pillar breaks on the pillar top (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts` and `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). `ROCK`.
- **Hitting him (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned": a claw swipe or a fireball only while he is stuck in the sand after a dive, a rock knocked onto him with a claw swipe, never a stomp; details Proposed):** a flying rock that touches him is a hit, in any phase, unless he is hurt or defeated. A claw swipe or a fireball (GDD-PWR-03) that touches him is a hit only while he is stuck in the sand, so only in phases 1 and 3; a fireball that touches him is used up either way, unless he is defeated, and passes through him then. Stomps never hit him. A hit on him is settled before his touch, so his own touch on the step that hits him does not hurt Poncho. A step that costs Poncho a life does not hit him (as such a step never reaches an exit, GDD-WLD-03).
- **Phase 2 in 1-4 (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`):** standing on a pillar top facing the middle (x = 40 or 440), a claw press knocks its rock onto him when it comes in a window of steps: on the pillar he drops beside, from the 12th to the 35th step after his drop starts (24 steps); on the pillar he climbs beside, from 36 steps before his climb starts to 28 steps after it (65 steps).
- **After a hit:** he is hurt for 1.0 s (60 steps), still, his body flashing white, 4 steps on and 4 off, and can neither be hit nor hurt anyone; then he climbs to the nearer end of his lap and starts the next phase. The third hit defeats him: his fruit is gone at once, he falls at 120 px/s, flashing white, until his box's bottom is on the floor, and 1.0 s (60 steps) after that hit the level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01, GDD-UI-02).
- **His defeat (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-04: "Crown pops off, he flops"):** on the hit that defeats him his crown, by then just the gold band with its tuna jewel, pops off his head, its bottom middle starting on top of his head in the frame he shows on the hit's step: tumbling, when he has room for it above the floor, 15 px from his middle on the side he faces and 4.5 px below his middle; lying on the floor, 11 px to the other side (he lies on his back) and 5 px below (`BOSS_ART.headTumble`, `headLying`, measured from the art by `scripts/art/boss.test.mjs`). It moves 45 px/s across towards the arena's middle (rightwards from the middle itself) and starts 180 px/s up under 1800 px/s², rising 7.5 px; it bounces once on the floor at 0.3 of its speed and rests there at its second touch (a step that puts it exactly on the floor is a touch), its frame tilted on every other 4 steps while in the air (`BOSS_DEFEAT` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `bossCrown` in `src/sim/boss.ts`; drawn 12 x 8 px from `assets/sprites/boss/crown.png`, its bottom on the floor). In 1-4, hit stuck at the start (x 232) it first touches the floor on the 14th step after the hit and rests on the 18th, after a bounce of 0.9 px; from wherever he can be defeated (his middle from y 56, the top of his lap, down to the floor) it rests by the 47th, before the clear screen (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts` and `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). Falling, he tumbles, flashing white, turning between two tumbling frames every 6 steps; a tumbling frame shows only while it fits above the floor (the one 12 px deep, the other 7 px), else the next one that does; on the floor he lies on his back, the three dazed dots over his head (`src/ui/bossPose.ts`). No frame reaches into the floor.
- **Played through:** a bot that waits at the start, runs 64 px past where a dive aims, claws him while he is stuck, climbs the left pillar in phase 2 and claws its rock when he drops beside it, beats him with all seven lives in under 1500 steps (25 s) (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). A player's first try takes longer, and with the lives carried through World 1 (GDD-LIFE-05) a player may reach him with fewer than seven.
- **Sounds (GDD-AUD-02; his own voice, user, 2026-11-04: "Telegraph + hit + defeat"):** real vultures have no song and can only hiss and grunt, so he does: a raspy hiss at the start of every telegraph, a warning before each attack; a grunt on a hit that leaves him hurt; a long deflating hiss on the hit that defeats him. His landing in the sand after a dive plays the land. Generated in code like the other sounds (GDD-AUD-02).

---

### GDD-BOS-03 World 2 boss

Status: Decided (La Estrella, a star piñata boss in her own arena, user, 2026-11-04: "La Estrella"; a piñata boss met only in her own arena, under a boss bar, is fine with the user, which settles GDD-PWR-05's "never hide a lethal surprise" for her). Her rules, numbers and arena are Proposed. Built first with placeholder art, as Don Zopilote's was, then with her final art ("Her look") (`src/sim/estrella.ts`, `src/levels/level2-4.ts`, `src/ui/estrellaPose.ts`; tested in `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`, `src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts`, `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts` and `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`); her placeholder look approved from screenshots with World 2's (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`); her final art designed with no reference images (user, 2026-11-09: "Design it, show me", with "Cut-out eyes") and approved from its design canvas (user, 2026-11-09: "I approve the la Estrellas design"; `assets/characters/la-estrella/`), built ("Her look", below). Three choices the main session made during the build (2026-11-04) are Proposed, for the user to confirm (Open Question 33): when she can be hit, her phase 3 drop through the awnings, and her telegraph's shake (below). Her redesign (user, 2026-11-08, answering the main session's boss design, which had asked for two phases split at 60% and five hits): her three phases and three hits stay ("Let's keep the 3-3 split"), the middle awning is torn away at the start of phase 3 ("Yes"; built, below), no new attacks are added (the design's candy rain and spin throw, "Not sure if we need it if we stick to 3-3"; left out, Open Question 35), the tier 3 bump while she glides stays ("Keep it"), and her fight gets the boss hit feedback of GDD-BOS-01, hit-stop and screen shake ("Yes I like the idea"; both built).

La Estrella de Siete Picos, a giant seven-pointed posada piñata hanging from a trolley on a cable stretched between two rooftops over the plaza, in the one-screen arena 2-4. In the posada tradition her seven points stand for the seven deadly sins and breaking her is good's triumph over them; here the curse of the story seed (GDD-VIS-01) has got into her. Her seven paper cones echo Poncho's seven triangles: each hit tears some off (7, then 5, then 3, then none), so the fight's progress is drawn on her as well as on the boss bar. **Her one idea:** she is hit the way any piñata is, by a head bump from below or the claw (GDD-PWR-05), but she hangs too high to reach from the floor, so every hit is earned with World 2's elements, a rebound off an awning (GDD-WLD-10) or a jump from a cordel (GDD-WLD-11); and like a real piñata on its rope she is yanked up and down (the *jalón*). Three phases, each teaching or testing World 2's elements (GDD-BOS-01), and three hits. She copies no other game's boss.

- **The arena, level 2-4, "La Plaza" (the name Proposed, Open Question 26):** 30 x 17 tiles, one screen (480 x 272 px), World 2's last level: "Next level" on 2-3's results menu starts it with 2-3's lives (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02), and `?level=2-4` opens it (`e2e/estrella.spec.ts`). The boss-level rule holds it (GDD-BOS-01, GDD-TEC-04): no ofrendas, exit, hilos or veladora, and the spawn is the only respawn. The floor is flat, its top at row 14 (y = 224) across the whole width, with no pit. **Three whole awnings,** each 4 tiles wide with its top at y = 176 (48 px above the floor, so Poncho walks under them): x = 48 to 112 (columns 3 to 6), 208 to 272 (13 to 16) and 368 to 432 (23 to 26); from the start of phase 3 the middle one is torn away and only the side two are left (see "The tear", below). **Two cordeles** between them, their tops at y = 136 (40 px above the awnings): x = 112 to 208 (columns 7 to 12) and 272 to 368 (17 to 22). The cable runs across the top at y = 8, from rooftop to rooftop (scenery), and her rope hangs from its trolley. Poncho starts on the floor at x = 240 (column 15, under the middle awning); she starts phase 1 high over x = 400, phase 2 over 320 and phase 3 over 400, her box's middle 256, 215 and 256 px from the spawn, more than 3 tiles (GDD-ENM-01; measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`, and checked by the validator, GDD-TEC-04). From the spawn a full jump rises through the middle awning and lands on it, and held through the landing it rebounds (measured). Drawn with World 2's art, the plaza with its kiosco in place of the rooftops (GDD-ART-06), with 1 decor item, a talavera pot (`src/ui/plants.test.ts`); its medal is gold for at most 3 lives lost in it, else silver, as in 1-4 (GDD-PRG-03).
- **Her look (Decided from the approved design canvas, user, 2026-11-09; how the game picks her frames Proposed):** a posada star piñata as `assets/characters/la-estrella/README.md` describes it: a terracotta clay olla, the box, with a cobalt talavera band; two almond eyes cut into the clay with a one-pixel yellow pupil (neutral, glancing at Poncho, wide, squinted when hurt, X's when defeated); seven crepe-paper cones behind the pot (rosa up, teal, yellow, cobalt down), the top three with glaze-white tips, a ragged stub where one is torn; cracks that grow with each hit; a three-strand tassel at each cone tip; an iron pulley trolley on the cable and an ixtle rope tied into her top cone's tip; her tejocote an orange fruit with a turning calyx. Drawn in code at the scale of Poncho's drawing (2 x 2 art px a drawing pixel), outlined, only in World 2's colours and the flash reds every world's enemies share; a frame is 72 x 72 game px with her box's middle at its middle. **Frame choices (the design's proposals and the build's, Proposed):** her in-air looks reach up to 14 px below her box, so she shows a splayed look (the bottom cones swung to the sides and crumpled short) from 14 px above a surface she is dropping onto (14 px itself counting): her rest look as she nears it, her land look (squashed 1 px, 1 px wider each side) on the step she lands, her rest look while she rests, her rise look (splayed, the tassels streaming down) as she rises until she is more than 14 px clear, then her yank look (wide eyes) for the rest of the rise, and her bounce look off an awning; nothing of her reaches below her box there or when she lies defeated. Her telegraph looks left or right, the way Poncho is from her; her glide trails its tassels against the way she moves (the glide frame mirrored); her hang leans with the way her sway moves, and toward Poncho where she does not sway (phase 1, a hang's first step). The trolley's wheel turns an eighth a 2.83 px of her glide (a wheel 3.6 px in radius, 7.2 px across), and a tejocote's calyx a quarter a 6.28 px it rolls (about 4 steps). Defeated, the pot keeps its seven torn stubs, falls without its rope and lies on its side once less than 2 px above the floor (`ESTRELLA_ART.fallBelow`; its falling look reaches 2 px below its box, so it never shows a pixel into the floor, measured over drops from every height by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). Built in `scripts/art/estrella.mjs` (`assets/sprites/boss/la-estrella.png`, 49 frames; `tejocote.png`, `trolley.png`, `rope.png`, `cable.png`), tested pixel for pixel against the approved design in `scripts/art/estrella.test.mjs`; the frame choice in `src/ui/estrellaPose.ts` (`ESTRELLA_ART` in `src/config/tuning.ts`), tested in `src/ui/estrellaPose.test.ts`, and over the real fight in `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`, every frame shown fitting where it is. The red telegraph flash and the white hurt flash are frames of their own; the eyes stay dark through both.
- **Her body:** a box of 32 x 32 px, the clay pot at her core, drawn as a star about 56 px across; her cones and streamers outside the box are paper and harmless, so a near miss feels fair. She cannot be stomped: her three top cones have pale tips (GDD-CMB-01), and landing on her is a touch like any other. Touching her (Poncho's hurtbox on her box; boxes that only share an edge do not touch) is a hit (GDD-CMB-03) while she glides, telegraphs, drops, is thrown up by an awning, rests, rises, hangs or is yanked; hurt or defeated she does no harm. Her box's bottom hangs at one of three heights: high, y = 40, her box's top then at the cable (y = 8); low, y = 112 in phase 1 and y = 72 in phases 2 and 3. Wherever she aims and however she sways, her box stays on the screen and under the cable and never ends a drop inside a tile (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`).
- **Hitting her** (in any phase, only while she glides, telegraphs, hangs or is yanked: never while she drops, is thrown up by an awning, rests on the floor or rises from it, when her box is low enough for a jump or a claw from the floor; the main session's reading, 2026-11-04, of the user's "only reachable from awning rebounds or cordel jumps", Proposed, Open Question 33; that she can be hit while she glides, so the expert's tier 3 bump stays, user, 2026-11-08: "Keep it"): a **bump**, Poncho rising with his head reaching her underside, his hurtbox's top at or below her box's bottom on the step before and above it after, and his body box overlapping her box's width (the piñata's rule, GDD-PWR-05, and the cactus buddy's from-below test, GDD-ENM-02), which stops his rise there as a piñata does, his head flush under her, so that, still holding jump, he falls back to the awning and the chain goes on: after a bump off a tier 1 rebound the next landing is tier 2 (GDD-WLD-10; `src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts`); or a **claw** swipe whose box overlaps her (GDD-CMB-02), in mid-air beside her. In no phase does a jump, a claw or a jump with a claw from the floor hit her, though such moves reach her box low in her drops, bounces, rests and rises (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). A hit on her is settled before her touch, so the step that hits her does not hurt him, and a step that costs Poncho a life does not hit her (as in GDD-BOS-02). **Fireballs** are used up on her and do nothing, since a piñata is not burnt open: her rule overrides GDD-PWR-03's "1 damage per fireball" (GDD-BOS-01); once she is defeated they pass through her.
- **Reach (measured over the real arena by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`, and against each height by `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`, L-003):** with jump held all the way, his hurtbox's top peaks at y = 136.56 from a floor jump, 88.56 from a jump off an awning, 67.92, 47.73 and 24.79 from held rebounds of tiers 1, 2 and 3 off an awning, and 48.56 from a jump off a cordel. So from the floor no jump reaches her at any height; a jump from an awning reaches her phase 1 low height (112) only; a tier 1 or tier 2 rebound reaches both her low heights (112 and 72), tier 1 barely; a jump from a cordel reaches her phases 2 and 3 low height (72) but not her phase 1 low, since standing on the cordel his head is already above 112, beside her, not under her; and only a tier 3 rebound reaches her at her high height: the expert's shortcut, never needed. (The plan had a tier 2 rebound or a cordel jump for the low height of phases 2 and 3; tier 1 reaches it too.)
- **Her cycle, in every phase:** she **glides** along the cable at her high height to her next drop spot; **telegraphs** for 0.5 s (30 steps, GDD-ENM-01): she stops, her body flashes red 4 steps on and 4 off, and her body and her trolley shake 2 px to either side on the cable, flipping every 4 steps (`ESTRELLA.shakePx`: the main session's choice, 2026-11-04, Proposed, in place of the planned zigzag of her rope, which has no length up there; her tassels, drawn with her final art, trail and settle as "Her look" says); then the **jalón**, a drop straight down until her box's bottom meets the surface under her middle, a whole awning's top or the floor (she passes through cordeles: they hold a cat, not a clay pot); in phase 3 she tears through the awnings instead (below). Every drop follows its 30 steps of telegraph high on the cable and falls for at least 23 steps, so it is seen to fall (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). Dropped onto an **awning**, she is thrown straight back up to her low height at 240 px/s (the tarp's dip under her is not drawn yet). Dropped onto the **floor**, she thuds, **rests** 0.3 s (18 steps) while fruit rolls out of her (below), and is pulled up to her low height at 120 px/s. She **hangs** low for her phase's window, the "dale, dale, dale" moment, then is **yanked** back up to her high height at 180 px/s and glides on.
- **Tejocotes:** her filling, the small orange fruit of the posada piñata: 8 x 8 px, rolling out of her middle on the floor at each thud, a pair at a time, one each way (`TEJOCOTE.pairs`, a pair in phases 1 and 2, two in phase 3, the second 24 steps after the first), at 90 px/s along the floor, gone at a wall (from x = 160, on the 105th step leftwards, the 211th rightwards; measured by `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`). Touching one is a hit (GDD-CMB-03); a claw swipe or a fireball (used up) bursts one. A short hop (about 15 px, GDD-MOV-02) clears one (worked out by hand), and on an awning or a cordel Poncho is clear of them. Her defeat clears them at once; a hurt does not. `TEJOCOTE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `size` 8, `rollSpeed` 90, `secondPairSteps` 24, `pairs` [1, 1, 2].
- **Phase 1, "Arriba" (teaches the awning):** her drop spots are x = 80 and x = 400, the middles of the side awnings, in turn: she starts high over 400 and glides at 80 px/s. She drops at 300 px/s onto the awning, which throws her up to y = 112; she hangs there 2.0 s (120 steps), still, then is yanked up and glides to the other side. Measured (`src/sim/estrella.test.ts`): from the fight's start she telegraphs over x = 80 on step 240, lands on the left awning on step 298, hangs on step 314 and is back at the cable on step 458. She never reaches the floor, so a Poncho on the floor is never touched in phase 1; on the awning under her drop her box spans x = 64 to 96 (384 to 416 over the right one), and with his 10 px hurtbox a Poncho standing at x = 60 or 420 is reached and one at 58 or 422 is not, nor one on a side awning's outer end in phases 1 and 2 (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). The intended play: wait beside the awning while she drops, jump onto it holding jump, and the tier 1 rebound bumps her; or jump from the settled awning. The awning she lands on is the awning that reaches her.
- **Phase 2, "Vaivén" (teaches the cordeles):** her drop spots become x = 160 and x = 320, over the cordeles' middles, where there is no awning: she drops at 300 px/s through the cordel to the floor, rests, and one tejocote rolls out each way; she is pulled up to y = 72, hangs there 1.5 s (90 steps) swaying 8 px each way on a 1.0 s sine, and is yanked up. She glides at 100 px/s; the phase starts with her high over 320, so her first drop is at 160. Measured (`src/sim/estrella.test.ts`): from the phase's start she telegraphs over x = 160 on step 96, is on the floor on step 163, rests 18 steps, hangs on step 257, and her yank reaches the cable in 11 steps. Her drop through a cordel hits a Poncho standing on it under her. The intended play: jump or bounce up onto a cordel, wait beside the drop spot, then step under her as she hangs and jump; or chain held rebounds on an awning and drift under her.
- **Phase 3, "Remolino" (tests both):** her drop spot is where Poncho is (from her second drop on, after the tear below): as the telegraph ends she takes the middle of his hurtbox, across only, kept between x = 56 and 424 (Don Zopilote's aim, GDD-BOS-02), and drops at 360 px/s in a straight line to the floor under that spot. **She tears straight through the awnings** (they stay whole, all but the middle one her first drop tears away, "The tear" below) to the floor (`ESTRELLA.tearsAwnings` [false, false, true]; the main session's choice, 2026-11-04, Proposed, in place of the plan's throw up from an awning), so standing under an awning is no shelter: standing still under the one she aims at, at x = 80, 240 or 400, her drop reaches him: before the tear, standing still under the middle awning at x = 240, her first drop, the tear, reaches him; after it, only the side two are left, and standing still under one at x = 80 or 400 her aimed drop reaches him (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). On the floor she thuds and two tejocotes roll each way, the second pair 0.4 s (24 steps) after the first, and she rests and rises to y = 72. She hangs 1.0 s (60 steps) swaying 16 px each way, glides at 120 px/s, and her glide always heads for the spot above Poncho (from her second drop on, after the tear below). The intended play: keep moving between awnings and cordeles, make her drop where he is not, then get under her within the second, from a cordel or with a tier 2 chain; or the expert's tier 3 bump while she glides.
- **The tear, phase 3's first drop (user, 2026-11-08: "Yes"; built, details Proposed; `Estrella.tear` and `tore` in `src/sim/estrella.ts`, the awning marked gone in `src/sim/run.ts`; tested in `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`, `src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts`, `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts` and `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`):** phase 3 starts, as now, with her high over x = 400, but her first glide heads for x = 240, the middle awning's middle, not for the spot above Poncho; there she telegraphs as in every drop (30 steps, the red flash and the shake) and drops at 360 px/s straight down at x = 240, without aiming at Poncho. On the step her box's bottom reaches the middle awning's top she tears it away: its four tiles are gone for the rest of the fight, so nothing stands or rebounds there and Poncho on it falls (drawn as any torn awning once gone, GDD-WLD-10: its posts, with its tarp's two halves hanging at its ends; the main session's choice for the build, 2026-11-08, in place of a tarp falling to the floor, so no new art; approved from screenshots, user, 2026-11-08: "Approved", `docs/screenshots/estrella-tear/`). She goes on to the floor and her cycle goes on as in any phase 3 drop (the thud, two pairs of tejocotes, the rest, the rise to y = 72, the hang); from her second drop on she aims at Poncho. A life lost in phase 3 before the tear starts phase 3 again with the middle awning whole and her first drop at 240 again; one lost after it starts phase 3 with the awning still gone and her first glide heading for the spot above Poncho, so the tear is seen once. A fresh fight (after the run restarts at 2-1, GDD-BOS-01) has the middle awning whole. The level's data keeps all three awnings (the validator's checks of 2-4 are unchanged, GDD-TEC-04): the tear is the fight's state (2-4's `tear`: x = 240, the top y = 176, awning 1). Measured from phase 3's start with Poncho away from her line (`src/sim/estrella.test.ts`, `src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts`): her drop at 240 starts on step 110, after 80 steps of glide and 30 of telegraph; her box's bottom is at 172 on step 132 and 178 on step 133, when she tears the awning; she is on the floor on step 141 and hangs on step 235. Why: the middle awning is over the spawn, the player's safe home in phases 1 and 2; losing it is phase 3's twist, and the arena becomes a valley between two sides. Its shake: GDD-BOS-01's hit feedback; its sound is still open (Open Question 35).
- **After a hit:** she is hurt for 1.0 s (60 steps), still, flashing white 4 steps on and 4 off, harmless and unhittable; two cones tear off (three in phase 3: `ESTRELLA.cones` [7, 5, 3, 0], the bottom ones first, the pale top three last). Then she is yanked straight up from where she is to her high height and starts the next phase from its drop spot nearest her (hurt over x = 400, her phase 2 starts at 320; measured by `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`). Not built yet: the torn cones drifting down as paper confetti (scenery).
- **A life lost mid-fight (as GDD-BOS-01 says for every boss):** she keeps her hits and starts her phase again from its start, high over that phase's first drop spot (a life lost while she is hurt starts the next phase); every tejocote is gone; Poncho respawns at the spawn (`src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts`, `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`). The middle awning she tore away stays gone after a life lost later in phase 3, and her first glide then heads for the spot above Poncho; a life lost before the tear, or while she is hurt after her second hit, starts phase 3 with it whole ("The tear"). Running out of lives sends the run back to 2-1 with seven (GDD-LIFE-05), and the fight starts afresh, with all three hits, when Poncho next reaches the arena.
- **Defeat, the third hit:** her rope snaps and the empty clay pot falls at 120 px/s to the floor and lies there, her tejocotes gone at once; 1.0 s (60 steps) after the hit the level clear screen comes up (GDD-UI-02). 2-4 is World 2's last level, so its results menu greys "Next level" while there is no World 3 (GDD-UI-02; `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`). Not built yet: her burst in a shower of paper and candy (scenery only: a boss drops no lives, GDD-BOS-01).
- **Sounds (GDD-AUD-02):** a hit on her plays the stomp, the end of her drop, on the floor or an awning, the land, and her defeat Poncho's meow; nothing else is new.
- **Boss bar (GDD-UI-01):** "La Estrella", the same in every language (her name's key `estrella` in `TEXT`, `src/ui/text.ts`), and three pips, one emptied by each hit (`src/ui/bossBar.ts`). Gliding high she can pass behind it, and the HUD (the lives, the boss bar) is drawn on top of her; her top cone may reach past the screen's top (Open Question 33).
- **Played through (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`):** a bot beats her from the spawn with all seven lives in 1035 steps (under 1800, about 17 s), hitting her on steps 344, 663 and 975. In phase 1 it waits on the floor under the left awning and, when she hangs over it, jumps onto the awning holding jump through the landing: the tier 1 rebound bumps her. In phase 2 it jumps from the left awning onto the left cordel and waits at x = 127, clear of her drop at 160; when she hangs it steps under her and a cordel jump bumps her. In phase 3 it stays on the left cordel and waits at its right end, x = 205, clear of her tear at 240 and above the tejocotes, and when she hangs a cordel jump bumps her once her sway brings her within 21 px across (a bump needs under 22; at 12 or 16 px the bot never hits her), so it never meets her aimed drops. The hit-stops (GDD-BOS-01) make the fight 20 steps' time longer (4, 4 and 12, about 0.33 s), in which no step runs, so they do not change the steps counted here. A player's first try takes longer, and with the lives carried through World 2 (GDD-LIFE-05) a player may reach her with fewer than seven.
- `ESTRELLA` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `width` 32, `height` 32, `highBottom` 40, `lowBottom` [112, 72, 72], `glideSpeed` [80, 100, 120], `telegraphSteps` 30, `dropSpeed` [300, 300, 360], `awningThrow` 240, `tearsAwnings` [false, false, true], `restSteps` 18, `riseSpeed` 120, `hangSteps` [120, 90, 60], `swayPx` [0, 8, 16], `swayPeriodSteps` 60, `yankSpeed` 180, `hurtSteps` 60, `defeatSteps` 60, `fallSpeed` 120, `hits` 3, `aimMinX` 56, `aimMaxX` 424, `cones` [7, 5, 3, 0], `coneTip` 28 (a star about 56 px across), `cableY` 8, `shakePx` 2, `flashSteps` 4.

### GDD-ENM-04 World 3 bestiary

Status: Decided (three new enemies to start with, the Murciélago, the Armadillo and the Cacomixtle, and the Cascabel and the Borrego cimarrón kept as later ideas, user, 2026-11-10: "Start with 3 and keep the other 2 in gdd", picked from the main session's five candidates). Their rules, names and numbers are Proposed: the World 3 design swarm's detail (user, 2026-11-10: "an agent swarm to design the remaining parts"), reconciled by the main session. Not built. "Measured" below means run on the real `src/sim/movement.ts` by the swarm's scratch probe outside the repository (a Poncho set as `src/sim/run.ts` sets him on a stomp bounce, in open air, at the full 120 px/s run), or read from `src/config/tuning.ts`; each is to be pinned by a test when the enemy is built. Everything else is a target.

World 3's enemies follow GDD-ENM-01 and World 2's line: animals of the Sierra Madre and things of its mines, no people (GDD-VIS-04); one idea each, a 0.5 s telegraph before any attack, each beaten at least one way, none starting within 3 tiles of a respawn, never more than one life per contact. The first three below are World 3's enemies, one new one for each level (GDD-WLD-16 to GDD-WLD-18), as World 2 had three, each met alone on safe ground before it is combined; the last two are later ideas, not built with them, as the Zanate is in World 2 (GDD-ENM-03). None of the three carries pale points on top (GDD-CMB-01): each can be landed on.

| Enemy | One idea | Defeated by | Met in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Murciélago (bat) | Flies a fixed loop that never reacts to Poncho. In a glide's way it is a thing to read; from above it is a step up | Stomp, claw or fireball; a rolling cart | 3-1; again in 3-3 |
| Armadillo | Walks; near Poncho it tucks (a 0.5 s telegraph) and rolls at him. The ball is safe to land on and never safe to touch from the side, and it rolls off ledges | Walking, tucking or dazed: stomp, claw or fireball. Rolling: nothing but a rolling cart; jump or bounce over it | 3-2; again in 3-3 |
| Cacomixtle (ringtail) | Rides a vagoneta down its rail at Poncho (GDD-WLD-15): the rail shows where the danger will be | Stomp, claw or fireball; or jump over it | 3-3 |
| Cascabel (rattlesnake) | Coiled on a ledge; rattles for 0.5 s, then strikes two tiles ahead and lies stretched out for a moment | Stomp or claw while stretched out; a fireball always | Later idea, not built |
| Borrego cimarrón (bighorn ram) | Stands on a ledge and charges along it; its butt is a hit with a far stronger push, off the ledge | Claw from behind, or let it run past and stomp | Later idea, not built |

- **Murciélago (bat):** a 16 x 10 px bat, World 3's basic enemy from 3-1 on. **Its one idea:** it is where the clock says, never where Poncho is, so it teaches reading the air before a glide, and that an enemy can be a step.
  - **Path:** the level gives the middle of its loop, `rx` (0 to 48 px), `ry` (0 to 24 px) and a start `phase` in steps. Its box's middle is at the loop's middle plus (`rx` cos a, `ry` sin a), a = 2π (steps + phase) / 240: one loop every 4.0 s (240 steps), counted like a moving platform's steps (GDD-WLD-05), so it is the same on every run. `rx` 0 is a bob up and down, `ry` 0 a shuttle, both 0 a hover. It ignores tiles, awnings and rails; the level validator checks that its loop's whole box touches no solid tile, spike or crumbling tile (GDD-TEC-04).
  - **Speeds (from those numbers):** at most 75 px/s across and 38 px/s up or down, slower than the glide's 70 px/s fall (`GLIDE.fallSpeed`), so a gliding Poncho above a bat always closes on it and a bat never drops away from under his feet.
  - **No attack, no telegraph,** like the Trompo (GDD-ENM-03). It has one state, flying. Touching it from the side or from below is a hit (GDD-CMB-03); rising into one on a bounce is the mistake it punishes.
  - **Stomp:** defeats it in any part of its loop, with the usual bounce (GDD-CMB-01). A glide holds jump, so a stomp out of a glide is always the high bounce, 410 px/s: his feet peak 56.4 px over the stomp at step 18 (0.3 s) and, jump still held, the glide starts again on that very step with no new press; he is back at the stomp's height 68 steps (1.13 s) and 136 px on (measured). A stomp from a plain fall with jump up peaks 22.9 px at step 12 (measured).
  - **The glide's stomp lock is fair here (GDD-MOV-07):** the lock is 9 steps and both bounces rise for longer (12 and 18 steps, measured), so it never delays a glide that is held or pressed after a stomp. Only a bounce cut short by letting go at once and pressing again starts its glide at step 9, one step after its peak (measured). No rule changes.
  - **A stomped bat stays gone** until a life is lost, like every enemy, and a row of bats is a reward route only: no level needs a bat as a step (the main session's default; Open Question 36). In a level row, bats up to 8 tiles (128 px) apart are reached at a full run by holding jump (he is 16 px up at 110 px, level at 136 px); in a climbing row each bat is at most 5 tiles on and 2 tiles up (he is 32 px up at 82 px); a bat in a row has `rx` 0 and `ry` at most 8 px (targets from the measured bounce, each to be checked by a test over its level). 3-3 has the one row (GDD-WLD-18).
  - **Claw and fireball** defeat it (a light enemy); the fireball is used up. **A rolling cart** defeats one whose box it runs into; one at a rider's head is the rider's hit (GDD-WLD-15). **Crumbling tiles and pits** mean nothing to it. **Avoidance:** let go of jump to drop under it, or glide over it. **A lost life** brings every bat back, its steps counted again from 0.
  - **Look (Proposed, not drawn; colours from World 3's palette, GDD-ART-06, to be approved from screenshots):** the lesser long-nosed bat, *el murciélago magueyero* (*Leptonycteris yerbabuenae*), which flies up the Sierra each year and pollinates the agaves mezcal is made from; a friend of the land, no fangs and no blood (GDD-VIS-04). Its silhouette is a wide scalloped M of wings with two tall ears, unlike the Zopilote's flat fingered wings and bare head. A rust body with ochre light wing fingers and ears (a dark bat vanishes against the canyon's mid layer and the gallery wall); a round friendly eye. Frame 32 x 24 px centred on its box: four flap frames of 5 steps; the wing tips reach outside the box, the body fills it. Defeated: the squash frame or the knocked-over frame, then the poof (GDD-ENM-02).
  - **Sound:** a soft leathery flap, two quick beats, once a loop when it is on screen, quiet; the stomp is the usual thud. No screech.
  - `MURCIELAGO` (to be): `width` 16, `height` 10, `periodSteps` 240, `maxRx` 48, `maxRy` 24, `rowRy` 8.
- **Armadillo:** a 16 x 12 px armadillo, walking or rolled up, 3-2's new enemy. **Its one idea:** a shell with a safe side: the ball is a hit from the side and a springboard from above. It teaches jumping over a low, fast thing under a low roof, and it is the enemy the cart is the answer to.
  - **Walk:** it starts heading right and walks at 20 px/s, turning at walls and ledge ends and following slopes like the Alacrán (`src/sim/walker.ts`).
  - **Startle:** while it walks, when Poncho's hurtbox centre is within 80 px of its middle across, on either side, and his feet within 24 px of its feet up or down, it stops, turns to face him and telegraphs for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps): it tucks nose to tail, flashing red 4 steps on and 4 off. A Poncho whose feet are more than 24 px above it does not startle it.
  - **Roll:** it then rolls the way it faced, without turning to follow him, at 130 px/s on the level, 160 px/s downhill and 100 px/s uphill, measured along the ground: just faster than his 120 px/s run, slower than a cart (GDD-WLD-15). Unlike every walker it rolls off a ledge's end: it falls as Poncho falls (GDD-MOV-04) keeping its speed across, rolls on where it lands, and is gone until a life is lost if it falls out of the level. It passes a buffer (GDD-WLD-15).
  - **Dazed:** a wall stops the roll. It lies uncurled on its back for 1.0 s (60 steps), harmless to touch, kicking its feet in the last 0.5 s (drawn only, as the Tlacuache twitches), then walks away from the wall, and may be startled again at once. A Poncho inside it when it gets up is hit. (The designer's text also has a slope's high side stopping the roll, which contradicts the uphill speed above; open, Open Question 36.)
  - **By state:** walking or tucking, a touch is a hit, and a stomp (the usual bounce), the claw, a fireball (used up) or a rolling cart defeats it. Rolling, a touch from the side or below is a hit; a stomp bounces Poncho (260 or 410 px/s) and harms neither, and it rolls on; the claw's swing rings off it; a fireball is used up and does nothing; only a rolling cart defeats it. Dazed, a touch is no hit, and a stomp, the claw, a fireball or a rolling cart defeats it.
  - **Fair to read (targets, from the measured jump):** a standing Poncho has 1.1 s from the first flash to contact (0.5 s, then 80 px at 130 px/s). The ball passes his hurtbox in 12 steps; a full jump has his feet over its 12 px for 30 steps (measured), so the jump has an 18-step window; the smallest hop clears it for 9 steps (measured), enough only when he runs at it (6 steps to pass). A jump held into a glide clears it for 56 steps (measured): the glide is the easy answer. Running into one in its telegraph is a hit, as with the Paloma. A claw rushed in during the tuck defeats it (25 of the 30 steps from 80 px).
  - **Not the Tlacuache, not the Trompo:** the Tlacuache sees only ahead, never leaves its ledge and can be stomped while it charges; the Trompo never stops and is opened by the claw. The Armadillo sees both ways, cannot be harmed at all while it rolls, carries Poncho's bounce on its back, leaves its ledge, and is beaten by timing (before the roll, or after the wall) or by a cart.
  - **With World 3's things:** it does not set off crumbling tiles; one that loses its ground, walking or rolling, falls. Rails are scenery to it. No Armadillo's walk or roll crosses a standing cart's start (the validator, GDD-TEC-04). A ball bounced on from a glide gives the high bounce, as a bat does. **A lost life** brings it back at its start, walking, heading right.
  - **Look (Proposed, not drawn; colours from GDD-ART-06):** the nine-banded armadillo (*Dasypus novemcinctus*), the *ayotochtli*, "turtle-rabbit" in Nahuatl. Its silhouette is a low dome with long mule ears and a tapering ringed tail walking, and a plain disc with one ear tip and the tail's end rolling. A shell in timber lit with mist white bands, so it never melts into the ochre rock. Frame 28 x 16 px walking, 20 x 16 px rolling, feet on the bottom row: a four-frame trot, one frame every 2 px; the tuck in two frames, flashing; the roll in four frames, one every 6 px, the bands going round, dust behind; dazed on its back with stars, feet kicking in its last 0.5 s. **The roll is a cartoon's:** the real nine-banded armadillo does not curl into a ball (as far as the designer knows, to be checked; it is said to jump straight up when startled), and the game says so plainly, as it is honest about the Chapulín's wings.
  - **Sound:** the tuck is a dry rattle of shell plates rising in pitch over its 0.5 s; the roll a low rumble while it is on screen; the wall a hollow knock; a stomp on the ball a hollow woody bonk in place of the thud; the claw on the ball a short tick.
  - `ARMADILLO` (to be): `width` 16, `height` 12, `speed` 20, `sightX` 80, `sightY` 24, `telegraphSteps` 30, `rollSpeed` 130, `rollDown` 160, `rollUp` 100, `dazedSteps` 60, `kickSteps` 30.
- **Cacomixtle (ringtail):** a 14 x 14 px ringtail standing on the floor of its own vagoneta (32 x 16 px, GDD-WLD-15), 3-3's new enemy. **Its one idea:** the rail is its lane: Poncho sees where it will come before it moves. It teaches getting off the line or above it, and it turns the cart from his tool into a threat.
  - **Its box** is the ringtail alone, standing in the middle of the cart's floor; it moves with the cart. The cart is a cart: its floor is a one-way floor to Poncho and its sides do not block or hurt him. A landing in the cart beside the ringtail (9 px of floor each side) is a landing, not a hit.
  - **Waiting:** cart and rider stand at the cart's start. Touching the ringtail is a hit; it can be stomped, clawed or burnt.
  - **Telegraph:** the cart rolls one way along its rail, the way the level gives. While it waits, when Poncho's hurtbox centre is ahead of it that way by more than 0 and up to 200 px and his feet are within 64 px of the cart's floor up or down, it stands up and telegraphs for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps): tail straight up, flashing red 4 steps on and 4 off, two rings of a cart bell, the cart still. Its sight is tested every step against his feet, so a lift or a jump can carry him into it. A Poncho behind it never starts it.
  - **Riding:** then the cart rolls with a cart's speeds (150 px/s level, 120 uphill, 180 downhill), never stopping or turning for him. At a buffer it stops for good and the ringtail stays in it, parked: a hit to touch, beaten the same ways, never starting again. At a broken end cart and rider fly off and fall, gone until a life is lost (no level as designed has one).
  - **Its sight stretch (Proposed; the validator, GDD-TEC-04):** from its cart's start to 200 px ahead or its buffer, whichever is nearer, the rail is level or uphill for it; every ground in that stretch is either within 64 px of the cart's floor less a full jump (so a jump cannot hide him) or more than 64 px plus a full jump from it (so a jump cannot wake it); and the cart, when it is first seen, is clear of the phone's buttons (GDD-CTL-03). Else the level's test names the exception. From the first flash a standing Poncho has 1.8 s, and one running at it 1.0 s (0.5 s, then 140 px closing at 270 px/s). The first one, in 3-3's Ki, rides uphill at 120 px/s toward a Poncho on solid ground with a perch to step onto (GDD-WLD-18).
  - **Stomp, claw, fireball:** each defeats it in any state (a light enemy; a fireball is used up by it and passes through the cart). The stomp bounces Poncho as usual. **The cart then rolls on empty** to its rail's end, an ordinary rolling cart that defeats every enemy it runs into. Unlike his own carts it does not return to its start: emptied or not, it stays where it stops until a life is lost, and Poncho can ride an emptied one from where it stands, one emptied while it waited included (the main session's default; GDD-WLD-15, Open Question 37).
  - **Jumping over it (target, from the measured jump):** the ringtail's top is 14 px over the cart's floor. Even with that floor 16 px over the ground a full jump has his feet higher for 23 steps (measured), and the ringtail passes a standing Poncho in 10 steps: a 13-step window. A glide passes over it with room to spare. Its cart rolls over crumbling tiles without setting them off (GDD-WLD-05).
  - **Other enemies and carts:** a cart Poncho sent rolling defeats a ringtail its box runs into. Carts do not touch each other, and no level lets two carts share a stretch of rail (GDD-WLD-15). **A lost life** puts the cart back at its start with the ringtail in it, waiting.
  - **Look (Proposed, not drawn; colours from GDD-ART-06):** the ringtail (*Bassariscus astutus*), *cacomixtle* from Nahuatl *tlahcomiztli*, "half mountain lion"; miners of the Sierra kept ringtails in their camps to hunt mice and called them miner's cats, so this is the mine's own cat meeting Poncho. Its silhouette is a tall tail, longer than its body, standing over the cart's rim like a flag, with big rounded ears. Ochre light fur, the tail ringed iron dark and mist white, white rings round huge dark eyes, a cheeky grin, paws on the rim. It carries no lantern: the warning post's is the only lit lamp in the play layer (GDD-ART-06). Frame 24 x 28 px, its feet on the cart's floor, drawn in front of the cart's far side and behind its near side: waiting, it dozes with the tail curled along the rim (the tail is still seen); the telegraph standing, tail up; riding, leaning forward, tail streaming back, ears flat, two frames of 4 steps; parked, it peeks over the rim. Defeated: the usual squash or knocked-over frame and poof. The cart keeps GDD-WLD-15's look.
  - **Sound:** the telegraph is two rings of a small cart bell under a chittering bark, 0.5 s; riding, the cart's own rattle, louder as it nears; the stomp is the usual thud, then the cart's rattle goes on alone.
  - `CACOMIXTLE` (to be): `width` 14, `height` 14, `sightX` 200, `sightY` 64, `telegraphSteps` 30; its speeds are the cart's.
- **Returning enemies (Proposed, as the three layouts use them):** the Alacrán, the Durango scorpion of this very Sierra, in 3-1 (three, the review enemy on its solid ledges) and in 3-2 (three, the cart's targets); the Zopilote in 3-3 only, one, over solid ground on the peaks, never over a chasm, a bat row or a rail's broken end. Of World 3's own, the Armadillo and the Murciélago return in 3-3. No cactus buddy (no desert plants grow in the Sierra, GDD-ART-06), no Tlacuache and no Paloma in World 3. The returning enemies keep their rules, their sprites and their own worlds' colours (GDD-ENM-02), outside World 3's 24; how they read against the Sierra's backdrops is not measured and is checked in the screenshots.

### GDD-BOS-04 World 3 boss
