Status: Decided (La Catorce, user, 2026-11-10: "Let's go with la catorce", picked from the main session's three candidates; her arena is the engine shed, "La Casa de Máquinas", user, 2026-11-10: "Engine shed", the option that read '"La Casa de Máquinas": an engine shed in daylight'; a cart Poncho is still inside at the rail's end does not hurt her, and he falls from it on his own, user, 2026-11-10: "He falls on his own"; her arena's crumbled ledges come back, user, 2026-11-10: "Come back after 2 s"). Her rules, numbers and layout below are Proposed: the World 3 design swarm's detail (user, 2026-11-10: "an agent swarm to design the remaining parts"), written before any code, following GDD-BOS-01: one screen, three phases, three hits, each phase testing the world's signature element. Not built. Nothing was run for this section: "measured, rule not built" is a figure GDD-WLD-15 took from the swarm's scratch test of the real jump; "from her numbers" is arithmetic on numbers chosen here; every other number is a hand estimate, a target that a test over the real arena must replace (L-003).

La Catorce, a small narrow-gauge mine engine, number 14, with the curse of the story seed (GDD-VIS-01) burning in her firebox, in the one-screen arena 3-4. She shuttles along the rail on the shed floor. **Her one idea:** she is hurt only by her own mine: an empty vagoneta (GDD-WLD-15) that rolls off the broken end of a high rail and falls on her. Poncho aims it with the moment he lands in it, and must be out of it before the rail ends. Claw, stomp and Fuego do nothing to her. An engine, like La Estrella a thing and not a creature, so nobody is hurt. She copies no other game's boss.

- **The arena, level 3-4, "La Casa de Máquinas" (the engine shed of the upper mine):** 30 x 17 tiles, one screen (480 x 272 px), so the camera does not move (GDD-UI-04). "Next level" on 3-3's results menu starts it with 3-3's lives (GDD-LIFE-05). The boss-level rule holds it (GDD-BOS-01): no ofrendas, exit, hilos or veladora. The glide is on, the wall cling off. It is mirrored about x = 240:
  - **Floor:** the shed's trodden floor, flat, its top at row 14 (y = 224), with no pit. The **low rail** lies on it wall to wall (scenery to Poncho), with a timber buffer drawn at each wall (it blocks nobody) and a **wheel stop** drawn on the rail at x = 48 and x = 432, where she halts in phases 1 and 2. The floor is her lane.
  - **Home, the inspection gantry:** two tiles at columns 14 and 15, row 10 (x = 224 to 256, top y = 160), hung from the roof trusses. Poncho starts and respawns on it (x = 240, y = 160). Solid; from phase 2 her slam shakes one tile of it down ("El derrumbe", below).
  - **Two springs** (GDD-WLD-05) in the floor at columns 12 and 17: a plain launch (about 77 px) steered toward the middle lands him on home, 64 px up (about 13 px to spare). No timed press is ever needed; a timed launch (about 120 px) reaches the cart's floor 104 px up, the expert's shortcut.
  - **Two high rails** carried by the trusses, level, their rail ground at y = 128 (row 8's top), so a cart's floor is at y = 120 and its box is y = 112 to 128. The left rail runs from x = 208 leftwards to a broken end at x = 112 (the left edge of column 7), 6 tiles; the right from x = 272 to x = 368. A cart stands at each start, its box at x = 176 to 208 (left) and 272 to 304 (right), its middle 80 px from the end. From home its floor is 40 px up: a jump peaks at 56.4 px (measured), so 16.4 px to spare. Each rolls outwards. Nothing solid stands in the 7 rows over the rails (the trusses are scenery).
  - **Two perches:** four crumbling tiles (GDD-WLD-05) at each wall, row 7 (top y = 112): columns 0 to 3 (x = 0 to 64) and 26 to 29 (x = 416 to 480), 3 tiles beyond each broken end and 8 px over the cart's floor.
  - **The arena's rail exemption:** GDD-WLD-15's limits for a level (a warning post 6 tiles before a broken end, 6 tiles of level rail before it, the end at least 12 tiles from the cart's start) do not apply in 3-4, and the validator's boss-level rule skips them (GDD-TEC-04). They exist because in a scrolling level the end comes into view during the ride. Here the whole rail, its end, the perch and she are on screen before he boards, the rail is the same on every ride, and the decision is taken standing on home, not in the 0.53 s of the ride. In place of the post, each broken end carries the warning board on its own last sleeper (Open Question 40).
- **Her body:** a box of 64 x 32 px on the floor (bottom y = 224, top y = 192, 16 px under home's underside), that ignores tiles. Touching her (hurtbox on box; a shared edge is no touch) is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), pushed away from her middle, whenever she is not hurt or defeated. She cannot be stomped: three pale steam-valve studs on her boiler top are the mark (GDD-CMB-01). She faces the way she runs. Her pattern is fixed; nothing Poncho does changes it but a hit.
- **Her cycle, in every phase:** she stands at a **stop**; **rests**; **telegraphs** for 0.5 s (30 steps, GDD-ENM-01): her whistle, her wheels spinning on the spot with sparks, her body flashing red 4 steps on and 4 off; **charges** along the rail to the other stop; and **slams** to a halt on the step she reaches it. One end to the other is a **leg**.

| Phase | Stops (her middle, x) | Speed | Rest | A leg, slam to slam (from her numbers) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 "La parada" | 80 and 400 (at the wheel stops) | 80 px/s | 3.0 s (180 steps) | 450 steps (180 + 30 + 240) |
| 2 "El derrumbe" | 80 and 400 | 110 px/s | 1.0 s (60 steps) | 265 steps (60 + 30 + 175) |
| 3 "Sin frenos" | 32 and 448 (at the wall buffers) | 150 px/s | none | 197 steps (30 + 167) |

- **Armoured until her first slam (Proposed, Open Question 40):** from the start of each phase, and again after a lost life, until the step of her first slam in it, her safety valve blows a white plume and nothing hurts her: a cart that lands on her then breaks with a clang. So every phase shows one whole charge before it can be answered.
- **The carts in 3-4** are GDD-WLD-15's (150 px/s on the level, boarded by any landing on the floor, standing or rolling; the carry rule), with these rules:
  - **The fall (GDD-WLD-15's):** on the step a cart's middle passes the broken end it leaves the rail and is no floor from that step; it keeps 150 px/s across and falls at 2250 px/s² up to 360 px/s. Its box's bottom reaches her roof (y = 192, a fall of 64 px) about 16 steps later and 39 px beyond the end, its middle at x = 73 (left) or 407 (right): the **drop spots**, each marked on the low rail by a pair of pale sleepers. It breaks on the step it touches her, a tile or the floor. A falling cart hurts nothing but her.
  - **Ridden off (the user's rule, 2026-11-10: "He falls on his own", written exactly):** a cart is ridden off if Poncho's feet were on its floor on the last step before it left the rail, that is, he was standing in it and carried by it on the step before its middle passed the end. A ridden-off cart never hurts her: it breaks on her with a clang. Nothing else decides it: no box test at the impact, and what he does in the air afterwards (the 0.1 s coyote jump, a glide) saves him but not the hit. He himself falls on his own from the end at the carried 150 px/s, onto her roof if he does nothing, which is a touch. A cart he left earlier, by a jump or by holding against the roll, is empty and counts, wherever he is when it lands.
  - **The return:** GDD-WLD-15's rule with the arena's number (a boss's own rule, GDD-BOS-01): a cart is back at its start, standing, 3.0 s (180 steps) after the step it left its rail, ridden off or not; for the last 0.5 s (30 steps) it is drawn coming down on the hoist's chain and is not yet a floor. A lost life puts both back at once.
- **Hitting her:** a falling cart, not ridden off, whose box overlaps her box is a hit, unless she is armoured, hurt or defeated. Her middle is then within 48 px of the cart's (from her numbers). Nothing else hits her: the claw passes through, a stomp is a touch, a fireball is used up on her and does nothing (her rule overrides GDD-PWR-03's, GDD-BOS-01). A hit on her is settled before her touch.
- **Aiming (targets; the build measures them first):** from the step he lands in a standing cart to its box reaching her roof is 48 steps: 32 of ride over 80 px (from the cart's 150 px/s) and about 16 of fall. From the jump press on home it is about 74 (the jump to the cart takes about 26 steps). Counted from the step of her slam at that end, he must land in the cart within the window below. In a phase's first leg only carts landing on or after the slam count. She is always in view and her legs never vary, so no window is blind.

| Phase | She is under the drop spot | Land in the cart | Rule of thumb |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 30 steps before the slam to 240 after | 78 before to 192 after (271 steps, 4.5 s) | any time she is parked on that side |
| 2 | 22 before to 112 after | 70 before to 64 after (135 steps, 2.25 s) | as she charges that way, or at the latest when home shakes (about 38 steps to react) |
| 3 | 35 before to 65 after | 83 before to 17 after (101 steps, 1.7 s) | as she charges that way, before she passes under home |

- **The jump out:** he must leave the floor before the rail's end. A plain jump keeps the cart's 150 px/s and carries 82.7 px back to the floor's height (measured, rule not built), a little less to the perch's top 8 px higher: taken with his middle between about x = 145 and the end at x = 112 (some 13 steps), it lands on the perch. Taken earlier, anywhere from the cart's start, the same jump held into a glide (170.0 px; measured, rule not built) reaches the perch and he lets go over it. So every takeoff in the ride has an answer, the wall behind the perch stops an overshoot, and he stops within 11.2 px of landing (measured). Holding against the roll he drops out behind the cart (27.9 px ahead of his takeoff; measured, rule not built) onto the floor: the cart still counts, and he is in her lane.
- **The perches** crumble 0.5 s (30 steps) after he lands on each tile and are whole again 2.0 s (120 steps) after they fall, World 3's rule (GDD-WLD-05): a landing, not a place to stay.
- **The way back:** from a perch, home is 10 tiles away and 3 tiles down. A jump held into a glide crosses 14 tiles at that drop, 13 with no run-up and 12 taken two tiles early (measured, GDD-WLD-03's table). That is over World 3's limit of 9 at that drop on purpose: a short glide here lands on the floor, not in a pit, and a spring is the way up. The glide is the way back across; the springs and home are the way back up. A glide that comes down in a standing cart boards it.
- **Phase 1, "La parada" (the stop):** she starts at the right stop and telegraphs at once. Home is solid; on the floor a pocket is left behind each stop (his middle under x = 42 or over 438, from her numbers). The intended play: when she parks on the left, jump from home into the left cart, jump out before the end onto the perch, watch the cart land on her, glide home.
- **Phase 2, "El derrumbe" (the cave-in):** her rest is shorter than a cart's trip, so the cart is sent while she is still coming. Her slam shakes the shed: on that step the home tile on her side (column 14 at the left stop, 15 at the right) starts to crumble, with its 0.5 s of warning, and is whole again 2.0 s after it falls. Poncho's weight never starts a home tile; how such a tile is drawn, so that it does not read as a plank that falls under him, is open (Open Question 45). The two are never gone together (from her numbers: 265 steps between the two sides' slams, and a tile is away for 120). The shake is the latest good moment to go for the cart on her side.
- **Phase 3, "Sin frenos" (no brakes):** her first charge breaks through the wheel stop ahead of her (the other on her way back) and she runs wall to wall with no rest: slam, 0.5 s of spinning wheels, back. The broken stops stay broken after a lost life in phase 3 (drawing only; her stops are the phase's numbers). The floor pockets are gone: on the floor only a held jump, a jump that becomes a glide, clears her (a target: at least 8 steps of press window at 150 px/s, or her speed comes down). The home tile on her side still falls at each slam. The cart must be boarded before she reaches the wall.
- **How each phase tests World 3's elements:** every hit is a cart and a jump out; phase 1 adds the crumbling perch and the glide home, phase 2 takes the ledge under his feet and makes him lead a moving target, phase 3 makes the glide the only safe crossing of her lane. (The first sketch had carts rolling on their own in phase 3 and the glide as the way back up; a timetabled cart against a fixed cycle always hits or always misses, so here boarding aims the cart and the glide is the way back across. Open Question 40.)
- **After a hit:** she is hurt for 1.0 s (60 steps), still, flashing white 4 steps on and 4 off, harmless and unhittable; one of her three lamps goes out. Then the next phase starts where she stands, armoured, with its telegraph.
- **A life lost mid-fight (GDD-BOS-01):** she keeps her hits and starts her phase again at its right stop, armoured, telegraphing (a life lost while she is hurt starts the next phase); both carts stand at their starts; every perch and home tile is whole; Poncho respawns on home, above her lane. Out of lives, the run goes back to 3-1 with seven (GDD-LIFE-05), and the fight starts afresh.
- **Safe spots and their risks:** home (in phase 1 all of it, later its standing tile): nothing reaches him, nothing is won. The floor pockets in phases 1 and 2: safe, but she parks beside them. A perch: 0.5 s a tile. The floor: hers. The fight can be played without touching the floor: home, cart, perch, glide, home, three times (a target, for a bot to show). No step is blind or needs a single-step press.
- **Her look (Proposed; to be designed with the user, as La Estrella was):** silhouette first: a squat saddle-tank engine, twice as long as tall, a fat funnel stack in front, a round smokebox door, a tiny open cab, four small wheels, a toothed buffer beam. Black iron, not rust: iron dark with gallery deep shade and steel glints, brass in lamp amber and ochre light, the plate "14" in lamp amber (World 3's colours, GDD-ART-06), so the rust cart reads against her as it falls and she reads against the shed's pale stone wall. Her firebox, her one round headlamp, her eye, and the three brass lamps along her tank that count her hits (three, two, one, none, with the boss bar's pips) burn with **the cold flame** ("llama fría", a pale blue-green), which would be a 25th colour of World 3 used by her alone (Open Question 40; its value a target, at least ΔE 12 from mist white, steel, the sky bands and Poncho's blue). Smoke, sparks and the armour's white plume outside her box are harmless. Grumpy and proud, never frightening (GDD-VIS-04). Defeated, she is an ordinary old engine with a lamp-amber light.
- **Her defeat, the third hit:** the cold flame goes out; her plate "14" pops off and tumbles to the floor (Don Zopilote's crown rule and numbers, GDD-BOS-02); she sags with a long sigh of white steam. Both carts and anything falling are gone at once. 1.0 s (60 steps) after the hit the level clear screen comes up.
- **Hit feedback (GDD-BOS-01):** a hit-stop of 4 steps on a hit that leaves her hurt, 12 on the last; a cart rolling on the other rail is held still through it. Shake: none on a hit; 1 px for 6 steps at each slam in phases 2 and 3; 2 px for 10 steps when she breaks a wheel stop; 3 px for 16 steps when her plate first touches the floor.
- **Sounds (GDD-AUD-02, in words):** the telegraph, a two-note steam whistle; the charge, a chuffing faster each phase; the stop, a brake squeal (phase 1) or a squeal and an iron clang (2 and 3); the armour, a steady hiss; the derrumbe, a timber creak; the cart, GDD-WLD-15's rumble; the hoist, a chain rattle; a scoring cart, wood crashing on iron and her whistle cracking; an armoured or ridden-off cart, a flat clang; a miss, a dull splintering; her defeat, a long falling whistle, a sigh and one bell.
- **Boss bar:** "La Catorce", the same in every language, three pips (GDD-UI-01). Its medal is gold for at most 3 lives lost in 3-4, else silver (GDD-PRG-03).
- **Played through (targets):** a bot is limited by the armour to her first slam of each phase: about 780 steps (13 s). A first clean fight, one leg watched and about one missed cart in each phase, and the glide home after each hit: 55 to 85 s (an estimate, to check in play). A first-time player is expected to lose 2 to 4 lives: one riding off the end onto her, one or two to her charge on the floor in phase 3, one dropping out short of the perch.
- **The build measures first:** the cart's fall (16 steps and 39 px, so the drop spots); boarding to her roof (48 steps) and the three windows; the jump from home to the cart and its 26 steps; the plain jump-out window onto the perch and the glided one from the cart's start; both spring launches; the held jump over her at 150 px/s; a glide from the perch against a standing cart; the bot's floor-free fight.
- `CATORCE` (to be): `width` 64, `height` 32, `stops` [[80, 400], [80, 400], [32, 448]], `speed` [80, 110, 150], `restSteps` [180, 60, 0], `telegraphSteps` 30, `shakesHome` [false, true, true], `armouredUntilSlam` true, `hurtSteps` 60, `defeatSteps` 60, `hits` 3, `lamps` [3, 2, 1, 0], `flashSteps` 4; the arena's cart `returnSteps` 180 (the levels' 120), `hoistSteps` 30.
- **Not picked, El Gran Murciélago:** a giant bat hanging from the roof of a cavern whose floor is a chasm with a few crumbling ledges. He swoops after a telegraph; he is hit by a stomp from a glide while he hangs low after a swoop. He tests the glide and the ledges, and no cart. A second flying boss after Don Zopilote (GDD-BOS-02).
- **Not picked, El Cimarrón:** a giant bighorn ram on the peaks who charges along ledges and knocks the crumbling ones out. He is hit by making him run into a cart Poncho sends down a rail at him. He tests carts and ledges; close to the Tlacuache's charge and to Don Zopilote's rocks.

## 10. Progression and meta

### GDD-PRG-01 Unlocks

Status: Proposed (the glide unlocked in every level once 2-4 is cleared is Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Glide everywhere")

- Clearing worlds unlocks permanent **abilities** in order (these are not the temporary power-ups of section 6): Glide (GDD-MOV-07, after World 2), Wall cling (GDD-MOV-08, after World 3). Further powers are open (GDD-MVP-02). Until the unlocks are built, a level turns an ability on itself: the sandbox has the glide and the wall cling on.
- **The glide's unlock (Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Glide everywhere"; not built):** clearing 2-4 unlocks the glide for good, in every level, replays of World 1 and World 2 included. It is built with World 3's first level (GDD-WLD-16), whose opening teaches it. Until then clearing 2-4 unlocks nothing, and the glide and the wall cling are off in every World 1 and World 2 level (GDD-MVP-02). What the glide does to the routes, secrets and bots of the levels built without it was measured by the World 3 design swarm's audit (2026-11-10: scratch probes and the repository's tests run with the glide forced on, nothing committed): no level becomes impossible, every height gate holds and no boss rule breaks; what it makes optional, and what is still open, is in Open Question 27. World 3's levels are laid out for the glide and are never played without it: until the unlock is built each turns the glide on itself, and `?level=3-1` to `?level=3-4` open them with it (Proposed).
- **The wall cling's unlock (Proposed):** clearing World 3's boss arena 3-4 (GDD-BOS-04) unlocks it, built with World 4; it is off in every World 3 level.
- Hilos buy cosmetics for the poncho (patterns and colours). Cosmetics never change gameplay.

### GDD-PRG-02 Save system

Status: Proposed

- Autosave to browser `localStorage` after each level and each checkpoint. Versioned schema with migration. Built: at the level clear (see "Progress", "World 1's run" and "World 2's run"), at each checkpoint (see "Saved run") and when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (see "Progress", "World 1's run" and "World 2's run").
- Saves: world progress, hilos total, unlocked powers, cosmetics, settings, assist flags.
- Save never blocks the main thread and survives storage being unavailable (play without saving).
- **Built (details Proposed):** the settings and whether the tutorial has been started, as `{ "version": 1, "settings": { "master": 0-10, "effects": 0-10, "language": "en", "es" or "de", "buttonOpacity": "low", "medium" or "high", "layout": "standard" or "mirrored", "shake": "on" or "off" }, "progress": { "tutorialPlayed": true or false, "worldLives": { "1": 0 to 7, "2": 0 to 7 }, "worldAt": { "1": "1-2", "2": "2-1" } } }` (`worldLives` and `worldAt`: see "World 1's run" and "World 2's run") under the key `poncho-siete-vidas` in local storage, written at each change of either and read when the game opens (GDD-UI-02). A save without `progress`, as the settings-only saves before it, or with anything but `true` there, reads as not played, and one without `language` takes the browser's (GDD-UI-02), so the version stays 1. Saving one part keeps the other. Where the browser refuses storage, the tutorial counts as played for the rest of the visit only. `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **Progress (built; user, 2026-10-31: each hilo once, the best medal, the best time and "cleared" per level, saved at the level clear; its hilos also when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up, user, 2026-11-02: "Keep them"; details Proposed):** the save gets `"levels": { "0-1": { "hilos": "0110…", "medal": "bronze", "silver" or "gold", "bestSteps": 8040, "cleared": true }, … }`, one entry per real level (0-1, 1-1, 1-2, 1-3 and the boss arena 1-4, whose `hilos` text is empty since it has no hilos, and World 2's 2-1, 2-2, 2-3 and its arena 2-4, empty too; the test levels, the sandbox, the slopes level, `lab-2m`, `lab-2e` and `lab-3c`, are never saved; `src/levels/levels.test.ts`). `hilos` is one character per hilo of the level, in the level's order, `1` for collected. `bestSteps` is the fastest clear time in simulation steps (GDD-UI-02, "Clear time"). It is written on the step Poncho reaches the exit (GDD-WLD-03), or in a boss level on the step its level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01), and its hilos also on the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (below); a run that quits or retries keeps nothing of its own. At the clear every hilo collected in the run is added to the saved ones (never removed), the medal is kept if it is better than the saved one (gold over silver over bronze), the time if it is shorter, and `cleared` becomes true; nothing else of the level's entry changes. On the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (GDD-LIFE-05), in every real level (user, 2026-11-02: "Keep them": "Saved for good when the Siete vidas gastadas screen comes up, in every level; that's what GDD-LIFE-05 says."; this replaces the rule of 2026-10-31 that only a clear saved a run's hilos), the hilos collected in the run are added to the saved ones in the same way, and nothing else of the level changes: the medal, the time and `cleared` stay as saved, and a level with nothing saved gets an entry with no medal, no time and `cleared` false. So the hilos stay when the restart plays another level (GDD-LIFE-05, Decided: "Hilos collected stay"). `recordHilos` in `src/sim/save.ts`. A save without `levels`, as the saves before it, or with an entry that is not readable, reads as nothing saved for that level: a `hilos` text whose length is not the level's hilo count, or with another character than `0` and `1`, reads as no hilo; a medal other than the three names as none; a `bestSteps` that is not a whole number above 0 as none; and `cleared` only as true when it is exactly true. So the version stays 1. `cleared` decides what "Start" plays only for a save that keeps no run for the world played (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"), and whether 1-4 is cleared decides whether "Start" plays World 2 (GDD-UI-02, "Which level, with two worlds"); the world map will read it too. Where the browser refuses storage, the progress counts for the rest of the visit only, like the tutorial flag. `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **World 1's run (built; user, 2026-11-02: "Save the lives": "The save keeps the World 1 lives too: Start plays the first level not cleared with the lives you had, and after a game over the world restarts at 1-1 with 7, however you get there."; "Follow the run": "The save remembers where your World 1 run is: Start plays the next level in order of the current run (1-1 after a game over, then 1-2, ...). Clears still count for medals.", which replaced "first level not cleared" for "Start"; details Proposed):** `progress` gets `"worldLives": { "1": 4 }` and `"worldAt": { "1": "1-2" }`, each per world by its number: `worldLives` the lives the save keeps for the world's run (GDD-LIFE-05), a whole number from 0 to 7, and `worldAt` the level its run has reached, the id of one of the world's real levels. The worlds with a real level have their runs kept, World 1's as below and World 2's the same way (see "World 2's run"); a test level adds no world (`SAVED_WORLDS` in `src/levels/index.ts`). Both change on the same two steps, and on no other. On the step Poncho reaches the exit of a World 1 level (GDD-WLD-03), or the level clear screen of the boss arena 1-4 comes up (GDD-BOS-01), `worldLives` becomes the lives he has there, the lives "Next level" carries (GDD-UI-02), and `worldAt` the next level of the world in the game's order (1-2 after 1-1, 1-3 after 1-2, 1-4 after 1-3); after the world's last level, the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02), it is the world's first level, 1-1: the run is over and a new one starts (Proposed), though "Start" now plays World 2's run instead (GDD-UI-02, "Which level, with two worlds"). On the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up in a World 1 level, `worldLives` becomes 0 and `worldAt` 1-1: the world restarts at 1-1 with seven however that is reached (GDD-LIFE-05). A life lost or won, a checkpoint, quitting, "Retry" and closing the game leave both as they were, so quitting a level and picking "Start" plays it again from its start with the lives Poncho had at the last clear (Open Question 18). The tutorial, outside the seven worlds, and the test levels write neither. "Start" reads them (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"). A clear still counts for the medal, the best time and the hilos, and `cleared` is still saved (see "Progress"), but it no longer decides what "Start" plays while the run is kept. A save without `worldLives`, as the saves before it, or with an entry that is not a whole number from 0 to 7, keeps no lives for that world, which reads as seven; a save without `worldAt`, or with an entry that names no real level of that world (a test level, the tutorial, another world's level, anything that is no level id), keeps no run for it, and "Start" goes by the levels cleared (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"); the version stays 1. Where the browser refuses storage both last for the rest of the visit, like the progress. `worldLivesAfter` and `worldAtAfter` in `src/levels/index.ts`, `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **World 2's run (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-04: "Latest world"; checked by `src/levels/levels.test.ts` and `e2e/world2.spec.ts`):** World 2's run is kept as World 1's is, under the world's number: `"worldLives": { "1": 4, "2": 6 }` and `"worldAt": { "1": "1-1", "2": "2-3" }`, `worldAt["2"]` the id of one of World 2's real levels (2-1, 2-2, 2-3 or the arena 2-4). They change on the same two steps as World 1's: at the exit of a World 2 level, or as 2-4's level clear screen comes up, the lives there and the next level of World 2 (2-2 after 2-1, 2-3 after 2-2, 2-4 after 2-3, and 2-1 after 2-4, a new run); as the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up in a World 2 level, 0 lives and 2-1. 1-4's clear writes World 1's run as before (its lives, and 1-1) and nothing of World 2's: "Start" goes to World 2 because 1-4 is held as cleared (see "Progress"), and without a World 2 run kept it goes by World 2's levels cleared, as for World 1 (2-1 always with seven), so a save from before World 2 that cleared 1-4 goes to 2-1 with seven (GDD-UI-02, "Which level, with two worlds"). World 2's levels get entries in `levels` and can hold the saved run like any real level. A save without World 2's keys, or with a value that names no real World 2 level, keeps no World 2 run, so the version stays 1.
- **Saved run (built; user, 2026-10-31: resumed by a "Continue" row; it keeps the lives, the run's hilos, the clock and the used ofrendas; dropped at the level clear and by the Siete vidas gastadas screen, not by quitting or by starting another level; details Proposed):** on the step the checkpoint moves to a later ofrenda (GDD-LIFE-02, GDD-LIFE-03) in a real level (0-1, 1-1, 1-2 or 1-3, and World 2's 2-1, 2-2 or 2-3, never a test level; the boss arenas 1-4 and 2-4 have no ofrenda, so no run is ever saved there, GDD-BOS-01), the save gets `"run": { "level": "0-1", "checkpoint": 1, "lives": 5, "livesIn": 7, "used": ["ofrenda:…", …], "hilos": "0110…", "clock": 4210 }`: the level, the position of that ofrenda in the level's order, the lives after its touch, the lives the run started with (GDD-PRG-03 counts the lives lost from them), every ofrenda and veladora used so far in the run (each restores a life once, so a resumed run gives none again), the hilos collected in the run (one character per hilo, as in "Progress", the saved ones included) and the clock (GDD-UI-02, "Clear time"). There is one saved run at most: a checkpoint reached in any run, of any level, replaces it. It is dropped on the step Poncho reaches the exit or a boss level's clear screen comes up (where "Progress" is written) and on the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (GDD-LIFE-05), in a real level only: a test level never drops it; quitting to the title, "Retry", starting another level and closing the game keep it. Picking "Continue" (GDD-UI-02) resumes it: the level starts (at once, as the next level does, when it is not the one behind the title), with the lives, the used ofrendas and veladora, the clock and the run's hilos as saved (added to the hilos saved for good, which stay faded, GDD-WLD-04), and Poncho comes in at that ofrenda by the respawn flip of a restart (GDD-LIFE-05: it cannot be skipped, the meow plays), in Base with no keys, and everything else at its start, as after a lost life. In 0-1 the tutorial tips show again for the stretch Poncho is in, as on a fresh start (user, 2026-11-01: "show it again"; GDD-UI-05). A saved run that does not read (an unknown level, a checkpoint that is not one of its ofrendas, lives that are not a whole number from 1 to 7, a `hilos` text of the wrong length, a clock that is not a whole number of 0 or more) reads as none; `used` keeps only texts. A `livesIn` that is not a whole number from 1 to 7, as in a run saved before it (which started with seven), reads as 7. Where the browser refuses storage it lasts for the rest of the visit, like the rest of the progress. `src/sim/save.ts`.

### GDD-PRG-03 Score and medals

Status: Proposed. Medals are kept (user, 2026-10-30); the score is optional and can be cut.

Score from hilos, enemies and time. Level medals: Bronze (clear), Silver (all hilos), Gold (all hilos and at most 3 lives lost in the level; user, 2026-11-02: "Lives lost in the level": "Gold if you lose at most 3 lives in that level, whatever you came in with."). Última Vida doubles hilo score (GDD-LIFE-04).

- **Medals (built; kept, user, 2026-10-30, details Proposed):** the medal is shown on the level clear screen (GDD-UI-02). "All hilos" counts every hilo of the level collected, those taken before a restart after the last life included, since they are kept (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-LIFE-05); a level without hilos has them all. "Lives lost in the level" is the lives the run started with (those carried into the level, GDD-LIFE-05, or seven) less the triangles still whole as Poncho reaches the exit, or in a boss level as its level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01), so a life restored by an ofrenda or the veladora counts against one lost (GDD-LIFE-03; Open Question 18), and a level started with seven gives gold for 4 or more lives at the exit, as before. After the restart that follows the last life the count starts again from its seven, as the clock does (GDD-UI-02, "Clear time"), and a run resumed by "Continue" counts from the lives it started with (GDD-PRG-02, "Saved run"). The boss arena 1-4 has no hilos (GDD-BOS-01), so it has them all: its medal is Gold for at most 3 lives lost in it, else Silver, never Bronze (entered with seven, Gold with 4 lives or more left at its clear; entered with five, with 2 or more; measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`), and so is La Estrella's arena 2-4's (`src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). 3 is `MEDAL.goldLivesLost` in `src/config/tuning.ts` (measured by `src/sim/run.test.ts` and `src/sim/results.test.ts`). Since progress is saved (GDD-PRG-02), every hilo saved from an earlier run counts too, so Silver and Gold can be built up over runs; the best medal is kept per level. `src/sim/results.ts`.
- **Not built yet:** the score, and Última Vida's double hilo score (Open Question 10).

## 11. UI and accessibility

### GDD-UI-01 HUD

Status: Proposed

- Top-left: seven triangles (GDD-LIFE-01), filled = life, outline = lost. Lives fill from the left, so a lost life empties the rightmost filled triangle. Filled and outline differ by shape, not colour alone (GDD-UI-03). Next to them, a small icon of the current power-up state (GDD-PWR-01). After it, one small key per key carried (GDD-WLD-05).
- Top-right: hilo counter. On touch the pause button takes the corner (GDD-UI-02), so the counter goes left of it.
- **Hilo counter (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-23: collected/total):** a small skein (the hilo pickup's look, in yellow) and then the hilos collected and the level's total, as "12/20", in 8 px pale monospace text. Its right edge is 8 px from the screen's right edge, as the lives are 8 px from the left, and its top is level with the lives. On a touch device it ends 6 px left of the pause button instead, wherever that button has grown or moved to (GDD-CTL-03, safe-area insets). In the boss arenas 1-4 and 2-4, which have no hilos, it reads "0/0" (Open Question 21). `src/ui/hiloCounter.ts`.
- **Boss bar (built in a boss level, details Proposed):** at the top middle of the screen, the boss's name ("Don Zopilote" in 1-4, "La Estrella" in 2-4, GDD-BOS-03; the same in every language, GDD-UI-02) in 8 px pale text (`#fff3dc`) with a 2 px dark outline (`#2a1f2e`, `BOSS_BAR.nameStroke` and `nameStrokePx`), so it reads on any sky, World 2's pale one and La Estrella's cable included (user, 2026-11-04: "Dark outline", Open Question 34; checked by `e2e/boss.spec.ts` and `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`), its middle 10 px from the top, and under it three pips 6 px square and 4 px apart, their top 18 px from the top, centred together on the screen's middle (x = 227 to 253): one filled (red `#b94842` with a 1 px pale edge) for each hit the boss has left, from the left, and a grey outline (`#9ba3c4`, 1 px) for each hit taken, so they differ by shape, not colour alone (GDD-UI-03). It is there for the whole level, the three outlines of a defeated boss included, and drawn with the rest of the HUD: above the pause, level clear and *Siete vidas gastadas* screens, under the title screen's veil. Nothing else is drawn there: the lives are top left, the hilo counter and the pause button top right. A level without a boss has none. Each boss has its name and its pips in one place (`bossBar` in `src/ui/bossBar.ts`, checked by `src/ui/bossBar.test.ts`); La Estrella can glide behind the bar, which is drawn on top of her (GDD-BOS-03). `BOSS_BAR` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/bossBar.ts`, `src/ui/bossPose.ts`.
- Minimal, semi-transparent, hidden during cutscenes. On touch, the touch controls keep clear of the safe-area insets (built, GDD-CTL-03). The HUD should keep clear of them too (user, 2026-11-02: "Later, own PR"); until then the lives, the hilo counter and the boss bar keep their places, the counter moving only with the pause button, so on a screen of about 16:9 with a cutout on the left the first triangles lie under it. On a landscape iPhone nothing of the HUD is covered: the side notches lie in the letterbox bars and there is no top inset.

### GDD-UI-02 Menus and flow

Status: Proposed

Boot, title, world map, level, pause, results, settings. Fully operable by touch, keyboard and gamepad. Pause happens automatically if the tab loses focus or the device rotates to portrait.

- **Pause in a level (built; the button top-right, auto-pause on focus and portrait and resume with the pause key or a tap decided by the user 2026-10-22, at first resume-only, a menu since 2026-10-23, details Proposed):** Escape or P (GDD-CTL-01), or the touch pause button, pauses. The pause button sits in the top-right corner, 6 px from both edges (of the safe area, where an inset reaches into the picture, GDD-CTL-03), 24 px, growing like the other buttons so it is never under 48 CSS px (`TOUCH.pauseSize` in `src/config/tuning.ts`); it shows only on touch devices, drawn as two upright bars. The level stands still under a dim layer with "Paused" and the pause menu below it. The HUD and the touch buttons stay on top.
- **Resume:** Escape or P again (each press counts once, on any keyboard layout), the pause button, a tap or click anywhere but on a menu row, or picking "Resume" from the pause menu. The game keys (jump, claw, Fuego, moving) do not resume by themselves, though Space and Z pick the chosen menu item and Up, W, Down and S choose one; on touch any tap off the menu rows does, on a game button too, but that tap is no game press. A button pressed while paused (the Jump button under the tap that resumed, a key pressed while the game stood still, a Left or Right key or button held through the resume, a finger on the slide stick, GDD-CTL-03) does nothing until it is released and pressed again, also on the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen (GDD-LIFE-05), wherever the finger is (the Resume row reaches into the stick's lower quarter, left or, mirrored, right); a key held at the pause and pressed again while paused is such a press too, and a quick tap of Left or Right while paused counts for nothing after the resume; a button held since before the pause stays held, so a run or a jump carries on.
- **Pause menu (built; a two-item menu decided by the user 2026-10-23, a third item, "Settings", 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** under "Paused", three items, "Resume", "Settings" and "Quit to title", in 12 px text, centred, one under the other. Each item is a row 160 px wide and 28 px tall, the first one's top 136 px from the screen's top, each next one directly below; on a small screen the rows grow so none is under 48 CSS px tall (`PAUSE_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The chosen item is pale with a ">" in front of it, the others grey. The menu opens with "Resume" chosen, every time the game pauses. On a keyboard Up or W chooses the item above and Down or S the item below (no wrap-around); Enter, Space or Z picks the chosen item. Each press counts once, on any keyboard layout, and the presses of one frame count in order, so Down, Down and then Enter in one frame quits (they are read from the browser's own key events, like Escape and P). A tap or click on an item's row picks that item at once, whichever is chosen. Picking "Settings" opens the settings screen; leaving it comes back to the pause menu with "Settings" chosen. There is no confirmation for quitting (user, 2026-10-23). While the phone is upright the menu is hidden and nothing on it can be chosen or picked; turned sideways again, the item chosen before is still chosen (the menu did not open again).
- **Quit to title:** picking "Quit to title" brings back the title screen, waiting for a press as when the game opens. The level behind it is a fresh run of the level "Start" plays (see "Which level"), as when the game opens (user, 2026-10-23): the lives "Start" gives it (seven, or the lives World 1 keeps, or the lives World 2 keeps, see "Which level" and "Which level, with two worlds"; a triangle the run quit had filled and the fresh one has not shows no crack, and a last life pulses from the level's start, GDD-LIFE-04), every ofrenda unused and the respawn point at the level start, every hilo not saved yet back (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-PRG-02), Poncho in Base with no keys, and every piñata, door, fragile wall, taco, enemy, crumbling tile, moving platform and moving block back at its start. Poncho stands at the level start with no respawn flip and no invulnerability, and no sound plays. The press that quit does not start the level: the button that picked counts as already held on the title screen, and an Enter press, tap or click in the same frame does not count there.
- **Automatic pause:** the tab or window losing focus or being hidden pauses; it never resumes by itself. A touch device whose screen is upright pauses (the screen's orientation decides, or the window's shape where the browser has none), and the pause menu gives way to "Turn your device sideways", in 12 px grey text, larger than the 8 px prompts since the whole level is drawn small; no key, button or tap resumes until it is sideways again, and then it stays paused until the player resumes. A desktop window made taller than wide does not pause, nor does a touchscreen laptop's, whose screen stays landscape. `src/sim/pause.ts`.
- **Title screen (built; the level behind a veil with the placeholder Poncho decided by the user 2026-10-23, the prompt replaced by the title menu 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** the game opens on it. The level "Start" plays (see "Which level" below) is drawn standing still (no simulation step runs) under a dark veil like the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen's (GDD-LIFE-05), which covers the HUD too; the touch buttons are hidden. Over the veil, centred: "PONCHO" in large pale text (32 px), "Siete Vidas" under it (16 px), Poncho's standing frame (GDD-ART-04) at his size in the level, standing left of the two lines with his feet level with the bottom of "Siete Vidas" (104 px from the screen's top) and 8 px between him and the wider line's left edge (user, 2026-10-31: beside the title, so four rows fit; it stood above the menu before), and under them the title menu (user, 2026-10-25; the third row, 2026-10-28; "Continue", 2026-10-31): "Continue" while a saved run exists (GDD-PRG-02, "Saved run"), then "Start", "Tutorial" and "Settings", drawn like the pause menu (12 px, 160 x 28 px rows growing to 48 CSS px, the first one's top 112 px from the screen's top, so four fit on a landscape phone, where they grow to 34 px; `TITLE_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`), with "Continue" chosen whenever the title screen appears with it, else "Start". "PONCHO" and "Siete Vidas" have their middles 68 and 96 px from the screen's top (Proposed).
- **Starting (the title menu, with the other buttons still starting, decided by the user 2026-10-25):** Up or W chooses the row above and Down or S the row below (no wrap-around); Enter, Space or Z picks the chosen item, and a tap or click on a row picks that row. Picking "Start" starts the level, picking "Tutorial" starts 0-1 (see "Which level" below) and picking "Continue" resumes the saved run (GDD-PRG-02); none of them drops the saved run. A tap or click anywhere off the rows (on the hidden buttons' places and the pause button's too) and a new press of claw, Fuego, Left or Right (GDD-CTL-01) start the level as "Start" does, whichever row is chosen. Picking "Settings" opens the settings screen; leaving it comes back to the title with "Settings" chosen. The jump keys start the level only by picking: Space and Z pick, Up and W choose. The touch buttons are hidden and press nothing on the title screen, nor on the settings screen opened from it: a finger on their places, the slide stick's lower quarter included (left, or right when mirrored), is only a tap (a finger on the hidden mode switch does not change the mode, GDD-CTL-03). Only a game button that was up on the frame before counts, so pressing a second button while holding one does; a tap shorter than one frame counts (the game buttons are latched, L-006; Enter, Space, Z and the menu keys are read from the browser's own key events like Escape and P). Escape and P do nothing on the title screen, and losing focus there does not pause the level behind it. The button that started the level counts as already held, as after a resume: a jump held from the title screen does not make Poncho jump until it is released and pressed again, and a Left or Right key, button or slide-stick finger down when the level starts does not move him until it is released. `src/sim/title.ts`.
- **Upright on the title screen:** a touch device whose screen is upright (the same test as the automatic pause) hides the title menu and shows "Turn your device sideways" in its place, in grey 12 px text, also when the game opens that way; nothing on the menu can be chosen or picked until it is sideways again (the presses of a frame count in order, like the pause menu's, so a pick of "Settings" just before a turn upright opens the settings screen, hidden until the phone is sideways), and the level starts only in a frame that ends sideways (so a tap and a turn upright together do not start it); sideways again, the title menu is back with the item chosen before still chosen. So the level never starts upright.
- **Settings screen (built; volume rows, Left and Right to change, "<" and ">" on touch, saved in the browser, decided by the user 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** opened from the pause menu or the title menu, it replaces that menu: the same dark layer, "Settings" in 16 px pale text, centred, its middle 44 px from the screen's top, and under it seven rows, "Master volume", "Effects volume", "Language", "Button opacity", "Layout", "Screen shake" and "Back", each 260 px wide and 28 px tall, centred, the first one's top 62 px from the screen's top, each next one directly below, growing like the pause menu's rows so none is under 48 CSS px tall (`SETTINGS_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`; on a landscape phone they grow to 34 px, so only six of the seven fit, ending 266 px from the top, and the list scrolls: see "Scrolling" below). A volume row shows its name in 12 px text from 20 px inside the row's left edge, a "<" 8 px inside its left edge, a ">" 8 px inside its right edge, and its value as 10 small upright bars, 5 px wide and 10 px tall with 2 px between them, centred in the row's height, the last one ending 20 px inside the row's right edge: as many filled as the value, the rest outlined. The chosen row is pale and the others grey; "Back", when chosen, has a ">" in front of it like the pause menu. The screen opens with "Master volume" chosen, every time. On a keyboard Up or W chooses the row above and Down or S the row below (no wrap-around); Left or A lowers the chosen volume by 1 and Right or D raises it by 1, from 0 to 10 (no wrap-around; the default is 10, `VOLUME` in `src/config/tuning.ts`); Enter, Space or Z on "Back", Escape, P or the touch pause button leave the screen, back to the menu it was opened from; Enter, Space or Z on a volume row does nothing. Each press counts once, read like the pause menu's keys, in order within a frame. On touch, a tap or click on a volume row chooses it and, on the row's left half, lowers it by 1, on its right half raises it by 1; a tap on "Back" leaves; a tap anywhere else does nothing (it does not resume). A change that moves a volume plays the jump sound at the new volume, so the player hears it; at 0, or at 10 when raised, nothing changes and nothing plays. Every sound effect plays at Master times Effects, each as a share of 10 (GDD-AUD-01, GDD-AUD-02). Opened from the pause menu, it lies under the HUD and the touch buttons like the pause menu, and a button or finger pressed on it is held back after the resume like any press made while paused; opened from the title, it covers everything like the title screen. A phone turned upright hides it like the pause menu, showing "Turn your device sideways"; turned sideways again, the same row is still chosen. Losing focus changes nothing on it. `src/sim/settings.ts`, `src/ui/settingsMenu.ts`.
- **Language (built; a Language row with English, Español and Deutsch, user, 2026-10-25; every text on screen switches and a first visit takes the browser's language, user, 2026-10-29; details and the Spanish and German texts Proposed):** the "Language" row is drawn like a volume row (its name from 20 px inside its left edge, "<" and ">"), with the chosen language's own name ("English", "Español" or "Deutsch", 12 px, in the row's colour) centred where a volume row's bars are, so it reads the same in every language. The languages go in that order. Left or A, or a tap on the row's left half, chooses the language before; Right or D, or a tap on its right half, the one after; at either end nothing changes (no wrap-around), and Enter, Space or Z on it does nothing, like the volume rows. A change plays no sound, is saved at once (see "Saving the settings") and from that frame every text below is drawn in the new language, the settings screen's own included, so its title and rows change under the player's eyes while the same row stays chosen. The names "PONCHO", "Siete Vidas", "Siete vidas gastadas", "Hilos", "Don Zopilote" (GDD-BOS-02) and the language names are the same in every language, and so is "La Estrella" (GDD-BOS-03), in the table below, and so are the placeholder and dev lines of the test levels (GDD-UI-05). The texts (12 px rows and prompts, 16 px titles and 8 px prompts as in English):
