### GDD-ART-04 Poncho animation set

Status: Proposed (the look of the animations, the taco, the chile and the claw swipe Decided, user: designed from his references and approved on an animated design page, 2026-11-05 to 2026-11-08, "Make it punchier" for the swipe, "More expressive", "Fire spit bigger", "Eat with crumbs", the victory pose left out at first, "Let's skip the victory pose for now. And just do the others.", then approved in the game, "Approved"; the victory pose Decided, user, 2026-11-08: the user's strip only recoloured, "Just take thie sprite for the victory and adapt it minimal to fit our colour palett", "Ok I approve!", shown above the clear screen's veil, "Above veil, in place", always with the taco, "Always the taco"; when each frame shows is Proposed)

The set planned: idle (blink, glances at the taco in the Taco and Fuego states), run, skid, jump up, apex, fall, land, claw swipe, fireball spit (Fuego), hurt (the sombrero tips off; a hit in Taco or Fuego costs the power, GDD-PWR-01), respawn flip, eating a taco or chile, victory, wall cling (post-MVP), glide (post-MVP). What is built is below.

The run cycle is the user's run drawing (`sprite-run.jpg`, GDD-ART-02), side-on, the tail swinging behind; the four-frame walks of the sprite references (`sprite-walk-a.jpg`, `sprite-walk-b.jpg`) stay the guide for a walk.

- **Built: the standing frame and the run cycle** (user, 2026-10-25), made by `scripts/art/make-sprites.mjs` from the user's drawing and run cycle (`sprite-pixel.jpg`, `sprite-run.jpg`; GDD-ART-02): frames 0 to 4 of `assets/sprites/poncho.png` (160 x 160 art pixels until 0.10.2, now placed unchanged in the 192 x 192 frame). The four run frames are the user's run legs under the standing drawing's body, with the tail's tip swaying.
- **Built: the animation set** (user, 2026-11-05: "Create animations for ponch, the taco and, Chile and the claw swipe aswell, Nintendo art and style."), in `assets/sprites/poncho.png` (rows of 8 frames of 192 x 192 art px, 48 x 48 game px, so the sheet stays under a phone's 4096 px texture limit), made by `scripts/art/make-sprites.mjs` with Poncho's pose rig (`scripts/art/poncho/rig.mjs`): the user's drawing cut into its parts (sombrero, face, ears, poncho, legs, tail) and put back together in new poses, moved, squashed and stretched by whole rows and columns, never repainted; only his expressions, his front paw (as the walk references show it) and new leg poses are drawn new, in the drawing's colours (GDD-ART-02). The standing frame and the four run frames are unchanged, pixel for pixel (checked by `scripts/art/poncho/rig.test.mjs`). The frames: idle, blink, glance (at the taco), jump0 (the take-off stretch), jump1 (rising, legs tucked), apex, fall0, fall1 (falling fast, the sombrero lifting off his head), land (the squash), skid, flip0 to flip7 (tucked into a ball), clawWind, clawSwipe, clawFollow, hurt0 and hurt1 (knocked back, the sombrero tipping off his head), eat0 (the reach for the bite) and eat1 (the chomp), spit0 (cheeks puffed) and spit1 (the blast); then the victory frames (below).
- **Built: the victory pose** (user, 2026-11-08: "Victory pose take the image as reference and the sprite.", then "Just take thie sprite for the victory and adapt it minimal to fit our colour palett"; approved, "Ok I approve!"): the four frames of the user's strip (`assets/characters/poncho/victory/strip.jpg`), read cell by cell on its 4 px grid (`scripts/art/poncho/victory-pixels.mjs`), one of its pixels a drawing px (2 x 2 art px), only mapped to his 16 colours (GDD-ART-03), nothing re-posed: victory0 (ready, both fists up, no taco), victory1, victory2 and victory3 (the taco raised higher in each, in his right paw). Front view, facing the player, the PB badge on the viewer's right as in the front references; he holds this taco in every power state, Base included ("Always the taco"), and no held item is drawn with these frames. They follow the strip's outline, 2 drawing px thick in places (GDD-ART-05 asks for 1; left as drawn, as the user asked only for the colours to change). `scripts/art/poncho/victory.mjs`, last in `assets/sprites/poncho.png`.
- **The taco, the chile and the claw swipe (built, `scripts/art/poncho/items.mjs`, `assets/sprites/poncho/`):** the taco pickup, a golden hard shell with lettuce, tomato and meat as the illustration's (`reference.jpg`), floats in a four-frame bob, one frame every 8 steps of the run's clock, and glints for the last 8 steps of every 120; the chile pickup, glossy red with a green stem and a ring of warm glow and heat wisps, does the same. A taco or chile that walks or falls (from a piñata) is lifted by its frames' reach below their 12 px square, so no bob frame dips below its feet: past that square each taco frame reaches at most 0.5 px on every side and each chile frame 0 behind, 0.5 in front, 1 above and 1 below (`PONCHO_ART.pickup.reach`, pinned by `scripts/art/poncho/items.test.mjs` and fitted over the real levels by `scripts/art/poncho/items.levels.test.mjs`, L-059). He holds the taco in his paw (Taco) and the taco with the chile beside it (Fuego) at each frame's hand point; eating shows the food at his mouth, then bitten, with crumbs and lettuce flying; the spit shows a flame burst at his mouth; the claw swipe is four smear frames of three pale crescent streaks with crimson edges, its frame laid on the claw's box (GDD-CMB-02). The HUD's power icon uses the same pictures (GDD-UI-01).
- **When each frame shows (Proposed; `PONCHO_ART` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/ponchoPose.ts`), first match wins:** on the level clear screen (GDD-UI-02), the victory, counted from the step the screen comes up as its first: victory0 for 12 steps, victory1 for 5, victory2 for 5, then victory3 and victory2 in turn for 24 steps each for as long as the screen is up, never mirrored and drawn whole, not blinking even when he reached the exit while blinking after a hit (GDD-LIFE-02) (`PONCHO_ART.victorySteps`, `victoryPumpSteps`); the respawn flip (the flip frame nearest the turn's angle, GDD-LIFE-02); hurt, on a hit that costs his power and not a life (hurt0 for 6 steps, then hurt1 for 18); the claw (clawWind in the wind-up, clawSwipe in the swing with swipe0 on its first step and swipe1 on the next two, then clawFollow for 10 steps with swipe2 for 3 and swipe3 for 3); the spit, when a fireball leaves him (spit0 for 2 steps, then spit1 for 8 with flame0 for 4 and flame1 for 4); eating, when his power goes up (eat0 for 10 steps with the food whole, then eat1 for 14 with the taco bitten and crumbs0 for 6 and crumbs1 for 6; a chile stays whole); in the air (jump0 for the 4 steps after a take-off, then by his vertical speed, down positive: jump1 under -120 px/s, apex under 60, fall0 under 250, else fall1); landing from a fall of 150 px/s or faster (land for 6 steps); a skid on the ground at 40 px/s or more against the way he faces; running (the run frames as before, `ANIM`); standing (blink for the last 6 of every 180 steps, and in Taco or Fuego glance at the taco for 40 of every 360). Facing left mirrors him and what he holds, but for the victory frames, front views, which never mirror; the claw and its swipe face the way the swing does.
- **Where (Proposed):** the frame's bottom row is level with his feet and its middle column on his body box's middle (the frame is 48 game px, `SPRITE_FRAME`). Each frame reaches past his body box at most 13 px behind, 14.5 in front and 11 above, and never below his feet but in the flip, drawn in the air; the victory frames, never mirrored, at most 10 px to the left (behind), 10 to the right (in front) and 10 above (`PONCHO_ART.reach`, per frame, pinned by `scripts/art/poncho.test.mjs`, L-059). Over the real levels (`scripts/art/poncho.levels.test.mjs`) the brim or the tail beside his body may lie over a wall's face or a slope's sand, as the enemies' tails may (user, 2026-11-05: "Allow beside the body"), and the sombrero above his body may lie over a ceiling tile, as in 1-3's two-tile passage or a ceiling bumped in a jump (user, 2026-11-08: "Allow it, pin it"); nothing ever lies below his feet. The glide bar and the wall-cling marks keep their placeholders (GDD-MOV-07, GDD-MOV-08).

### GDD-ART-05 Pixel rules

Status: Proposed

- **Outline:** every character, enemy, item and pickup has a near-black outline (#070509, as in the main reference, GDD-ART-02, which replaces the dark plum first proposed), 2 art pixels thick on the outside (one pixel of the user's drawing); Poncho's victory frames keep the user's strip's outline, 4 art pixels thick in places (GDD-ART-04). Tiles and scenery have no outline, so what can be touched stands out. World 2's touchable world objects, the awnings, the cordeles' flags and the rollos, carry it too (Proposed, GDD-ART-06), while their posts, poles and lines do not.
- **Light** comes from the upper left. Each material has three shades: base, a shadow on the lower right and a highlight on the upper left. No soft gradients, no blurred or blended edges, no anti-aliasing.
- **Dither** (a checker of two colours) only in skies and far scenery, never on characters or pickups.
- **Glow:** the chile pickup's ring of warm glow and its heat wisps sit outside its outline, as a glow does; nothing else draws outside its outline (Proposed, GDD-ART-04).
- **Three layers:** far scenery (sky, mountains, clouds; low contrast, scrolls slower than the level), the play area (tiles, mid contrast) and characters and pickups (highest contrast, outlined).
- **Hazards** have a pale edge or tip (cream on a spike's points, a scorpion's sting): this is how the high-contrast hazard outlines of GDD-UI-03 are drawn, so danger never rests on colour alone. Hazard tiles get this edge although other tiles have no outline.
- **Silhouette first:** Poncho must be told apart by shape alone, the wide brim and tall crown above a poncho and a tail.
- Sprites are drawn facing right and flipped for left, but for Poncho's victory frames, front views that never flip, whose outline is the user's strip's, 4 art pixels (2 of the strip's pixels) thick in places (GDD-ART-04).

### GDD-ART-06 World moods

Status: Decided (a mood per world, darker towards Mictlán, user, 2026-10-25; World 1's blend, user, 2026-10-25; World 2's art designed without reference images and approved from screenshots, user, 2026-11-04: "Design it, show me", then "Approved", `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`). The other worlds' moods are Proposed, and so are World 2's details. World 3's art direction below is Proposed: the World 3 design swarm's, not drawn, to be approved from screenshots like World 2's (user, 2026-11-10: "Design it, show me"); that its arena is the engine shed is Decided (user, 2026-11-10: "Engine shed").

| # | World | Mood |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Desierto | **Decided (user, 2026-10-25): a blend.** The scenery of the user's desert scene (`assets/worlds/desierto/reference-scene.jpg`): blue and purple mountains far away, clouds, the cacti and plants of the Chihuahuan desert in place of the scene's saguaros (user, 2026-10-25: keep the Chihuahuan desert and swap in cacti that grow there): prickly pears (nopal), cholla, barrel cactus (biznaga), with palm yucca and ocotillo as the tall shapes (the species are Proposed), agave (lechuguilla) and red and orange flowers in front, a strip of grass along the sandy path, red earth below. Under a warmer golden sky than in that scene, with a bright sun. |
| 2 | Mercado | Early afternoon, still bright: pale blue sky, pink and terracotta walls in the first long shadows, papel picado strings in pink, gold, teal and blue. |
| 3 | Sierra | Cool afternoon: grey-blue sky, green-grey peaks, ochre rock. |
| 4 | Selva | Golden hour in the jungle: amber sky, deep greens, mossy ruins. |
| 5 | Cenote | Underground: dark teal, shafts of light from above, turquoise water. |
| 6 | Volcán | Dusk: red and black sky, a glowing orange crater. |
| 7 | Mictlán | Night: deep purple, a pale moon, marigold paths and candle light. |

Each world is a little darker than the one before, so the trip from World 1 to 7 goes from midday through the afternoon and dusk to night (the Cenote, underground, sits between golden hour and dusk). Proposed: the HUD and Poncho's palette stay the same in every world.

- **World 1's backdrop and ground (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-26: the backdrop and the ground first, from the desert scene alone; the look approved from screenshots the same day).** The levels of World 0 and World 1 (0-1, GDD-WLD-06, 1-1, GDD-WLD-07, 1-2, GDD-WLD-08, 1-3, GDD-WLD-09, the boss arena 1-4, GDD-BOS-02, and the test levels, the sandbox and the slopes level, which count as World 1) are drawn in the desert look; the art of hazards, pickups and moving things comes later and keeps its placeholder look until then (but Don Zopilote, his cactus fruit and the arena's rocks, drawn in World 1's colours since 0.9.5, GDD-BOS-02, and World 1's four enemies, since 0.10.1, GDD-ENM-02; the plants are below). Drawn in code like Poncho's sprite (GDD-ART-01) by `scripts/art/make-desert.mjs` into `assets/sprites/desierto/`, at 4 x 4 art pixels per game pixel, with no outline on any of it (GDD-ART-05).
  - **Four far layers behind the level**, back to front (the fourth, the far desert strip in front of the mountains, is described under the plants below), each filling the screen's width and repeating sideways without a seam, never moving up or down (but for a boss's screen shake, GDD-BOS-01):
    - **Sky:** fixed to the screen (it shakes with the level in a boss's screen shake, GDD-BOS-01). Four horizontal bands of gold, from a deep amber at the top to a pale gold at the horizon, with a two-colour checker dither, 2 art pixels tall, between each pair of bands (GDD-ART-05: dither only in skies and far scenery). The **sun** is a round pale disc 24 game px across with a gold ring and short pixel rays, its centre at x = 104, y = 36 on the screen, fixed with the sky.
    - **Clouds:** a few flat-bottomed clouds, cream on top and a warm shade underneath, between y = 16 and y = 88, scrolling at 0.1 of the camera's speed.
    - **Mountains:** two ranges of peaks between y = 92 and the screen's bottom, the far one purple, the near one blue with rust-red light on the slopes facing the upper left (GDD-ART-05) and a darker blue in shadow; below the peaks the blue fills down to the screen's bottom, so a pit shows mountains and not a hole. They scroll at 0.3 of the camera's speed.
    - A layer that scrolls at a share of the camera's speed is drawn at that share of the camera's scroll, rounded to a whole art pixel (GDD-UI-04).
  - **Ground tiles:** every solid tile of the level is a ground tile 64 x 64 art pixels. A tile with no solid tile and no sand slope directly above it (or at the level's top row) is a **top tile**: a strip of green grass along its top with a ragged lower edge and a few tufts, then pale sand, then red earth. Any other solid tile, but one under a sand slope (see Slope tiles), is an **earth tile**: red earth with darker specks. Each kind has two versions, so the ground does not look stamped: the version is (7 x column + 3 x row) mod 2, which alternates like a checkerboard. Light from the upper left: each grass tuft has its light green on the upper left and its shade on the right and below, and each sand speck has a pale cell on its left. The crumbling tiles, doors, fragile walls, piñatas, springs and the rest keep their own placeholder look.
  - **Slope tiles (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-02, "Extend the desert tiles": "The same approved sand and rock, cut along the slope by the same script, shown to you in screenshots before it lands."; the look approved from screenshots the same day: "Approved"):** a sand slope (GDD-MOV-09) and the tile under it are drawn as one 16 x 32 game px strip: the top tile over the earth tile of the same version, each one game px column moved down until its top cell is the cell the slope's surface passes through at the column's middle, then cut back into the two tiles. Above the surface the slope tile is empty, so the backdrop shows. So the grass strip, the sand and the red earth run along the slope at the top tile's thickness, in whole game pixels; at a slope's high end they meet the flat top tile beside it without a seam (checked by `scripts/art/desert.test.mjs`), and at its foot the surface starts one game px above the flat ground's. The tile under a slope takes the slope's version, so its part of the strip lines up. The ground sheet has 28 frames: the four tiles as before (frames 0 to 3, unchanged), then the six slopes in the order `/ \ r R F f`, two versions each (frame 4 + 2 x the slope's place in that order + the version), then the six tiles under them in the same order (frame 16 + 2 x place + the slope's version). Drawn by `scripts/art/make-desert.mjs` like the rest; `src/ui/groundTiles.ts`.
  - **Palette (20 colours, within GDD-ART-03's 24 for a world):** sky #E39A48, #EDB15A, #F4C66E, #F9DA8E; sun #FFF6D0, #FFD25A; clouds #FFF4DC, #E9B98A; mountains #8E6A96 (far purple), #A65B5B (rust light), #4D5A9C (blue), #3A4580 (blue shadow); grass #9CC24E, #6FA03A, #4E7A2C; sand #F4D29C, #E8B87A, #D19A5E; earth #A0472F, #7A3326. The four left are the plants' (below). `BACKDROP` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/backdrop.ts`, `src/ui/groundTiles.ts`.
- **World 1's plants (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-26: drawn from the desert scene, the species it lacks from how they look, with a far plant layer too; the look approved from screenshots, 2026-10-27).** Scenery only: nothing touches them, they never hide a thing Poncho can touch, and they keep the backdrop's rules (drawn in code by `scripts/art/make-desert.mjs`, no outline, blocky at whole game pixels, light from the upper left).
  - **Seven plants** (sizes in game px, width x height): nopal (prickly pear, round pads with a few red fruit; 16 x 16), cholla (a short branching cactus with pale spines; 12 x 20), biznaga (a barrel cactus, ribbed, with a crown of orange flowers; 10 x 10), palm yucca (a brown trunk with a spiky ball of leaves; 14 x 36), ocotillo (thin brown stems fanning up from one point, red tips; 16 x 40), agave (a blue-green rosette of pointed leaves; 16 x 12) and a flower clump (red and orange flowers on green stems; 12 x 8). No saguaros (GDD-WLD-02), and none has the cactus buddy's look of a short column with two raised arms (GDD-ENM-02), so scenery is never mistaken for an enemy.
  - **Where they grow:** on top tiles of the ground (GDD-ART-06 above), rooted at the tile's top in the middle of the tile, behind the ground tiles' front edge and behind everything else in the level (in front of the backdrop). A top tile (a level tile with no level tile and no sand slope directly above it, GDD-MOV-09) at (column, row) gets a plant when (37 x column + 11 x row) mod 9 is 0 or 1 (two top tiles in nine); its kind is the seven in the order above, chosen by (3 x column + 5 x row) mod 7, so neighbouring plants differ. 0-1 gets 12 plants of all seven kinds, the sandbox 4, the slopes level 1, a nopal at column 0, 1-2 11 of five kinds (no biznaga and no flower clump), none on a sand slope or on the tile under one, 1-3 16 of six kinds (no palm yucca), none on a sand slope or on the tile under one and none in a moving block's column, and the boss arena 1-4 gets 4, on the floor at columns 8, 9, 17 and 18 (a palm yucca, a flower clump, a biznaga and an agave), none on a pillar top, where the rocks rest (checked by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`). A top tile gets none when the tiles above it, up to the plant's height, are not all empty: a level tile, a fragile wall, a crumbling tile, a piñata or a door there stops it (so a plant never pokes into a wall or a platform, even one that breaks later), or when any of these stands within 2 columns of it on the same row or the row above: the spawn, an ofrenda, the exit, a spike, a spring, a door, a key, a piñata, a taco, the veladora, or an enemy's start. That keeps the spots that teach or warn clear, and keeps cacti away from the spikes and the cactus buddy. `src/ui/plants.ts`.
  - **Far plants:** a strip of far desert between the mountains and the level, from y = 180 to y = 224 on the screen, scrolling at 0.5 of the camera's speed like the other far layers and repeating sideways without a seam. It is sand in its two darker shades with a ragged top, dotted with small plants 4 to 10 px tall in the plants' shapes and colours, drawn flat (two shades each). Below y = 224, the floor's top in every level so far, the mountains' blue foot still shows, so a pit still reads as a hole.
  - **The palette's last four colours:** #D8473A (red: flowers, fruit, ocotillo tips), #F08A3C (orange: flowers), #6F9C8E (agave and yucca blue-green), #2F5A3A (deep green: the cacti's shade). The plants and the far strip use only the 24 colours of this palette. `BACKDROP.desertFactor` (0.5), `BACKDROP.desertTop` (180) in `src/config/tuning.ts`.
- **World 2's backdrop, ground and decor (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-04: "Design it, show me": no reference images, the art designed from World 2's mood line above, following the art study's recommended defaults, and approved from screenshots, which are pending).** World 2's levels (2-1 to 2-3, GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14, La Estrella's arena 2-4, GDD-BOS-03, and World 2's labs `lab-2m` and `lab-2e`) are drawn in the market look; World 0, World 1 and World 1's test levels keep the desert, and so does World 3's cart lab `lab-3c` until World 3's art is drawn (GDD-WLD-15), (`src/ui/scenery/looks.test.ts`). The idea: what Poncho cannot touch is cool, pale and soft (the sky, a lilac hill town far off, the rooftops in the middle distance); what he can touch is warm, saturated and crisp (the terracotta roofs he runs on, the market's things); and what he touches in play, the awnings and papel picado among it, carries the near-black outline. Drawn in code like World 1's (GDD-ART-01) by `scripts/art/make-mercado.mjs` into `assets/sprites/mercado/`, at 4 x 4 art pixels per game pixel, light from the upper left, no outline on the scenery or the tiles (GDD-ART-05); `scripts/art/mercado.test.mjs` checks that the committed images are the ones the script makes, their sizes, the palette and the rules below. The game puts each world's look together from its numbers (`src/ui/scenery/looks.ts`, `src/ui/scenery/scenery.ts`) and loads only the files of the level's world (`src/ui/scenery/files.ts`, GDD-TEC-03), and the awning, cordel and rollo sheets only in a level that has them (`src/ui/MarketThings.ts`); `e2e/world2.spec.ts` checks it in the browser.
  - **Palette (24 colours, GDD-ART-03's budget for a world, none of them World 1's):** sky #86BEE0 (top), #A9D3EA (middle), #CFE8F1 (horizon); far town #C3C6DE (lit), #9AA6CD (base), #7C86B5 (shade); mid rooftops #E6C2B8 (rose), #C99A9E (rose shade), #E4CC9E (ochre), #4E4A78 (the street, in deep shade); tejas (roof tiles) #E07A52 (lit), #C45E3E, #8A3B30 (dark); plaster #F2B0A6 (lit), #E07F8E, #B85E6E (shade); talavera #1C3F9E (cobalt), #F4EFE2 (glaze white), #E9B53F (yellow); the market #D9327E (rosa mexicano), #2AA198 (teal), #4F8F4C (leaf), #3B2B3C (wrought iron), #7E5A3E (wood). The mood line's papel picado is #D9327E, #E9B53F, #2AA198 and #1C3F9E. Only the awnings, the cordeles' flags and the rollos add an outline, Poncho's #070509 (below). No colour of the ground or the decor is within ΔE 12 of Poncho's (checked by `scripts/art/mercado.test.mjs`).
  - **Layers behind the level**, back to front, each filling the screen's width, repeating sideways every 480 game px without a seam and never moving up or down (but for a boss's screen shake, GDD-BOS-01); they scroll at World 1's shares of the camera's speed (Proposed; `e2e/world2.spec.ts`):
    - **Sky:** fixed to the screen (it shakes with the level in a boss's screen shake, GDD-BOS-01). Three bands, lighter towards the horizon: #86BEE0 from y = 0 to 60, #A9D3EA to 110 and #CFE8F1 below (the camera's background colour, which also fills the strip a shake leaves at the screen's edge), with a two-colour checker dither 2 art pixels tall at each border (`sky-strips.png`; the flat bands are drawn by the game). No sun: early in the afternoon it is above the frame, and the light still comes from the upper left.
    - **Clouds:** three small flat-bottomed clouds, glaze white on top and horizon blue underneath and on their lower right, about 35 x 12, 47 x 16 and 28 x 10 game px, centred near x = 90, 250 and 410 between y = 20 and 58, none touching the strip's sides; one image each (`cloud-0.png` to `cloud-2.png`), scrolling at 0.1.
    - **Far town** (`far-town.png`, 480 x 116 game px, y = 84 to 200, scrolling at 0.3): a lilac hill whose ridge rises from about y = 170 at the strip's sides to a shoulder at y = 140 (x = 122 to 178) and a crest at y = 128 (x = 331 to 399), its slopes that face the upper left lit in streaks; small houses in terraces climbing it, fronts lit and right sides in shade, clear of the ridge above them; a parish church on the shoulder (x = 129 to 172: a nave, a bell tower in three tiers with bell openings and a cross whose tip, at y = 84, is the layer's highest pixel, and a ribbed talavera dome with a glint) and a small chapel with its bell gable on the crest (x = 358 to 372). The far colours only, with the horizon blue for the dome's glint, and a checker into shade at the foot.
    - **Mid rooftops** (`mid-roofs.png`, 480 x 76 game px, y = 148 to 224, scrolling at 0.5; its bottom is the floor's top, row 14, as in every level): eleven flat-roofed blocks (*azoteas*) with roof lines between y = 164 and 190, fronts in rose and ochre with broken 1 px parapets and small dark windows with sills; a block taller than its right-hand neighbour shows its right side in shade and casts a 45° stair-step shadow across that neighbour's front, "the first long shadows" of the mood line; four *tinacos* (plain water tanks, no logo), two washing lines, two rooftop huts with arched doorways, the market hall (the *mercado municipal*, x = 300 to 400: its barrel vault's top at y = 170, a row of clerestory windows and one big arched entrance) and two muted papel picado strings, half the size of the playfield's and without outline. Its last six rows go into the street's dark through a checker.
    - **The foot:** below the far band the flat foot is the street's #4E4A78, drawn flat by the game; the mid layer covers it down to y = 224, so a pit between two roofs shows a dark street far below and reads as a drop.
    - **The arena's plaza** (`plaza.png`, the size and place of the rooftops): in La Estrella's arena 2-4 the mid layer is a plaza instead: a kiosco (the bandstand: a raised platform with railings, thin columns, a scalloped eave and a ribbed dome with a lantern) in the middle, x = 202 to 278, arcaded *portales* round it, and papel picado strung from the kiosco to them, in the mid layer's colours.
  - **Ground tiles** (`ground.png`, 28 frames of 64 x 64 art pixels in World 1's layout, so a tile's frame is chosen as there): a **top tile** is a roof's eave: the walk line (one unbroken row of #E07A52), the tejas' channels and round ends, a scalloped shadow under them, the eave's shadow on the wall, a talavera band (cobalt borders round cobalt diamonds with yellow centres on glaze white) and pink plaster. Every pattern repeats every 4 columns, so tiles meet without a seam, and the walk line and the talavera band are the same in both versions (a glint on one teja in version 0, a chipped tile end in version 1). A **wall tile** is pink plaster with specks, in version 0 with a chip that shows two courses of adobe bricks, in version 1 with a hairline crack. The six slope frames and the tiles under them are the top tile over the wall tile cut along the slope, as World 1's (Slope tiles, above): the tejas step down a pitched roof.
  - **Decor** (`decor.png`, seven frames of 16 x 40 game px, the frame of World 1's plants; scenery under the plants' rules): the market's things, in this order of kinds: a talavera pot with geraniums (10 x 12), a bougainvillea in a terracotta planter (16 x 20), a crate heaped with oranges and limes (14 x 10, never a loose round fruit, which would read as a hilo), three clay pots (14 x 14), an unlit wrought-iron lamp post (8 x 40), a wooden rack with three folded rebozos (16 x 24) and a lotería card of our own, a sun, on an easel (12 x 16, no title and no number). None looks like something that matters in play: no piñatas, cacti, striped canvas, papel picado, cats or birds. **Where they stand** is World 1's plant rule (above), with the decor's own heights: the same formula for the spots and the kinds, two top tiles in nine, the same clearances, with two more that change nothing in World 1, which has none of these things (built, the main session's reading, 2026-11-04, Proposed): an awning, a cordel or a rollo (its whole line, rolled up or not) blocks the decor in every tile its top crosses, like a level tile, so no item stands under one within its height; and no item stands below one within 2 columns of either of its end columns, where its poles stand. On the real levels the rule gives 2-1 20 items, 2-2 22 and 2-3 20, each of all seven kinds, and the arena 2-4 1, a talavera pot (`plantSpots` in `src/ui/plants.ts`, measured by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`).
  - **The touchable world objects** carry the outline (Proposed, GDD-ART-05). The **awning** (`awning.png`, GDD-WLD-10): frames of 16 x 16 game px in three columns, the left end, a middle piece that repeats and the right end, so any awning from 2 to 6 tiles wide is put together from them; its rows are the whole awning at rest and sagging 2, 4 and 6 px, the torn one at rest and sagging, the torn halves, and the posts. Rosa mexicano and white stripes 4 px wide, starting with rosa at the left end, under a white top row, 6 px of canvas over a valance of scallops, 8 px in all, the outline drawn inside its two ends so that the drawing is exactly as wide as what Poncho lands on; the torn one faded to #E07F8E with a teal and a yellow patch and gaps in its valance; the halves and the 1 px iron posts are scenery, without outline. The **cordel** (`cordel.png`, GDD-WLD-11): frames of 24 x 16 game px, a tile of line with 4 px on each side for the flags: a 2 px line of iron twisted with wood and two outlined flags, pink and gold or teal and blue, so a cordel alternates them by tile, at rest and swayed 1 px either way; then each post's top and its pole, which repeats downwards. The **rollo** (`rollo.png`, GDD-WLD-11): frames of 24 x 24 game px, a roll of the pink paper with its wound flags' spiral on its end, tied with the cord, outlined, at rest, swayed 1 px and flapping open, by a left post and by a right post.
  - **Files:** `sky-strips.png` (1920 x 4 art pixels), `cloud-0.png` to `cloud-2.png`, `far-town.png` (1920 x 464), `mid-roofs.png` and `plaza.png` (1920 x 304 each), `ground.png` (1792 x 64), `decor.png` (448 x 160), `awning.png` (192 x 640), `cordel.png` (960 x 64) and `rollo.png` (576 x 96), about 71 KB in all; only the files of the world being played are loaded (GDD-TEC-03).
  - **`MERCADO` in `src/config/tuning.ts`:** `cloudFactor` 0.1, `farFactor` 0.3, `midFactor` 0.5, `skyBands` #86BEE0, #A9D3EA and #CFE8F1, `skyEnds` 240 and 440 art px (y = 60 and 110), `stripArt` 2, `clouds` with their top left at (72, 20), (227, 20) and (396, 48), `farTop` 84, `farBandArt` 464, `midTop` 148, `midBandArt` 304, `footColour` #4E4A78, and `decor`, the seven kinds' sizes above (`maceta` 10 x 12, `bugambilia` 16 x 20, `huacal` 14 x 10, `ollas` 14 x 14, `farol` 8 x 40, `rebozos` 16 x 24, `loteria` 12 x 16); checked by `src/ui/scenery/looks.test.ts`.
  - **Not drawn yet:** 2-3's chimneys and high roofs of their own and the star piñata in its background (GDD-WLD-14, Open Question 32); and World 2's other tiles and things, which keep their game-wide placeholders (the fragile wall's pink is close to the plaster's, Open Question 24; no World 2 level has a fragile wall).
- **World 3's backdrop, ground, decor and things (Proposed, the World 3 design swarm's art direction, 2026-11-10; designed without reference images from the mood line "Cool afternoon: grey-blue sky, green-grey peaks, ochre rock", user, 2026-11-10: "Design it, show me"; nothing is drawn or built, and it is approved from screenshots when it is; Open Question 45).** Text only: sizes and positions are targets until the drawing script exists. The contrast figures are measured, worked out with the `lab` and `deltaE` functions of `scripts/art/mercado.test.mjs` (CIE L\*a\*b\*, ΔE 76) on the proposed colours; a difference in L\* is written ΔL. World 3's levels (3-1 to 3-3, GDD-WLD-16 to GDD-WLD-18, and La Catorce's arena 3-4, GDD-BOS-04) are drawn in the Sierra look. The idea is World 2's, turned to the Sierra: what Poncho cannot touch is cool and quiet (the grey-blue sky, the green-grey peaks, the pines, and underground the violet-grey gallery wall); what he stands on is warm ochre rock, in daylight and underground alike; what he touches in play carries the near-black outline; and iron on timber always means the railway. To be drawn in code like World 2's (GDD-ART-01) by a `scripts/art/make-sierra.mjs` into `assets/sprites/sierra/`, at 4 x 4 art pixels per game pixel, light from the upper left, no outline on scenery or tiles (GDD-ART-05).
  - **Three places and an arena, one palette.** World 3 is the first world whose levels do not share one backdrop: a level, not a world, chooses its backdrop, ground sheet and decor sheet, as 2-4 has its plaza. 3-1 "La Barranca" is the canyon, 3-2 "La Mina" is underground, 3-3 "Las Cumbres" is the heights, 3-4 is the engine shed. The things and the palette are the same in all four.
  - **Palette (24 colours, GDD-ART-03's budget for a world, none of them World 1's or World 2's):**

| Role | Colour | L\* | Used for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sky top | #527391 | 47 | sky from y = 0 to 60, behind the HUD |
| Sky middle | #8AA5B8 | 66 | sky to y = 110; the clouds' underside |
| Sky horizon | #BFCDD2 | 82 | sky below y = 110; haze at the far peaks' feet |
| Mist white | #EEF1EA | 95 | cloud tops, mist, glints on silver ore and steel, the pale edge of hazards (GDD-ART-05) |
| Peak lit | #A9B8AC | 73 | far peaks facing the upper left; agave highlight; the shed's stone |
| Peak base | #7F9488 | 59 | far peaks; agave and sotol leaves; lichen |
| Peak shade | #5B7067 | 45 | far peaks in shade; the canyon's far wall; 3-3's foot |
| Pine deep | #24403A | 25 | pine shade; 3-1's foot (the canyon's depth) |
| Pine green | #3F6350 | 39 | pines and oaks; the grass's shade |
| Zacate | #A3A85A | 67 | the grass strip of the top tile; bunch grass |
| Ochre light | #EDCB86 | 83 | the walk line and lit rock; a lamp's flame core |
| Ochre | #CC983F | 66 | rock, the base colour of everything he stands on |
| Ochre shade | #9A6A2C | 49 | strata lines, rock in shade |
| Rock deep | #5A3B28 | 28 | cracks, overhang shadow, the madroño's dark bark |
| Timber lit | #A89480 | 63 | weathered timber, lit faces and end grain |
| Timber | #75614F | 43 | timber base: vigas, buffers, sleepers, planks |
| Timber dark | #43362F | 24 | timber in shade; trestle bents; backdrop timbering |
| Steel | #93A0AE | 65 | the rail's top row, cart rim and rivets, ore |
| Iron dark | #353A48 | 24 | the rail's lower row, wheels, straps, lantern cages |
| Rust | #B0562E | 47 | the vagoneta's tub; madroño bark; rust streaks |
| Gallery deep | #221D2E | 12 | the mine's roof band and its foot (a shaft) |
| Gallery base | #3D3650 | 24 | the gallery wall, the mine's background colour |
| Gallery lit | #5C5269 | 37 | the gallery wall in lamplight |
| Lamp amber | #F0A23A | 73 | lamp glow, the warning board |

  - **Beside the 24:** only the outline, Poncho's #070509, is added, on the things listed under "Things". Three shades for each material (GDD-ART-05): rock (ochre light, ochre, ochre shade, with rock deep for cracks), timber, peaks, gallery; iron has two and mist white as its glint. La Catorce's cold flame would be a 25th colour, hers alone (GDD-BOS-04, Open Question 40). The returning Alacrán and Zopilote keep their own worlds' colours (GDD-ENM-04).
  - **Poncho and the HUD against it (measured on the proposed colours).** No colour of the ground, the things or the decor is within ΔE 12 of Poncho's body colours, the rule `scripts/art/mercado.test.mjs` checks for World 2. The nearest pairs: rust and his dark fur, 13; timber lit and his cream shade, 14; ochre and his fur, 16; lamp amber and his fur, 17; rust and his red, 25. His blue is 28 or more from every sky band and 35 from the gallery wall. The HUD's lost-life outline (#9BA3C4, GDD-UI-01) is the weakest thing on a grey-blue sky, so the sky's top band, which lies behind the whole HUD, is the darkest: ΔL 20 and ΔE 22 against it (World 2's sky gives it ΔL 7 and ΔE 16); the middle band, at ΔE 11, never comes above y = 60. On the sky top the pale HUD text has ΔL 49; the red triangles have ΔE 68 by hue but ΔL 1, so their pale edge does the work there, as it does now. Underground everything of the HUD has more.
  - **What must stand out from what (measured; the rules a palette test should hold):**
    - **The outline on the gallery.** Poncho's outline is near-black, and so is a mine. Behind the rows he plays in, the mine's backdrop uses gallery base and gallery lit only (ΔL 23 and 35 from the outline); gallery deep (ΔL 10) is kept to the roof band above y = 40 and to the foot below the floor.
    - **The rail on every ground.** A rail is two rows, steel over iron dark. Steel alone vanishes on the sky's middle band (ΔE 5) and iron dark alone on the gallery wall (ΔE 10); together one row always reads: iron dark on sky and ochre (ΔL 23 to 57), steel on the gallery (ΔL 41).
    - **The cart on every ground.** Its tub is rust: ΔL 19 and ΔE 33 from the ochre floor, ΔL 23 from the gallery wall, and a different hue from every sky band. Steel has ΔL 1 against both the ochre and the sky's middle, so it is only the rim.
    - **Timber on rock.** Timber is grey where rock is yellow: ΔE 40 (timber lit) and 46 (timber) from ochre, so a beam is never read as a ledge.
    - **Pickups.** A yellow hilo (#E8B83A) is 18 from ochre and 16 from lamp amber, and an ofrenda's flame is 4 from lamp amber, so lit lamps are kept out of the play layer but for one use (the warning post, below), and no decor shows a candle or an open flame, which would read as the veladora or an ofrenda.
    - **Enemies** take their colours from these 24 (GDD-ART-03; a sketch, GDD-ENM-04, approved from screenshots). A dark animal disappears in the mine and against the canyon's mid layer: an enemy met underground needs a body at least 20 L\* from gallery base, so not the gallery colours, iron dark, timber dark or pine deep. The Murciélago is rust with ochre light wing fingers and ears; the Armadillo's shell timber lit with mist white bands (ΔE 40 from the ochre it walks on; a timber-lit ball beside a timber beam is to be judged in the screenshots); the Cacomixtle ochre light with a tail ringed iron dark and mist white (not measured against the ochre it rides over). La Catorce is black iron, so the arena's wall behind her lane is the pale stone (peak lit and peak base), never the gallery colours.
  - **Layers behind the level**, back to front, each filling the screen's width and repeating every 480 game px without a seam, at World 1's and World 2's shares of the camera's speed sideways: clouds 0.1, far 0.3, mid 0.5.
    - **Up and down, in a tall level (Proposed, with GDD-UI-04's camera):** the sky and the clouds are fixed up and down. The far strip and the foot shift at 0.1 and the mid strip at 0.2: the layers rest where this section puts them when the camera is at the level's lowest position, and for every pixel the camera is above that the far strip and the foot are drawn 0.1 px lower and the mid strip 0.2 px lower, at whole art pixels; in a 51-row level that is at most 54 px and 109 px. At the bottom of a level the backdrop is the one-screen picture; as Poncho climbs, the ranges sink toward the screen's bottom and the sky opens. The layers only ever move down from their rest, so no strip shows its lower edge. A one-screen level has no room to move, so its shift is 0 and the approved pictures of Worlds 1 and 2 hold. A boss's screen shake still moves every layer by its whole offset, on top of the shift (GDD-BOS-01).
    - **Sky (3-1, 3-3, 3-4):** fixed to the screen. Three bands, lighter towards the horizon: #527391 from y = 0 to 60, #8AA5B8 to 110, #BFCDD2 below, with a checker dither 2 art pixels tall at each border. No sun: the afternoon light is flat and comes from the upper left.
    - **Clouds (3-1, 3-3, 3-4):** three long flat streaks of high cloud, about 60 x 8, 44 x 6 and 72 x 10 game px, mist white on top and sky middle underneath, all between y = 20 and 58, so they lie on the top band; in 3-3 a fourth, a low bank 96 x 10 between y = 96 and 106.
    - **3-1 La Barranca.** *Far* (`far-barranca.png`, 480 x 116, y = 84 to 200): two ranges of the sierra. The farther one in peak lit with haze (a checker of sky horizon) at its feet, its crest near y = 96; the nearer one in peak base, its faces to the upper left lit and its right sides in peak shade, crests between y = 120 and 150, pine forest as a ragged pine green fringe on its shoulders. On the rim at the left (x = 60 to 110) the hill town Poncho has just left, a dozen blocks and one bell tower 6 px tall in the peak colours; on a far ridge at the right (x = 370 to 400) where he is going: a mine's chimney and the hoist frame over its shaft, 8 px tall. *Mid* (`mid-barranca.png`, 480 x 76, y = 148 to 224): the canyon's far wall, stepped cliffs in peak shade and peak base with level strata lines, pines standing on its ledges in pine green and pine deep, a mule trail zigzagging down it with one small stone arch bridge, and a thin waterfall, 1 px of mist white. Its last six rows go into pine deep through a checker. *Foot:* pine deep, so a chasm shows the forest far below and reads as a drop.
    - **3-2 La Mina, underground.** The backdrop keeps its four slots, filled with rock. *The sky slot* is the rock itself, fixed to the screen: gallery deep from y = 0 to 40 (the roof, behind the HUD), gallery base below, the camera's background colour, a checker strip between. *No clouds.* *Far* (`far-mina.png`, 480 x 160, y = 40 to 200): the old workings: the wall's strata as long slanting lines of gallery lit, two dark side tunnels (gallery deep) with small timber frames, an ore vein that crosses the strip as a broken line of steel with a mist white pixel every 20 px or so. *Mid* (`mid-mina.png`, 480 x 124, y = 100 to 224, see-through between its pieces): the gallery's timber sets, a pair of posts and a cap every 80 px, in timber dark with gallery lit on their left edges; from every second cap hangs a carbide lamp, a caged lantern 6 x 8 px (iron dark cage, ochre light core, lamp amber round it), with its pool of light on the wall, gallery lit in an eight-sided patch about 40 px across, a checker at its rim. An ore chute and a ladder stand between two sets. *Foot:* gallery deep: a pit is a black shaft. The lamps never flicker and the pools never move: the light is painted on the wall, it is not a light source.
    - **3-3 Las Cumbres.** *Far* (`far-cumbres.png`, as 3-1's): the sierra seen from above: four ridges one behind the other, their crests between y = 120 and 170, lower in the strip than 3-1's so that more sky shows, each paler than the one in front (peak shade, peak base, peak lit, then peak lit in a checker with sky horizon), and mist lying in the valleys between them. *Mid* (`mid-cumbres.png`, as 3-1's): the near crags and the tops of pines in peak shade, pine green and pine deep, and far off the line he came by: a small timber trestle in timber dark crossing between two crags. *Foot:* peak shade, hazier than 3-1's, so the heights read as higher.
    - **3-4, La Catorce's arena, La Casa de Máquinas (the engine shed, Decided, user, 2026-11-10: "Engine shed"; `mid-casa.png`, the size and place of the other mids).** The sky, the clouds and 3-3's far strip, and in the mid layer the shed's back wall: dressed stone in peak base with peak lit courses, three tall round-arched openings (x = 60 to 140, 200 to 280 and 340 to 420) through which the sky and the ridges show, timber roof trusses above in timber dark, which carry the arena's high rails and its gantry, a round water tank on a timber stand and a coal bunker against the wall. No number 14 is painted on the wall: it is on her. Daylight through the arches keeps a black engine readable. *Foot:* rock deep, never seen, since the arena has no pit. 3-3's last five columns are the shed's stone gable (GDD-WLD-18).
  - **Ground tiles** (`ground.png` and `ground-mina.png`, 28 frames of 64 x 64 art pixels each in World 1's layout, so a tile's frame is chosen as there):
    - **Top tile, outdoors (3-1, 3-3):** the walk line (one unbroken row of ochre light), a strip of zacate 3 px deep along it with a ragged lower edge and a few tufts standing 1 or 2 px over the line, shaded pine green on the right, then a pale ledge of ochre light 2 px thick, then ochre rock with one strata line. The walk line and the ledge are the same in both versions; version 0 has a tuft more, version 1 a pebble.
    - **Top tile, underground and in the shed (3-2, 3-4):** the same walk line and ledge with no grass: a trodden floor with a little rubble, two chips of ochre shade and one of steel (ore). Bare rock underfoot is how a level says "inside".
    - **Rock tile (the body tile, both sheets):** ochre, with strata: two level lines of ochre shade that run through the tile at the same heights in both versions, so tiles meet without a seam and a wall of them reads as bedded rock; light specks of ochre light on the upper left of each line. Version 0 has a crack of rock deep, version 1 a patch of lichen (peak base) outdoors and a streak of ore (steel, one mist white pixel) underground.
    - **Cliff face:** a stack of rock tiles is the cliff; no new tile kind is needed. An option: two more frames per version for a rock tile with nothing to its left (a 2 px lit edge of ochre light, broken in two places) and with nothing to its right (a 3 px edge of ochre shade with rock deep in its notches), so the canyon's two sides are told apart by the light. Without them the cliff is drawn with the plain rock tile and still reads.
    - **Slopes:** the top tile over the rock tile cut along the slope, as World 1's (Slope tiles): a scree slope outdoors, a ramp underground; they lie under every sloped rail that is not on a trestle (GDD-WLD-15).
    - **Mine timbering** is never a ground tile. Timber underground is one of three things: the backdrop's timber sets (dark, untouchable), the low beams and the planks of crumbling ledges (below). The gallery's rock roof is the rock tile.
  - **Things** (the touchable world objects carry the outline, as World 2's do; their posts and supports do not):
    - **Rail and sleepers** (riel y durmientes; `rail.png`, frames of 16 x 16 game px: level, up, down; no outline, since Poncho passes through it, GDD-WLD-15): in the bottom 4 px of its tile, a rail 2 px tall, one row of steel over one row of iron dark, on sleepers seen end on, timber blocks 4 px wide and 2 px tall every 8 px, with the backdrop showing between them. On a slope the rail steps 1 px per px. A rail reads as "not a floor" by three signs: it is 4 px thin where every floor is at least 8, it is see-through, and it has no outline. Where a rail has no ground under it, or the crumbling tiles under it are gone, the game draws a **trestle** below it, scenery without outline: a bent every 32 px (two posts 2 px wide in timber dark, leaning in, with one cross brace) down to the first ground tile or the screen's bottom, and a stringer 2 px tall under the sleepers.
    - **Buffer** (tope; 16 x 16): a block, three squared timbers laid one on another between two short posts, with an iron plate on the side the cart comes from. It closes the rail: a square block means "the ride ends here, safely". The art direction draws it outlined, in the full timber colours; since a buffer blocks carts only (GDD-WLD-15) and the outline and full-coloured timber elsewhere mean solid, whether it keeps them is settled with the screenshots (Open Question 45).
    - **Broken end** (16 x 16, no outline, a hazard's pale edge): the two rails bend up and apart over the last 6 px, their torn tips mist white (GDD-ART-05: hazards have a pale tip), the last sleeper hangs by one end, and nothing is drawn beyond. An open, splayed shape means "the rail ends here, jump".
    - **Warning post** (8 x 24, the board and the lamp outlined, the pole not): a timber pole 2 px wide with a diamond board 8 x 8 at its top, lamp amber with a mist white rim and a dark broken line across it (a rail with a gap, drawn at art pixels), and under the board a small lit lantern 4 x 5. It is the only lit lamp in the play layer, in all four levels: a lit lamp beside a rail means the rail breaks 6 tiles on (GDD-WLD-15; the art direction's first text had 2). In the arena the board sits on the rail's last sleeper (GDD-BOS-04). The three ends differ by shape, not by colour (GDD-UI-03): a square block, splayed tips, a diamond on a pole.
    - **Vagoneta** (`vagoneta.png`; 32 x 16 px on the level, outlined, the outline inside its box so the drawing is as wide as what he lands in): a tipping tub of riveted plate, 30 px wide at its rim and 24 at its floor, rust with a steel rim 2 px tall and three steel rivets, a seam of rock deep under the rim; two wheels 6 px across in iron dark with steel hubs, their bottoms on the rail. It is drawn in two parts: the back wall and the dark inside behind Poncho, the front wall in front of him, so he stands in it and its front hides his feet and shins (his feet are on a floor 8 px over the rail's ground, GDD-WLD-15). **Standing:** wheels still, a timber chock wedged under its leading wheel, the inside showing empty. **Rolling:** the chock is gone, the hubs' glint turns through two frames (one frame every 4 steps), two pixels of dust or spark trail the rear wheel, and the tub sits 1 px lower on its frame on alternate frames. Standing and rolling differ by the chock, not only by motion. On a slope the same three frames are drawn tilted 45° (36 x 36 frames; up and down are mirror images). Off a broken end it falls in its last rolling frame, without turning.
    - **Low beam** (viga; `viga.png`, 16 x 16 pieces: a left end, a middle that repeats, a right end; outlined, as a solid thing he bumps into): a squared cap timber 10 px thick, drawn against its tile's bottom edge, where it stops him (the art direction's first text had it in the tile's upper part, 6 px above where it collides): timber lit on top, timber below, timber dark underneath, the saw-cut end grain as rings on each end, an iron strap round it every 16 px, and a row of mist white along its lower front edge so the edge he must clear reads on the dark wall. Its two posts are scenery in timber dark behind the level. Timber lit and timber are never used in the mine's backdrop.
    - **Crumbling ledges, World 3's dress** (`ledge.png`, 16 x 16, outlined; the rule, the 0.5 s shake and the pale flash stay GDD-WLD-05's): two looks for the one mechanic, both a thin slab over empty air, where solid ground always has a body under it. *The rock ledge* (3-1's cliff faces): a slab of ochre light and ochre 6 px thick with two cracks of rock deep through it, and loose stones hanging under it for 3 px, the rest of the tile empty. *The plank* (3-3 and 3-4: old scaffolds and the trestle's deck): one timber plank 5 px thick, split along its grain, with a nail head of iron dark at each end and a short broken bracket under it. A rail can lie on a plank (3-3's bridges, GDD-WLD-18). Cracks and the empty tile under the slab are the sign, not the colour (GDD-UI-03). How the arena's gantry tiles, which only La Catorce's slam brings down, are told from a plank is open (GDD-BOS-04, Open Question 45).
  - **Decor** (two sheets of seven frames of 16 x 40 game px, the frame of World 1's plants; scenery under the plants' rules: nothing touches it, no outline, behind everything in the level):
    - **Outdoors, the pine-oak forest of the Sierra Madre** (`decor.png`; 3-1 and 3-3), in this order of kinds: pino (a pine, a rust-dark trunk with three tiers of pine green boughs, 16 x 40), encino (an oak, a short forked trunk under a wide round crown, 16 x 28), madroño (a small tree with smooth rust-red bark and a tuft of leaves, 14 x 24), maguey (a rosette of broad peak-base leaves with lit edges, 16 x 12), sotol (a low spiky rosette with one tall flower stalk, 10 x 32), a tocón (a pine stump with a fallen branch, 14 x 8) and zacatón (a clump of bunch grass, 12 x 8).
    - **Underground and in the shed** (`decor-mina.png`; 3-2 and 3-4): a heap of ore (angular grey stones with silver glints, 14 x 8), a stack of spare timbers lying flat (16 x 10), a barrel (10 x 12), a pick and a shovel leaning together (12 x 16), an ore bucket with its rope coiled (10 x 10), an unlit lantern hung on a peg post (8 x 24) and a pile of ore sacks (14 x 10).
    - **None looks like something that matters in play:** nothing round and banded or ball-shaped (the Armadillo rolls as a ball, so there is no round boulder: the stump takes its place), nothing with wheels, no upright timber block (the buffer), no diamond, no lit lamp, no candle or flame, no loose plank in the air, no bat, ringtail or cat, and no loose round nugget, which would read as a hilo.
    - **Where they stand** is World 1's plant rule with each kind's own height: the same formula for spots and kinds, two top tiles in nine, the same clearances, and three more for World 3's things: a rail blocks the decor in every tile it crosses, like a level tile, so nothing grows on a track; no item stands within 2 columns of a buffer, a broken end, a warning post or a cart's start, on the same row or the row above; and a low beam, being solid, stops any item taller than the space under it by the rule as it is. Crumbling ledges are not ground tiles and get none.
  - **Readability in the mine (GDD-UI-03).** The mine is dark scenery, not a dark game. No darkness is laid over the level: no vignette, no spotlight round Poncho, no fog; Poncho, enemies, pickups, hazards, tiles and the HUD are drawn in 3-2 exactly as bright as outdoors. The floor stays ochre, 42 L\* lighter than the wall behind it, and a pit is the darkest thing on screen, so where he can stand is plain without any lamp. Lamps are static: no flicker, no pulsing pools, nothing to turn off for reduced motion. Nothing in the mine is told by colour alone: the three rail ends differ by shape, hazards keep their pale tip, a crumbling ledge is a cracked slab over empty air, a low beam has its pale lower edge. A level darker than World 4's golden hour is the one break in "each world a little darker than the one before": 3-1 and 3-3 keep the world's cool afternoon, and 3-2 is an interior between them, as the Cenote is among the worlds.
  - **With care (GDD-VIS-04).** An old silver-mining town of the Sierra Madre, of no one place: no real town's skyline, tunnel, church or mine is copied, and the 14 is an engine's number. The scenery shows the work, not the workers: tools, timber, lamps and carts, no people, no pack animals, no ghosts, bones or skulls. No shrine, cross or candle stands at the mine's mouth or in its galleries: candle light belongs to the ofrendas and the veladora. The plants are those of the sierra's pine-oak forest, not the desert's of World 1 (no nopal, cholla or biznaga here, and no cactus buddy, GDD-ENM-04).
  - **Files (to be):** `sky-strips.png`, `cloud-0.png` to `cloud-3.png`, `far-barranca.png`, `far-cumbres.png` (1920 x 464 art px each), `far-mina.png` (1920 x 640), `mid-barranca.png`, `mid-cumbres.png`, `mid-casa.png` (1920 x 304 each), `mid-mina.png` (1920 x 496), `ground.png`, `ground-mina.png`, `decor.png`, `decor-mina.png`, `rail.png`, `buffer.png`, `vagoneta.png`, `viga.png`, `ledge.png`. Only the files of the level being played are loaded (GDD-TEC-03).

### GDD-ART-07 UI look

Status: Proposed

- **Font:** a blocky pixel font drawn in code like the art (GDD-ART-01), cream text (#F1F3E3) with a dark plum outline, so it reads on any background. Gameplay text stays readable at the sizes GDD-UI-01 and GDD-UI-02 give.
- **Serape band:** menus, the title screen and the pause screen are framed by a woven band in Poncho's blue, red and cream triangles.
- **Lives triangles** (GDD-UI-01): filled in Poncho's red with a cream edge; a lost one is only the cream edge.
- **Title:** "PONCHO" in large cream letters with a red shadow, "Siete Vidas" in gold, Poncho beside it. The layout, sizes and menus stay those of GDD-UI-01 and GDD-UI-02; only the look changes.
- **Not built yet:** the HUD, menus and title still use the placeholder look described in GDD-UI-01 and GDD-UI-02 (plain text, outlined triangles), until the art switch (GDD-ART-01).
