## 14. Technical

### GDD-TEC-01 Stack

Status: Decided (user, 2026-10-19: state of the art, latest releases). See `docs/decisions/0002-state-of-the-art-stack.md`.

Phaser 4, TypeScript 7 (native compiler, strict), Vite 8, Vitest 5 for unit tests, Playwright for end-to-end playtests, Oxlint with type-aware rules for linting, Node 24 LTS in CI. Exact versions are in the ADR and `package-lock.json`. The first decision (Phaser 3) is superseded.

### GDD-TEC-02 Deterministic simulation

Status: Proposed

- Fixed timestep of 60 Hz for gameplay logic; rendering interpolates.
- Simulation code (movement, lives, damage, collision) has no Phaser imports and no `Math.random()` without a seeded source, so it is unit-testable and replayable.
- Scene code only wires input, rendering and audio to the simulation.

### GDD-TEC-03 Performance and mobile targets

Status: Proposed

- 60 fps on a mid-range phone from the last 4 years, 30 fps floor on low end with automatic quality fallback.
- Initial download under 5 MB for the first level; later assets lazy-loaded. The 1920 x 1080 art (GDD-ART-01) is drawn in code and saved as images: the first world's images must fit this budget too.
- Works offline once loaded (service worker, PWA installable).
- Handles device pixel ratio, safe areas, orientation change and tab suspension.

### GDD-TEC-04 Level authoring

Status: Proposed

Levels are authored in Tiled and exported as JSON (tilemap and object layers: spawn, ofrendas, hilos, enemies, hazards, exit). A validator test loads every level and checks: exactly one spawn, 3 ofrendas, an exit (a boss level: neither, GDD-BOS-01), no gap wider than the world's limit (GDD-WLD-03).

- The validator is built (`src/levels/validate.ts`) and a test runs it over every level in `src/levels/index.ts`: 0-1 (GDD-WLD-06), 1-1 (GDD-WLD-07), 1-2 (GDD-WLD-08), 1-3 (GDD-WLD-09), the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02), 2-1 (GDD-WLD-12), 2-2 (GDD-WLD-13), 2-3 (GDD-WLD-14), La Estrella's arena 2-4 (GDD-BOS-03), the sandbox and the slopes level (GDD-MOV-09), and World 2's two test levels, the Mercado lab `lab-2m` (an awning ladder, a torn awning, a cordel staircase, a rollo bridge, an awning over a pit and a far-post rollo, `src/levels/lab2m.test.ts`) and the enemy lab `lab-2e` (a Paloma, a Chapulín and a Trompo in pens, `src/levels/lab2e.test.ts`), each two screens wide and opened by its address; they add no world to the save (GDD-PRG-02). World 3's cart lab `lab-3c` (GDD-WLD-15: four rails, one level, one climbing onto a plateau no jump reaches, one coming down from it, one rolling left; `src/levels/lab3c.test.ts`) is validated the same way, as a World 3 test level: of World 3's rules the validator has a same-height gap limit of 6 tiles and the 8 screens (`LEVEL_RULES`), and not yet the part of its gap rule that depends on the drop (GDD-WLD-03). The Tiled JSON loader is not built yet: levels are still written in code, and the loader will feed the same validator (user decision, 2026-10-23). One spawn is guaranteed by the level data, which has exactly one; the loader will count spawn objects.
- It checks 3 ofrendas, an exit, a length of 3 screens to the world's longest, 6 in Worlds 0 and 1 and 8 from World 2 on (GDD-WLD-03; a world with no length limit fails, like one with no gap limit), no gap wider than the world's limit, and no enemy starting within 3 tiles of the spawn or an ofrenda (GDD-ENM-01). It also checks that every sand slope tile has a level tile directly under it, and holds no spike, spring, crumbling tile, door, fragile wall or piñata (GDD-MOV-09). A world with no gap limit in GDD-WLD-03 is itself a problem; World 1 and the tutorial (World 0, with World 1's 3 tiles) have one, and World 2 has 4 tiles (Proposed, GDD-WLD-03, Open Question 13); World 3's is written (Proposed, GDD-WLD-03) and not in the validator, which has no limit for Worlds 3 to 7 yet.
- **World 2 (built, Proposed):** the validator holds World 2's levels (2-1 to 2-3, and the arena 2-4 under the boss-level rule, GDD-BOS-03) to its gap limit of 4 tiles and the covered-pit rule (GDD-WLD-03, `openGaps`): a gap's width counts only its uncovered pit columns, those with no whole awning, cordel or rollo spanning the column with its top at or above the ground at the pit's nearer edge and at most 3 tiles above it; a torn awning covers nothing. It also checks that an awning's top is no higher than y = 160 (`AWNING.minTopY`, GDD-WLD-10), that no awning (8 px thick, `AWNING.height`) and no cordel or rollo line (2 px, `ROLLO.linePx`) overlaps a level tile, that the 3-tile rule holds for the Paloma, the Chapulín and the Trompo, and that La Estrella's start in each of her three phases is at least 3 tiles from the spawn (GDD-BOS-01). Its own tests (`src/levels/validate.test.ts`) have a level that breaks each part (L-028). That no enemy starts on an awning, and that no awning or cordel stands over a sand slope, are left to each level's tests (World 2's levels have no slopes).
- **World 3 (Proposed, the World 3 design swarm's; not built):** the validator is to hold World 3's levels (3-1 to 3-3, and the arena 3-4 under the boss-level rule, GDD-BOS-04) to these, each with a level in its own tests that breaks it (L-028):
  - **The gap rule of GDD-WLD-03:** the limit by drop (6 tiles plus the drop, up to 14) and by rise (4, 3, 2), the roof's reduction, the drop read left to right from the ground top at the gap's two edges, and a crumbling tile counted as a bank, not a pit column. A rail, a trestle, a cart, a buffer and a moving platform cover nothing.
  - **Height and bottoms:** a level is 17 to 51 rows tall. "A pit column is one whose bottom row is empty" stays, with the bottom row read per column: the raised bottom's row in a span, the last row elsewhere (GDD-WLD-03, Open Question 38). Every span's row is inside the level; under a raised bottom every column is solid or empty all the way down; the start, every respawn point and the exit are above their column's bottom row. GDD-WLD-03's sight rules are checked as far as they are geometry.
  - **Rails (GDD-WLD-15):** a broken end has its post 6 tiles before it, 6 or more level tiles before it and 12 or more tiles from its cart's start, and from the rail's end to the bank at most 5 tiles plus the drop; 7 empty tiles over the rail's ground where he must jump from a cart; a beam's underside 2 tiles over the rail's ground; no two carts on one stretch of rail. A boss level is exempt from the post, the level run and the 12 tiles (GDD-BOS-04), and its rule otherwise holds 3-4's rails, springs and crumbling tiles like any level's.
  - **Enemies (GDD-ENM-04):** a Murciélago's whole loop touches no solid tile, spike or crumbling tile; no Armadillo's walk or roll crosses a standing cart's start; a Cacomixtle's sight stretch (level or uphill for it to 200 px or its buffer, with no ground that lets a jump hide him or wake it early); the 3-tile rule for all three. No check asks for a row of bats over a pit: a row is a reward only.
- A gap is a run of adjacent pit columns, and a pit column is one whose bottom row is empty, where Poncho falls out of the level. A slope tile never sits in the bottom row (it needs a level tile under it), so a slope beside a gap does not change its width. A floating tile higher up does not fill a pit (user decision, 2026-10-23), and a column whose bottom row alone is empty counts, on the safe side.
- A level marked as a test level (the sandbox, the slopes level, `lab-2m`, `lab-2e`, `lab-3c`) skips the exit and length checks and gets the others (user decision, 2026-10-23), the slope checks included (GDD-MOV-09). The sandbox's pit, columns 14 to 16, is a gap of 3 tiles.
- A level with a boss (the arena 1-4, GDD-BOS-02, and La Estrella's 2-4, GDD-BOS-03) is a boss level, held to a rule of its own (built, Proposed): instead of 3 ofrendas, an exit and 3 to 6 screens, it must have no ofrendas and no exit and be exactly one screen (30 tiles) wide, and the boss's starting point must be at least 3 tiles from the spawn, the arena's only respawn (GDD-BOS-01, GDD-ENM-01). It keeps the other checks every level gets: no gap wider than the world's limit and no enemy starting within 3 tiles of the spawn (checked by `src/levels/validate.test.ts` with a boss level that breaks each), and the sand slope checks (GDD-MOV-09).

### GDD-TEC-05 Tuning in one place

Status: Proposed

All gameplay numbers in this document live in a single typed file, `src/config/tuning.ts`, each constant commented with its GDD ID. A unit test simulates a full jump and asserts the derived values stated in section 4: peak 56 px within 1.5 px, and horizontal distance 66 px at full run within one step of travel (2 px).

### GDD-TEC-07 Dependency policy

Status: Decided (use the latest stable releases, user, 2026-10-19). The review cadence and the holdback rule are Proposed.

- Track the latest stable release of every dependency. No pre-release versions in `main`.
- At every GDD minor version bump, run `npm outdated`, upgrade, run the full gate and the playtests, and record the result in the changelog or an ADR.
- Anything that must stay behind (a blocker like a peer-dependency conflict) is written down in an ADR with the condition for upgrading.
- Phaser APIs marked deprecated (the Canvas renderer, custom pipelines) are not used.

### GDD-TEC-06 Testing strategy

Status: Proposed

- Unit tests (Vitest) for simulation: movement numbers, forgiveness windows, lives rules, save migration.
- Level validation tests (GDD-TEC-04): every level in `src/levels/index.ts` passes the validator.
- Playwright playtests per the `harness-playtest` skill: boot, jump, life loss, touch input, console free of errors.
- `scripts/check.mjs` runs all of these through npm scripts once they exist.

---

## 15. Scope

### GDD-MVP-01 Vertical slice

Status: Proposed

The first playable milestone:

- Poncho with run, jump (GDD-MOV-01 to GDD-MOV-06), stomp and claw (GDD-CMB-01 to GDD-CMB-03).
- Seven-lives system complete (GDD-LIFE-01 to GDD-LIFE-05) and HUD (GDD-UI-01).
- Power-ups: Taco, Chile and Fuego, piñatas (GDD-PWR-01, GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-03, GDD-PWR-05, GDD-PWR-06).
- World 1 with 3 levels and the boss, bestiary of GDD-ENM-02.
- Keyboard and touch controls (GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03), including the Fuego button; the gamepad (GDD-CTL-02) is built ahead of it.
- Autosave (GDD-PRG-02), pause, settings with volume and language.
- Placeholder music and SFX are acceptable.

Done means: the `harness-playtest` skill passes on desktop and a phone viewport, and the user has played it.

### GDD-MVP-02 Later scope

Status: Proposed

Worlds 2 to 7 (World 2, Mercado, is built: GDD-WLD-10 to GDD-WLD-14, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03; its playtests and the user's approval of its screenshots are pending; World 3, Sierra, is designed and not built: GDD-WLD-15 to GDD-WLD-18, GDD-ENM-04, GDD-BOS-04), Chile Fantasma (GDD-PWR-04; its mechanic and art are built ahead of the MVP, user, 2026-11-10, and its piñatas in 1-2 and 2-2 come next), glide, wall cling, further powers, score, cosmetics shop, full soundtrack, localisation beyond English, Spanish and German, native packaging (new ADR).

The glide and the wall cling are built early but stay off in real levels; only the sandbox turns them on (GDD-MOV-07, GDD-MOV-08). The glide comes on in every level when its unlock is built with World 3 (user, 2026-11-10: "Glide everywhere", GDD-PRG-01).

---

## Open Questions

Answer these to move items from Proposed to Decided. Until then the Proposed text stands as the working assumption.

1. **Taco as shield (GDD-PWR-01, GDD-CMB-03):** Taco and Fuego absorb hits like Mario's mushroom and flower. This makes lives less scarce. Right balance, or should power-ups be rarer or not absorb hits?
2. **Power-up set (section 6):** Taco, Chile (Fuego fireballs), Chile Fantasma (star), piñata blocks. Anything you want added, such as a different fireball style, a reserve slot, or other foods?
3. **Seven worlds (GDD-WLD-02):** are the world themes and names right? Anything you want, or want avoided? (World 1 is answered: the Chihuahuan desert, user, 2026-10-25.)
4. **Story (GDD-VIS-01):** is the curse-and-seven-lives story seed what you want, or a different premise?
5. **Pronouns (GDD-ART-02):** answered: he (user, 2026-10-25).
6. **Brand and chain scope (GDD-VIS-05):** the draft keeps the game free of wallets, tokens and on-chain features. Is that right? Should the game mention the Poncho on Base token or community anywhere (credits, links), and who owns the final brand rules? Note that app stores restrict crypto features, which affects distribution (question 11).
7. **Combat (GDD-CMB-02):** keep both claw swipe and stomp, with fireballs on top? Partly answered (user, 2026-10-21): the claw swipe is a standard mechanic that Poncho always has, not a power-up. Stomp and fireballs alongside it are still open.
8. **Difficulty (GDD-VIS-02):** is assist mode and a kid-friendly tone right, or do you want a harder game?
9. **Audio (GDD-AUD-01):** will you provide music, or should placeholders be generated and licensed assets chosen?
10. **Score (GDD-PRG-03):** keep or cut? Medals are kept (user, 2026-10-30).
11. **Distribution:** where will it be hosted (itch.io, own site, stores)? This drives PWA versus native packaging.
12. **Taco pickups in Taco (GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-06):** a taco lying on the floor is left where it is when Poncho is already Taco, but a walking taco released from a piñata is used up when he touches it. Should both behave the same?
13. **Gap limits after World 1 (GDD-WLD-03, GDD-TEC-04):** World 1 has one (3 tiles), and World 2's is built at 4 tiles (Proposed), which needs a run-up, with a covered-pit rule: pit columns under a whole awning, a cordel or a rollo, within 3 tiles above the pit's nearer edge, do not count, and no uncovered run may be wider than 4. The level validator fails a level in any other world until that world has a limit. Is 4 right for World 2 (another way: keep 3 everywhere and let only awnings and rollos make wider pits)? Should the limit grow by world, up to the 5 tiles a running jump can cross? World 3's rule is written and not built (Proposed, the design swarm's): with the glide, 6 tiles plus one per tile the far side lies lower, up to 14, less for a higher far side or a low roof, two thirds of what a perfect glided jump crosses (measured by the swarm's scratch test). The designer recommends it over a gentler 5 tiles plus the drop, which would also survive a short hop with a late glide but would let a plain jump cross every level gap. Which?
14. **Detail on phones (GDD-ART-01):** at 1920 x 1080, one art pixel is less than one screen pixel on most phones, so the finest detail blurs there. Fine as it is, or should the sprites keep their key details (eye, triangles) at least 2 art pixels wide?
15. **Secrets (GDD-WLD-03, GDD-WLD-06 to GDD-WLD-09, GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14, GDD-WLD-16 to GDD-WLD-18):** tiles cannot hide anything yet, so the veladoras of 0-1, 1-1, 1-2 and 1-3 are off the path but in plain view, and World 2's, in 2-1, 2-2 and 2-3, are in plain view too, but high, each reached by a tier 3 rebound as their sections have it (2-2's floats in the top row; the sky above it stays unused: the camera does not follow him up there; 2-3's is also reached without tier 3, Open Question 46). World 3's, as designed, are in plain view as well, each asking for the move its level does not: 3-1's on a pillar under the last glide, taken by not gliding; 3-2's at a buffer in a cutting, taken by staying in a cart; 3-3's in a hollow under the first trestle, taken by a glide the wrong way. Fine for now, or should a secret be truly hidden (a false wall, a room off the screen's top)?
16. **Short phone windows (GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-03):** on a landscape phone whose browser window is under 360 CSS px tall (a browser bar showing), the menu rows grow so far that the last row of the title menu and of the results menu runs off the bottom of the screen (found in the review of PR #62). Since the title menu moved up to 112 px (0.8.7) its four rows fit down to about 340 CSS px; the results menu's do not. The settings screen's six rows (0.8.10, from 62 px) fit down to about 381 CSS px, and since its seventh row (Screen shake, 0.10.8) its list scrolls instead of running off the screen (GDD-UI-02, "Scrolling"); five rows from 80 px fitted down to about 341, four from 96 px to about 302. Shrink the rows there, move the menus up (into the medal line on the results screen), or accept it?
17. **What 1-2 brings back (GDD-WLD-08):** besides its new things it has one cactus buddy and two piñatas from 1-1, and no Alacrán or Zopilote; its secret veladora is reached by the spring's timed launch, which no level teaches (only the sandbox has it), or by a full jump left from the top of the 4-tile wall the plain launch throws Poncho onto. Right, or should 1-2 bring back more of 1-1's enemies, or move the veladora so that it asks for the timed launch alone?
18. **The details of World 1's run and lives (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-PRG-03):** built as decided (user, 2026-11-02: "Build it as decided", "Save the lives", "Lives at the clear", "Keep them", "Lives lost in the level", "Follow the run"), with these Proposed details. (a) The save writes World 1's run only at a clear (the lives at the exit and the next level) and when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (0 lives and 1-1), so quitting a level, or closing the game, and picking "Start" plays it again from its start with the lives Poncho had at the last clear, the lives lost in it given back (another way: write the lives at every life lost or won, which keeps the losses but lets a player who quits after an ofrenda win its life again on every new start). (b) An address (`?level=…`) always starts with seven. (c) "Start" plays 1-1, World 1's start, always with seven, and once the run has cleared World 1's last level a new run starts there (another way: the last level cleared, the boss arena 1-4, again); partly answered (user, 2026-11-04: "Latest world"): "Start" goes on to World 2's run after 1-4's clear (built), and World 2 works the same way, 2-1 always with seven and a new run after 2-4. (d) A save from before the run was kept goes by the levels cleared: one with 1-1 cleared opens on 1-2 with seven. (e) Gold counts the lives lost as the lives the level started with less those at its exit, so a life won back at an ofrenda or the veladora counts against one lost (that keeps "4 lives at the exit" for a level started with seven); another way: count every life lost, won-back lives not helping. After the restart that follows the last life the count starts again from seven. (f) "Retry" on the tutorial 0-1's results menu starts it with seven, since its lives carry nowhere (another way: the lives at its clear, as "Lives at the clear" gives a World 1 level). (g) Out of lives in 1-1 the restart stays in the same scene, where a Left or Right key or a finger held through the press moves Poncho as soon as the flip ends, as before; out of lives in 1-2, 1-3 or 1-4 the restart in 1-1 starts as the next level does, where a button held through the press does nothing until it is lifted (GDD-UI-02). Right?
19. **The pillar tops in Don Zopilote's arena (GDD-BOS-02):** in 1-4 nothing of his reaches Poncho on a pillar top in any phase, except on a lip of about 5 px at each pillar's inner edge, where the dive or the drop beside that pillar clips him. Fine for World 1's boss (the lip a warning not to crowd the edge), or should the whole top be safe (his lap's ends a few px further in), or should a dive or a fruit reach the tops?
20. **His fruit's telegraph (GDD-ENM-01, GDD-BOS-02):** a cactus fruit has no 0.5 s telegraph of its own; it falls in view for 0.7 to 0.9 s before it reaches Poncho on the floor. Is the fall its telegraph, or should Don Zopilote flash or call out before each drop?
21. **Hilos in the boss arena (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-UI-01, GDD-UI-02):** 1-4 has none, so the HUD counter reads "0/0" and the level clear screen "Hilos 0/0", and so does La Estrella's 2-4, which has none either (GDD-BOS-03). Hide both in a boss level, or give the arenas some hilos?
22. **Awnings (GDD-WLD-10):** the hold ladder is decided (user, 2026-11-04: "Hold ladder"). Its details are proposed: three tiers at 480, 540 and 600 px/s, reusing the spring's two launches with one between (another way: two tiers, 480 and 600); and a plain landing settles him after at most one small hop, so he can stand on an awning (another way: awnings always bounce, pure trampolines). Right? They are built that way; the build's own details (a late press up to 6 steps after a plain landing, a torn awning's plain rebound of at least 180 px/s, what counts as held) are in GDD-WLD-10.
23. **Papel picado (GDD-WLD-11):** cordeles are one-way floors that never move or bounce; a rollo is unrolled into a bridge by the claw or a fireball; the claw does not cut cordeles. Right, or should the claw cut cordeles (like the fragile walls, but a swipe at an enemy could cut the next bridge), or should cordeles be scenery only?
24. **The fragile wall in World 2 (GDD-WLD-05, GDD-ART-06):** the game-wide fragile wall is a pink papel picado tile, close in hue to World 2's pink plaster walls and in theme to its hanging strings. Recolour it game-wide to rosa mexicano with a white scalloped edge and the outline when its final art is drawn, and check it in World 2's screenshots, or something else?
25. **World 2's look (GDD-ART-06):** designed without reference images (user, 2026-11-04: "Design it, show me"), on the art study's defaults: a parish church with a tiled dome and a hilltop chapel on the skyline (no real town copied), no sun, roofs of terracotta tejas over a talavera band over pink plaster, awnings in rosa mexicano and white only (other colourways later, always with white stripes and scallops), an outline on the awnings and papel picado flags as on characters, World 1's parallax speeds, no decorative piñatas, and a plaza with a kiosco behind La Estrella's arena. They are in the game now, and the user approved them from screenshots (2026-11-04: "Approved", `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`); any of these defaults can still be changed.
26. **World 2's names and tone (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03, GDD-VIS-04):** the levels "El Tianguis" (2-1), "Mercado Municipal" (2-2), "Azoteas" (2-3) and "La Plaza" (2-4); the enemies' names (Paloma, Chapulín, Trompo, and the Zanate later); defeated birds fly off in a puff of feathers and toys tumble away, nothing shown dying; and the guardrails: animals of the town and animated craft objects only, no people or vendors, no rats, cockroaches or flies, no brand names, lotería pictures of our own without titles. Right?
27. **The glide in the levels built without it (GDD-PRG-01, GDD-MOV-07):** the glide works in every level once clearing 2-4 unlocks it (user, 2026-11-10: "Glide everywhere"). Measured since (2026-11-10, the World 3 design swarm's audit: the glide forced on in 0-1, 1-1 to 1-4 and 2-1 to 2-4, by scratch probes on the real simulation and by the repository's own tests, nothing committed). The glide adds reach and no height: at the same height a running jump crosses 9 tiles with it, not 5 (GDD-WLD-03's table). No level becomes impossible, every height gate holds (walls, doors, lifts, cliffs, the tier 2 and tier 3 rooftops, the tier 3 veladoras of 2-1 and 2-2), and no boss rule breaks: nothing from the floor hits La Estrella, and Don Zopilote is only easier to dodge. It makes optional: the pits its probes crossed in one glided jump (2-1's 8-tile awning pit among them), seven of 2-2's nine rollos, the fireball one included, three crumbling bridges of 1-2 and 1-3, and the enemies under a glide from a rooftop or a ledge. It makes one thing harder: a jump held through an awning's rebound glides on the way down, so the held ladder climbs the same tiers more slowly (tier 3 on step 265, not 119), while letting go as he falls and pressing again just over the tarp keeps the plain rhythm. With the glide on, 1572 of the levels' and the simulation's tests pass and 35 fail, most of them bots and scripts that hold jump while falling; 2-2's scripted bot loses all seven lives, out of step, not at a broken level. Proposed (the audit's recommendation): accept all of that as the reward for the unlock, and do not make the old levels glide-proof. Still open: (a) a torn awning (GDD-WLD-10) that is bounced on and steered back from leaves him alive on the near side with the awning gone and no way on, at 2-3's T2 and Tb without the glide too, and at its exam's T5 with the glide only; the audit proposes that a torn awning is whole again 3.0 s after it tore, once his body box is no longer over it, La Estrella's tear in 2-4 left out (an idea, not in GDD-WLD-10; the other choice is to leave it, and the player jumps into the pit to reset). (b) One best time per level, glide or not (recommended), or two? (c) When the unlock is built, the levels' bots run both ways, the Worlds 0 to 2 bots without the glide as the reference. Right?
28. **Level 2-1's near misses and its Palomas (GDD-WLD-12, GDD-ENM-03):** (a) on the ladder awning a tier 1 rebound peaks 2.9 px under the first rooftop's top, so only the chain gets him up; tight, but deterministic. Fine, or a wider miss so it reads as "not this one"? (b) The "wait" answer to a Paloma means waiting for its hover and walking under it; waiting out of its sight does not get Poncho past one, since any approach startles it again (GDD-ENM-03's rule as written). Right? (c) One Paloma on a rooftop (the first rooftop's, an easier copy of the pit's twist over a floor) stands for the plan's "Palomas on the rooftops between awnings": enough, or a second? (d) The pit awning is 3 tiles wide in an 8-tile pit, 3 open tiles before it and 2 after (the main session's choice, 2026-11-04: a 2-tile awning let a plain landing carry him off its far end, and a 9-tile pit made nearly every held crossing fail). Keep?
29. **Level 2-2's beats (GDD-WLD-13):** (a) its first "pit" is a sunken aisle he cannot die in, with a step back up, so the first rollo cannot be failed, and the first lethal pit comes after the first ofrenda. Keep? (b) The twist that replaces the Zanate's beat: the first chile of World 2 from a piñata, and its fireball unrolling a bundle the claw cannot reach, with the spikes below costing only the power in Taco or Fuego, so it is never a dead end. Keep, or another twist (a bundle clawed in mid-air from an awning bounce, which its last act already does)? (c) The veladora floats in the top row with nothing to stand on beside it, at the top of a column of hilos. Fine, or should it sit on something?
30. **Every rollo needs a hop (GDD-WLD-11):** a bundle hangs under its line and the claw's box ends at his feet, so a line whose bundle the claw reaches from the ground is always at least 8 px above his feet, and he hops up onto it ("claw, then hop") instead of walking straight on. Fine as a rule, or should a line at most 8 px up be stepped onto?
31. **Claw and jump together (GDD-WLD-11, GDD-CMB-02):** a jump pressed during the claw's 2-step wind-up lifts the swing over a bundle at his feet, so the claw and the hop must be two beats. Is a forgiveness wanted (the bundle counted for a swing that started level with it)?
32. **Level 2-3's choices and art (GDD-WLD-14, GDD-ART-06):** (a) the plan's Ten gave the optional fireball route to the far rollo over the alley; the layout gives it to a second far-post rollo over the Fuego pit, before the alley (the alley's rollo can be unrolled by a fireball spat from an awning rebound too, not measured). Keep, or move the fireball route onto the alley's rollo? (b) The chimneys' 1-tile tops let a careful player perch between the torn awnings, a slower way than the chain. Accept, or make them unstandable? (c) Its rooftops use World 2's ground tiles; should the chimneys (1 tile wide, 4 tall) and the high roofs have rooftop pieces of their own, and the giant star piñata planned for the background over its exit, La Estrella foreshadowed, be drawn now?
33. **La Estrella's build choices (GDD-BOS-03):** made by the main session during the build (2026-11-04), each Proposed. (a) She can be hit only while she glides, telegraphs, hangs or is yanked, never while she drops, is thrown up by an awning, rests on the floor or rises from it, so that, as you said, she is reached only from awning rebounds and cordel jumps, never from the floor. (b) In phase 3 her drop tears straight through the awnings, which stay whole (all but the middle one, torn away by her first drop, user, 2026-11-08), to the floor, so standing under an awning is no shelter (the plan had the awning throw her up there too). (c) Her telegraph shakes her body and her trolley 2 px sideways with the red flash, since her rope has no length at her high position to zigzag. (d) Gliding high she can pass behind the boss bar, which is drawn on top of her, and her top cone can reach past the screen's top. (e) A cordel jump cannot bump her at her phase 1 low height, since standing on the cordel his head is already above her bottom (the plan's reach table had not looked at it; a jump from the awning does). Right? (a) is partly answered (user, 2026-11-08): the tier 3 bump while she glides stays ("Keep it").
34. **World 2's contrast on screen (GDD-ART-06, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-UI-01):** answered (user, 2026-11-04): the Chapulín's leaf green is darkened to #3E7A3C, 18 L* from the pink walls ("Darken it"), and the boss bar's name gets a 2 px dark outline in both worlds ("Dark outline").
35. **The rest of La Estrella's design (GDD-BOS-03, GDD-BOS-01, GDD-AUD-02):** the main session's boss design (2026-11-08) also had two new attacks, a candy rain the awnings shelter Poncho from and a spin throw of tejocotes in marked arcs; with the 3-3 split kept they are left out (user, 2026-11-08: "Not sure if we need it if we stick to 3-3"; the main session recommends leaving them out: each phase already has its one lesson). Still open from the design's polish, beyond the hit-stop and the shake: a shadow under her that grows through each telegraph, the awning's tarp dipping under her landing, a bigger squash on landing than the 1 px her art has (GDD-BOS-03, "Her look") and pop on a bump, torn cones falling as paper with confetti, dust at a thud, sparks at the trolley, her burst in confetti and candy at her defeat, a hollow clay "tok" for a hit on her pitched a step higher each hit, a crowd's "¡Dale, dale, dale!" while she hangs low (sound only), a trolley squeal at each telegraph, a tarp "boomf" on an awning landing and a rip when she tears the middle awning away. Which of these, if any?
36. **World 3's enemies' rules (GDD-ENM-04, GDD-CMB-03):** the enemies are picked (user, 2026-11-10: the Murciélago, the Armadillo and the Cacomixtle to start with), and their rules and numbers are now written (the design swarm's, Proposed; La Catorce's are in Open Question 40). To confirm: (a) a stomped Murciélago stays gone like every enemy, and a row of bats is a reward only, never a needed step (recommended; the other way, a bat that dips and flaps back, makes one enemy that cannot be stomped away). (b) The Armadillo's roll is a cartoon's: as far as the designer knows the real nine-banded armadillo does not curl into a ball, which is to be checked; keep the roll and say so (recommended), or give it another idea (it jumps straight up when startled)? (c) A dazed Armadillo is harmless to touch, like a stunned Trompo (recommended). (d) Poncho can ride a Cacomixtle's emptied cart from where it stands (recommended). (e) The returning enemies: the Alacrán in 3-1 and 3-2, the Zopilote in 3-3, no cactus buddy, Tlacuache or Paloma. (f) Not settled by the designs: the Armadillo rolls uphill at 100 px/s in one sentence and is stopped by a slope's high side in another; and a hit taken in a glide over a pit, whether a hit a taco absorbs may still drop him into the pit, two losses from one contact (3-1's second and last chasms). (g) Their colours are a sketch from World 3's palette, to be approved from screenshots (Open Question 45).
37. **Vagonetas (GDD-WLD-15):** no brake, one way, and a rolling cart defeats every enemy it hits, heavy ones too (user, 2026-11-10: "Approved"); a rider still in it at a broken end falls on his own (user, 2026-11-10: "He falls on his own"); a jump keeps the cart's speed, a rolling cart can be boarded, and an empty cart is back at its start 2.0 s after its rail's end (user, 2026-11-11: "Yes to all three, build the cart"). Still the swarm's or the main session's defaults, to confirm: (a) the speeds, 150 px/s on the level, 120 up and 180 down along the rail, against his 120 (the descent is slower across the screen, 127 px/s, than the level run). (b) The warning post 6 tiles before a broken end, not 2; 7 tiles of headroom over a cart jump; buffers that block carts only; slope tiles under sloped rails; the floor 8 px over the rail's ground. (c) The cart first moves on the step after he lands in it; and a Poncho who lands at a standstill in a passing cart slides back in it, off its back if he lands in its back third: fine, or should the cart catch him? (d) Not settled: where a returning cart appears if Poncho or an enemy stands at its start (the cross-check suggests the arena's hoist for every cart: drawn arriving for its last 30 steps, not yet a floor), and what counts as stepping off a cart at a buffer. (e) During a boss's hit-stop a cart is drawn between its last two steps like a platform; 3-4 will need it held still (GDD-BOS-01).
38. **The up-and-down camera and pits in a tall level (GDD-UI-04, GDD-WLD-03, GDD-TEC-04):** the camera follows up and down (user, 2026-11-10: "Camera follows up and down"); its rule is the design swarm's, Proposed. (a) Does a pit high in a tall level cost a life at once, by a raised bottom at most 4 rows under its lower lip (recommended: without it 3-1's early chasms are falls of up to 30 rows on camera, and 3-3's take about 1.5 s; 3-3's designer asks for the same thing as "lost a screen below the lower bank, the camera stopping 7 tiles below it"), or is the only deadly fall at the very bottom of the level? (b) Where he stands on the screen: his feet between 43% and 58% of its height on the ground (recommended: 7 tiles below his feet are always in view and his body is clear of the phone's buttons), or lower, near today's 83%? A tall level then frames him higher than Worlds 1 and 2 do; one-screen levels do not change. (c) At a respawn a cut (recommended, as sideways today) or a pan back, up to 1.7 s? (d) Levels up to 51 rows, with 3-2 kept at 17 (recommended: carts on long slopes are the camera's least comfortable case). (e) The sight rules for a tall level in GDD-WLD-03, which 3-1 and 3-3 have not been checked against.
39. **What the World 3 levels ask of the camera beyond its rule (GDD-UI-04, GDD-WLD-16, GDD-WLD-18):** the two tall layouts were drawn against a working assumption, before the camera's rule was written, and state needs it does not give. 3-1: at least 14 tiles in view ahead when he runs left along its galleries (today's dead zone gives exactly 14, with nothing to spare, not checked in play). 3-3: while he rises (a climbing cart, a lift, the plank stairs) he is held low on the screen, at the 60% line, so that 10 tiles show above him, and at the 40% line while he falls or rides down (the rule's band holds him between 43% and 58% and does not look up); in a rolling cart he is held at 40% of the screen's width so that 18 tiles show ahead, needed at one place, R3's post, where the far bank's lip is 15 tiles ahead and today's dead zone shows 14 to 16 (recommended by its designer for every cart in World 3; the other way moves L1 one tile nearer, and a perfectly timed plain jump from R3's end then reaches it); and where a Cacomixtle is in view he never stands below the 60% line, so that its cart, first seen 200 to 240 px ahead, is clear of a phone's Jump button (K1's rail is 14 px clear at most). 3-1's other need, his feet at 58% of the height or higher on the ground, the band's lower edge already gives. Add a look-up and a sideways lead to the rule, or adjust the layouts?
40. **La Catorce's fight (GDD-BOS-04, GDD-BOS-01, GDD-ART-06):** the boss, her arena and the ridden-off rule are decided (user, 2026-11-10: "Let's go with la catorce", "Engine shed", "He falls on his own"); her numbers are the design swarm's, Proposed, all hand estimates. (a) Armoured until her first slam of each phase, so each phase shows one whole charge first (taken as the default for pacing; the other way lets a well-aimed first cart count, and a bot then wins in about 12 s). (b) The cold flame as a 25th colour of World 3, used only by her (recommended), or the curse shown with lamp amber alone? (c) The arena's rails with no warning post and shorter than the levels' limits, under a written exemption (recommended), or each rail stretched to 12 tiles, a ride of 1.3 s that narrows every window? (d) Her size, 64 x 32 px (recommended: it closes phase 3's wall pocket), or 48 x 32? (e) Against the first sketch: phase 3 has no carts rolling on their own, boarding aims the cart, and the glide is the way back across, not up (recommended).
41. **Level 3-1's choices (GDD-WLD-16):** (a) the fold: for 23 columns the path runs right to left in a gallery under the ledges (recommended: it makes the cliff face tall without a climb; the other way is a straight descent of about 32 s). (b) The longest replay, from the pit of Ten back to the second ofrenda, about 12 s through both galleries (recommended: accept; the other way moves the second ofrenda into the lower gallery, at column 140). (c) Two bats that can hit on the main line and four that cannot: enough for the level after a boss, or should the Murciélago bite more often? (d) The middle gallery has no enemy (recommended: a patrol bat under its 4-tile roof hit a hopping bot in 13 of 24 start times). (e) The tip's second sentence, "Let go to drop", and its texts in the three languages. (f) Found by the review of PR #93, to fix when the level is built: the 7-tile drop through the middle gallery's crumbling floor (146 to 149) lands on the lower pen's rock, in its Alacrán's walk, against GDD-WLD-03's sight rule (nothing that hurts within 3 tiles of where a drop of more than 6 tiles lands).
42. **Level 3-2's choices (GDD-WLD-17):** (a) ofrendas at 27%, 46% and 76%, not 25%, 55% and 85% (recommended: each stands just before a new threat and the three-cart chain stays whole; the other way puts the third on K2's bank, column 203). (b) Staying in rail F costs a life on spikes, or the taco (recommended, with the lit post, the hilo arc and the taco before it; the gentler way is a buffer and a dead end). (c) Carts are never required: every pit can be crossed on foot (recommended for a first cart level). (d) The hanging-beam hop added to its Shō, and no Tlacuache or Murciélago. Its layout is the first round's, with the later decisions applied in words only; it has not been played.
43. **Level 3-3's choices (GDD-WLD-18):** (a) the perch in its Ki, a place where the first Cacomixtle cannot reach him (recommended: it is the lesson; without it K1 is simply jumped on solid ground). (b) Bridge B with one stone pier (recommended; with none it is the purest "the only floor falls", with two a gentler bridge). (c) All three ways into the secret hollow, and the secret needing the glide. (d) Found by the review of PR #93, to fix when the level is built: K2's cart waits on columns 158 and 159 at the end of its ride, and 159 is bridge B's first plank, against "parks on stone or rock only".
44. **World 3's run lengths (GDD-WLD-03, GDD-WLD-16 to GDD-WLD-18, GDD-BOS-04):** GDD-WLD-03's "about 45 s" for a perfect run is what 8 screens cost at World 2's pace. A glide holds 120 px/s and a cart 150, so the designs come out shorter: 3-1 37.9 s by its bot (40.9 s with three stops), 3-2 about 28 s and 3-3 about 35 s for an expert and 43 s careful (both counted by hand), and La Catorce about 13 s for a bot and 55 to 85 s for a first clean fight (an estimate). 3-2 and 3-3 have five and eight enemies against World 2's six to nine. Accept about 30 to 40 s for a World 3 level (recommended; the levels are at the 8 screens allowed, and more would be waiting), and decide on more enemies only after the first playtest against GDD-LOOP-02's 2 to 4 minutes?
45. **World 3's look (GDD-ART-06, GDD-ENM-04, GDD-BOS-04):** designed without reference images (user, 2026-11-10: "Design it, show me"), not drawn; everything is approved from screenshots. The art direction's defaults: (a) an ochre rock floor underground as outdoors (recommended, for readability), or a darker floor of its own for mood? (b) The cart rust-red with a steel rim (recommended, the best contrast measured), or grey iron, or another colour (not Poncho's blue or red)? (c) The far strips show the hill town left behind and the mine's chimney and hoist frame ahead, and no shrine, cross or candle anywhere in the mine, so that candle light stays the ofrendas' and the veladora's; or a miners' niche or chapel? (d) The Spanish names used in the document (riel, durmientes, tope, vagoneta, viga, pino, encino, madroño, maguey, sotol, tocón, zacatón) and the arena's other dressing; its name, "La Casa de Máquinas", is decided with the engine shed (GDD-BOS-04). (e) The three enemies' colours (rust bat, timber-lit Armadillo, ochre-light Cacomixtle), and the returning Alacrán and Zopilote against the Sierra's backdrops. (f) The buffer drawn outlined in the full timber colours although it blocks carts only. (g) How the arena's gantry tiles, which only La Catorce's slam brings down, are told from a plank that falls under him. (h) The optional lit and shaded cliff-face frames.
46. **2-3's veladora (GDD-WLD-14):** the glide audit found that it is not "tier 3 only" as GDD-WLD-14 says: a plain jump from the rooftop F1's left end and a jump from the unrolled rollo R1 take it, with no glide (a scratch probe, not a test in the repository). Reword the secret as a reward for unrolling R1 (recommended), or move the veladora 2 tiles up, to row 4, so that it is tier 3 only again, which needs re-measuring?
47. **The Chile Fantasma in World 3 (GDD-PWR-04, GDD-ENM-04, GDD-WLD-16 to GDD-WLD-18):** each world gets one special piñata off the main path (user, 2026-11-10: "One hidden per world"), and World 3's layouts were drawn before that rule and have none. Which level gets it (recommended: 3-2, as 1-2 and 2-2 have theirs), and where? And his touch defeats every regular enemy: does that hold for a rolling Armadillo, armoured against everything but a cart, and for a Cacomixtle in its cart (recommended: yes to both, the cart rolling on empty)?
